# Japanese Immersion Extension — Project Plan

_Last updated: 2026-08-15_

This is the **full source of truth** for the project — vision, target user, competitive analysis, scope, technical architecture, build order, decisions, and open questions. `CLAUDE.md` is a short session-start briefing that points back here; if you need the detail behind a decision or a task, it's in this document.

---

## 1. Product Vision & Core Goal

**Core goal:** Let learners who are already studying Japanese elsewhere (WaniKani, Bunpro, a textbook, a tutor) use Crunchyroll anime as real immersion practice, with just enough support to confirm what they half-know without breaking the scene. **The extension's job is to keep them watching, not to teach them.**

This is the product's north star, not just a feature list. Every scope decision traces back to it — see Section 4's litmus test.

**The premise this is built on:** Crunchyroll has no native Japanese subtitle track in any form — confirmed against Crunchyroll's own support documentation, which lists official subtitle/CC support only in Arabic, English, French, German, Portuguese, and Spanish (verified 2026-06-23). The extension fills a real, confirmed gap rather than duplicating an existing platform feature.

**Positioning — three pillars, not independent (the first causes the other two):**
1. **Lean by design, not by compromise.** No backend, no AI API calls. Originally a cost/architecture decision, but it doubles as the product's philosophy: nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary — the right default for a user who already has a more rigorous resource for grammar instruction elsewhere.
2. **Structurally cheaper to run.** No per-user AI-API or server cost, which gives room to compete on price or reinvest in the product without the margin pressure competitors running AI/server infrastructure carry.
3. **Depth on one platform, not breadth across many.** ManabiDojo spans Crunchyroll, Netflix, YouTube, manga, and general web. We go deep on Crunchyroll-specific problems (forced-subtitle coexistence, fansub drift, anime-specific vocab) instead of spreading thin.

---

## 2. Target User

**Not a beginner looking for their first grammar explanation.** Someone who already has a study system — WaniKani or similar for kanji/vocab SRS, Bunpro or similar for grammar SRS, maybe a textbook or tutor — and uses anime as immersion time: the place they meet studied material in the wild, build real reading/listening speed, and confirm or correct what they think they already know.

For this user, immersion time has one job: exposure and confirmation, fast, without breaking the flow of watching. It is **not** tutoring time. They already have a trusted resource for grammar instruction; an AI explanation competing with that resource is a worse experience than no explanation, not a better one. A typical session is mostly watching, with a click only when a word stops them — recognized-but-unsure, or an inflection that threw them — followed by a two-second confirmation, then back to the show.

**What this user actually wants, in priority order:**
1. Japanese subtitles to exist at all on Crunchyroll, synced well enough not to be annoying
2. A fast way to check a word without leaving the player
3. A way to send anything that caught their attention into the SRS system they already use and trust (Anki) — not a second, competing review system

**What this user does *not* want:**
- To be taught grammar mid-episode by an AI (duplicates/competes with Bunpro-equivalent)
- A built-in flashcard/quiz system that duplicates Anki, where they already have months of review history

---

## 3. Competitive Landscape

**Main competitor: ManabiDojo** — 2-person team, ~2 years old, ~5,000–8,000 Chrome Web Store users, 4.7–4.8 rating. Free tier covers Crunchyroll-catalog shows with basic lookups; $6/month or $120 lifetime unlocks AI features, flashcards, Netflix support, and — notably — parsing of raw *imported* subtitles for dictionary lookup (a user who imports a missing show via Jimaku still needs to pay to get word-segmented, clickable text on it, even though import itself is free).

### 3.1 Where ManabiDojo already leads (parity, not a gap for us to close)

Confirmed directly from their Features page (2026-06-30) and changelog:
- Up to four toggleable subtitle layers: Japanese (with furigana-above-kanji while paused), Kana, Romaji, and Native (dual subtitles in any officially-supported language) — their "Native" layer covers the same end-user need as our English-subtitle feature, which shipped in Phase 5 (2026-07-23)
- Jimaku import with **auto-offset-detection** (aligns against the platform's native subtitle track) and per-season "Jimaku history" (remembers preferred uploader/file, viewable/clearable) — materially more polished than our current manual-offset-only flow. **Gap partly closed:** per-show-per-season uploader memory (with sticky fallback) shipped 2026-07-16. Still open: per-season *offset* memory — the saved offset is keyed per episode, not per season — and auto-offset-detection, which remains scheduled into Phase 6. See Section 6.
- AI sentence/grammar breakdown (chunk-by-chunk, refreshable) and AI in-context translation (meaning of the clicked word *in this specific sentence*)
- Color-coded frequency-rank badges (green/yellow/orange/gray) — more granular than a simple common/uncommon flag. **Gap closed (2026-07-19):** shipped as a real three-tier frequency-rank badge behind an opt-in toggle, replacing the earlier common-word flag — see Section 5 and Decisions Log.
- POS tags, inflection info, kanji ON/KUN + JLPT level, a Names dictionary tab. **Declined, not gaps (2026-07-04):** kanji ON/KUN display and a Names dictionary were both considered and rejected — see Decisions Log. (Kanji-containing names are still made clickable as ordinary words, a separate, narrower fix — see Section 5.)
- Custom dictionary URL buttons (up to 3, e.g. Jisho), choice of dictionary API source. **Declined (2026-07-04):** cuts against the single-dictionary "nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary" positioning — see Decisions Log.
- Built-in Wordbook with mastery levels and Anki-compatible export
- Romaji toggle, hotkeys, furigana display — all already shipped and mature

**Root cause of their reliability issues:** their parsing stack (Ichiran/Ichimoe + CaboCha) is confirmed **server-side**, not client-side, via their own changelog describing uploaded subtitles being sent to "our server" for parsing, with explicit capacity limits.

### 3.2 Known user complaints (our opportunity map)

1. **Paywall trust issue** — free features moved behind subscription after launch; specifically, the parsing/lookup step on imported subtitles is gated even though import itself is free
2. **Reliability** — intermittent bugs, server connectivity, regional subtitle issues (very likely downstream of the server-side parsing stack above)
3. **Price** — €6/month seen as expensive by some users relative to free alternatives (Yomitan + ASBPlayer); multiple reviews mention pairing ManabiDojo with Yomitan, suggesting a segment of their own users already prefers a leaner, non-AI dictionary-lookup tool

### 3.3 Our differentiation strategy

Revised 2026-06-30 after direct review of ManabiDojo's site, and again 2026-07-02 once Anki export moved into the core loop. Don't re-litigate 3.1/3.2 without new evidence.

- **Anki export that isn't paywalled a second time.** Core to the loop from the start (see Section 4), not bundled inside a wordbook system the user may not want, and not gated behind premium the way theirs is.
- **No AI middleman — a stance, not a missing feature.** Doesn't duplicate/compete with a grammar resource (e.g. Bunpro) the target user already trusts, and doesn't break immersion mid-episode. Directly answers complaint #1/#2's underlying cause (server-side AI dependency) by design.
- **Free, ungated parsing on imported subtitles.** Universal Jimaku import with full dictionary lookup from day one — ManabiDojo paywalls exactly this step.
- **Crunchyroll-specific depth vs. multi-platform breadth.** Forced-subtitle coexistence, fansub drift handling, anime-specific vocabulary, instead of spreading across Netflix/YouTube/manga/web.
- **Structurally lower cost base.** No backend/AI-API cost per user, giving room to price sustainably even though our pricing *model* (subscription + lifetime) now matches theirs and WaniKani/Bunpro's — see Decisions Log 2026-06-30 and Build Order Phase 7. This is a cost-structure advantage, not a pricing-model differentiator.

**All three Jimaku-import UX gaps vs. ManabiDojo are now decided and scheduled, none left open.** Auto-offset-detection on import (aligning automatically against the platform's native subtitle track, rather than requiring a manual nudge the first time) is decided and scheduled into Phase 6 — see Decisions Log 2026-07-05. Per-season "remember my preferred uploader" memory is decided and scheduled into Phase 4.5 (Section 6), and the title-search mismatch (Crunchyroll's English titles vs. Jimaku's JP/romanized indexing) is decided in favor of TMDB/AniList ID resolution, also Phase 4.5 (Section 6) — see Decisions Log 2026-07-04 and 2026-07-05 for both.

---

## 4. V1 Scope

**Litmus test for anything added to this list:** Does it help the user capture something quickly and keep watching, or feed something into a system they already use? Or does it ask them to stop and be taught, or duplicate a tool they already trust? First → in. Second → out, regardless of whether ManabiDojo has it.

### Must-have (core loop)

- [ ] Crunchyroll support only (no Netflix, no YouTube, no manga yet)
- [ ] Universal subtitle import from Jimaku — automatic show/episode detection from the Crunchyroll page, not a user-facing search box (clarified 2026-07-05; the original "search by show" wording only meant "not a curated list," not literally a typed search), and not a curated list. Full design in Section 6, Phase 4.5.
- [ ] Manual subtitle file upload fallback (.srt/.ass) for anything not on Jimaku — also the answer to "shows with zero Jimaku subtitles at all" (see Open Questions, Resolved); built 2026-07-06, see Section 5
- [ ] Display a synced Japanese subtitle overlay on the Crunchyroll player — the core premise (no native Japanese track exists at all) and the target user's #1 priority (Section 2)
- [ ] Subtitle offset/sync adjustment tools (hotkeys + manual nudge) — must-have since community subs often drift
- [ ] Word-level segmentation of Japanese subtitle text (morphological analysis)
- [ ] Click/hover word → popup with definition + reading (JMdict data)
- [ ] **Anki export** — capture a clicked word or sentence and send it to the user's existing Anki deck via **AnkiConnect** (decided 2026-07-02 over offline .apkg/CSV — see Decisions Log). Moved from the differentiation layer into the core loop 2026-07-02: this *is* the "keep watching" loop's endpoint, not a bolt-on. Full card fields, capture UX, and metadata-toggle spec live in Section 5 (Technical Architecture). The sentence-translation and audio fields both shipped in Phase 5; the screenshot field was dropped from the roadmap entirely on 2026-07-17, confirmed DRM-blocked rather than deferred. JLPT's toggle is a narrow, 2026-07-02 exception to the original JLPT exclusion, opt-in/off-by-default only, never an always-on label/filter/sort — it doesn't reopen the AI-explanation or wordbook/SRS exclusions below, which stand for unrelated reasons.
- [ ] **English subtitle display** — dual-display below the Japanese line, sourced from Crunchyroll's own caption file rather than a second Jimaku fetch (decided 2026-07-23 after fact-finding ruled the Jimaku route out; see Decisions Log). Moved from the differentiation layer into the core loop 2026-07-23 alongside the Anki `Translation` field it feeds.

### Additional differentiation features (build after core loop works)

- [ ] Furigana-only display mode (no romaji, no translation overlay)
- [ ] Subtitle appearance controls (font size, position, background opacity)
- [ ] Hotkeys (toggle subtitles, pause-and-reveal, offset adjust)
- [ ] Auto-pause on new subtitle line (Anki-style reveal)

### Explicitly excluded from v1 (resist scope creep)

- **AI grammar/sentence explanations** — excluded on principle, not just cost. Competes with a resource the target user already trusts more (e.g. Bunpro) and breaks the immersion the product exists to protect. BYOK (user's own LLM API key, called client-side) would be the path if ever revisited without compromising the no-backend architecture — but this is a deliberate stance, not a missing feature.
- **Built-in wordbook / SRS / quiz system** — would duplicate Anki, where the target user already has review history. Anki export feeds that system instead of competing with it.
- Other video platforms (Netflix, YouTube, etc.) — Crunchyroll-only by design, not a Netflix-specific gap; see Section 1's "depth on one platform" positioning
- Manga OCR / general web support
- **Romaji subtitle-display toggle** — excluded 2026-07-04, not just deprioritized. A subtitle-level romaji layer (as opposed to anything in the popup) is a total-beginner feature — it exists for learners who can't read kana yet, which the target user by definition already can. Same shape of exclusion as kanji ON/KUN readings: not a priority call, this isn't the product's job for this persona.
- **Kana-only subtitle-display mode** — excluded 2026-07-04. Unlike romaji, this isn't just lower-value, it actively works against furigana mode: furigana keeps the kanji visible and adds the reading on top, reinforcing kanji the target user is likely also studying elsewhere; kana-only would strip the kanji out entirely, which is a step backward for that goal, not a lighter version of the same feature.

---

## 5. Technical Architecture

Current/final state of each piece, organized to match Section 6's phase ordering: general/cross-cutting architecture first, then grouped by the build phase each piece belongs to. Where something evolved through more than one attempt, only the final state is described here — the rejected alternatives and reasoning live in the Decisions Log (Section 7) to avoid duplicating that history in two places.

### General / cross-cutting architecture

Decisions and constraints that apply across the whole extension, not tied to a single build phase.

- **Platform: Chrome extension, Manifest V3.** Not really a choice — MV2 has been fully disabled in stable Chrome since October 2024, with no path back; the last enterprise-policy workaround closed in 2025, and even the developer-flag workaround was being phased out by mid-2026.
- **Context split between service worker and content script is forced, not stylistic** (confirmed during Phase 2/3 build): Chrome is deprecating cross-origin `fetch` from content scripts, so all Jimaku network calls live in the background service worker; kuromoji's XHR-based dictionary loader forces tokenization into the content script. Net effect: network I/O → background, anything needing a real DOM/XHR → content script.
- **Settings/storage:** Chrome extension local storage. Jimaku API key stored via a toolbar popup UI, never in a config file (2026-06-23).

### Phase 1–2: Extension skeleton & subtitle pipeline

- **Subtitle source: Jimaku API.** REST API gated by a personal account + API key; entries keyed to TMDB/AniList IDs with subtitle files attached underneath. Rate limit 25 req/min per key — cache search results client-side rather than re-querying on every load. Known footgun: a year number in a title search breaks results (strip years before searching by name). Exact contract: `GET /api/entries/search` (query/anilist_id/tmdb_id/anime/after/before), `GET /api/entries/{id}`, `GET /api/entries/{id}/files?episode=N`. Auth is the raw API key in an `Authorization` header (no `Bearer` prefix); all endpoints return `x-ratelimit-*` headers and `429` on limit. Auth is per-user (needed for the Phase 7 onboarding flow — the user needs their own Jimaku account + key). No viable alternative source exists for this niche. `JIMAKU_API_KEY` is now set as a persistent environment variable in the user's `~/.zshenv` (2026-07-03), so `scripts/batch-test.js` runs directly (`cd scripts && node batch-test.js`) without needing the key re-entered. **Archive-file filtering gap fixed (2026-07-06):** `background.js`'s `fetchSubtitles` picked `files[0]` blindly with no filtering — `scripts/batch-test.js` already had an `ARCHIVE_RE` filter (`.7z`/`.zip`/`.rar`/etc. can't be parsed as subtitle text directly) but it was never ported to the actual production code. Confirmed real via live testing: Naruto ep 1's Jimaku files list a `.zip` as the first entry, which would have been fetched and failed to parse as `.srt`/`.ass`. Ported the same filter into `fetchSubtitles`, throwing a clear "only archive files found, use manual upload" error in the (currently theoretical) case where every file for an episode happens to be an archive. **`fileHint` manual file-selection override added (2026-07-06), a stopgap ahead of Phase 4.5's real ranked selection.** Confirmed a second, worse real data-quality issue while setting up Naruto: Shippuuden ep 1 for testing: this entry's Jimaku files include a whole season's worth of Hulu-sourced releases using a per-season "SxxE01" restart numbering (S07E01 = 第144話, S15E01 = 第296話, etc.) — Jimaku's own per-file episode tagging marks EVERY one of these "episode 1," so the (already-fixed) first-non-archive-file pick would have silently served the wrong episode's subtitles entirely, not just a suboptimal one. `FETCH_SUBTITLES` now accepts an optional `fileHint` (case-insensitive filename substring) content.js can supply to disambiguate; `fetchSubtitles` prefers a matching file, falling back to the first text file when no hint is given or nothing matches. Deliberately scoped as a manual override, not automated "best file" selection — that's Phase 4.5's own already-planned, more rigorous ranking work (Section 6).
- **Subtitle parsing:** `.srt`/`.ass` into timed text blocks. Verified 2026-06-30 (Bocchi/SubsPlease files) that long dialogue lines are sometimes split across multiple `Dialogue:` events at the same timestamp — `cues.filter()` + `.join("\n")` reassembles the full line (SubsPlease convention, not a bug).
- **Overlay UI:** injected DOM overlay synced to video playback time. Must coexist with Crunchyroll's own forced-subtitle layer on some titles — confirmed 2026-06-23 this is a player-rendered text/caption layer, not burned into video pixels, so it needs a z-index/positioning strategy, not a video-corruption workaround. Still untested hands-on (Section 8). **Fullscreen parenting fix (2026-06-30):** DOM elements appended to `document.body` don't render when a fullscreen element is active; fixed with `getContainer()` (`document.fullscreenElement ?? document.body`) + a `fullscreenchange` listener that re-parents overlay elements.

### Phase 3: Word parsing & dictionary lookup (foundational)

- **Dictionary: JMdict via jmdict-simplified** (JSON build, not raw EDRDG XML — the raw format's DTD entities are awkward to parse in JS). Actively maintained (daily generation since mid-2006, 200k+ entries), CC BY-SA, commercial use explicitly permitted, attribution required, no open-source requirement on consuming software.
- **JMdict delivery format (2026-06-23, extended 2026-06-24).** The raw `jmdict-eng` release (117MB) is too large to `JSON.parse` synchronously without stalling either extension context. Ships instead as a custom-trimmed `jmdict-compact.json` (~29MB as of 2026-06-24), built once offline: indexes every kanji/kana surface form to an entry index, keeping first reading, a capped set of senses/glosses (exact mechanism below), `partOfSpeech` codes, and a `common` flag, plus an 83-code `posTags` map. Loaded/cached in memory by the background service worker; content script sends a word, gets back entry objects + `posTags`. Known limitations discovered through later Phase 4 testing (gloss/sense cap, single-reading-per-entry, duplicate near-identical entries) are covered under Phase 4 below, since that's where they were found and are being fixed.
- **Word segmentation: kuromoji.js** (client-side, pure JS), locked in 2026-06-23 after checking ManabiDojo's own stack (Ichiran/Ichimoe + CaboCha, confirmed server-side). Bundled locally, runs in the content script (its dictionary loader needs `XMLHttpRequest`, unavailable in service workers). Each token carries `conjugated_type`/`conjugated_form` (Japanese grammatical labels), a katakana `reading`, and `basic_form`/`pos`. Known limitations of the library itself, discovered through later Phase 4 testing, are covered under Phase 4 below.

### Phase 4: Sync tooling & multi-show validation

**Dictionary-data precision:**

- **`jmdict-compact.json`'s sense/gloss cap, and `g`'s sense-grouped structure (bumped and restructured 2026-07-06).** The gloss-per-sense cap is 3, and `g` is GROUPED BY SENSE — an array of arrays, one sub-array per included sense — instead of one flattened array, so content.js can render numbered sense groups (see Popup content & display below). Within each sense, glosses are still taken by position (not "first N unique") and deduped against glosses already collected from an earlier sense without backfilling a dropped duplicate; a sense that loses every gloss to dedup is dropped entirely rather than kept as an empty numbered row. Regenerated and validated same session as the `k` field below (see the regeneration entry); consumers that expected a flat array (content.js's duplicate-gloss first-gloss check, `fix-jmdict-priority.js`'s fingerprint key) were updated to read `g[0]?.[0]` instead of `g[0]`. **Sense-count cap itself resolved 2026-07-13** — see the sense-selection budget entry below.
- **Sense-selection budget fixed to only spend its cap on VISIBLE senses (2026-07-12).** `generate-jmdict-compact.js`'s `selectSensesView` previously counted a sense against the 3-slot budget as soon as it was considered, even if gloss-dedup left it fully empty (no visible row) — found via live testing on よ (JMdict id 2029090, 4 real senses): sense 1 ("hey") fully deduped away against sense 0's "hey" but still burned a slot, so the popup only ever showed 2 near-duplicate rows ("hey; you" / "hold on") and never reached the entry's genuinely distinct 4th (`int`) sense. Budget now only decrements on senses that actually render.
- **Per-reading sense filtering for `appliesToKana`-restricted entries (2026-07-12).** な/なあ/なー/なぁ share one JMdict entry (id 2029110), but its "don't"/"do" senses are tagged `appliesToKana: ["な"]` — restricted to な specifically, not shared with なあ's attention-getting/admiring family. The generator previously ignored this restriction entirely, so a なあ lookup showed な's prohibitive senses. Fixed by computing a separate filtered sense-view per kana reading when its restrictions differ from the primary reading's, indexed as its own compact-file entry (readings with an identical view still share the primary entry — no bloat for the ~99.6% unaffected case). Affects 802 of 217,856 raw entries (confirmed count). Symmetric `appliesToKanji` restrictions (463 entries) are a known, unfixed gap — no live-test case has shown one actually producing a wrong card yet.
- **Function-word (particle/interjection) sense budget raised to 8, NOT globally (2026-07-13) — resolves the "does the 3-sense cap lose meanings that matter" open question.** Two real cases surfaced in live testing: の was missing its sentence-final question sense (6th position) and ただ今 was missing "right away" (4th position). Verified via a full scan of the raw release before picking a number: content words have an effectively unbounded long tail of real senses (落ちる has 27, する has 17, 273 *common* entries exceed 6) where any modestly-raised global cap would still constantly clip real content while cluttering the common 1-2-sense case for no benefit. Particle/interjection-*primary* entries (first sense with a gloss tagged `prt`/`int`) are a fundamentally different, tightly bounded shape — only 806 such entries exist at all, and a cap of 8 fully covers 802 of them (99.5%), including both confirmed cases. The 4 remaining outliers (か: 15 senses, が: 10, に: 9, quotative って: 9) are accepted as a smaller, more defensible tradeoff than an unbounded cap. Content-word entries stay at 3, untouched.
- **`lookupWord`'s particle filter now also prefers an entry reached via its own PRIMARY reading over one reached only through an alternate (2026-07-12).** Found via live testing: bare て (plain connective, its own `prt`/`conj` entry) was losing to quotative って's entry, which legitimately lists て as a colloquial-contraction alternate reading — both pass the `prt` filter, so POS alone couldn't distinguish them. The rs-swap display-precision feature (くる → 刳る) is unaffected — it only changes how an already-selected entry displays its reading, never which entries get selected.
- **New `isHonorificSuffix` lookup filter, mirroring `isParticle` (2026-07-13).** さん/さま/くん/ちゃん/様/氏 share their bare kana reading with unrelated ordinary-noun homographs (さん also reaches 酸 "acid", 三 "three", 讃 "praise") — the existing generic 名詞-category POS matcher can't exclude these since they're nouns too. `tokenize-utils.js` flags a token kuromoji tags uniformly as 名詞/接尾/人名 (confirmed general across all four honorific suffixes, not word-specific); `background.js` filters to JMdict POS code `suf`. Doesn't fire for a title/profession noun like 先生 that can stand alone (tagged plain 名詞/一般 by kuromoji, not 接尾).
- **Regeneration pipeline order matters — `apply-tubelex-frequency.js` must be re-run after any `generate-jmdict-compact.js` regen, not just `fix-jmdict-priority.js` (regression caught 2026-07-13).** Regenerating the base compact file and re-running only the rK/sK heuristic pass reverted homograph ordering to pre-TUBELEX state for こと/僕/くる (each ranking a rare/untagged entry above a far more common one) — caught via live-test feedback before it shipped, not via `batch-test.js` (which doesn't check ordering). Both priority passes are required, in order, after every base regen; `batch-test.js`'s pattern counts were unaffected by the regression (it doesn't test ordering), so this class of bug needs a live/manual ordering spot-check, not just the corpus suite, to catch.
- **`jmdict-compact.json` now carries a `k` field (kanji spellings), added 2026-07-06.** An array of the entry's kanji spelling(s) (`w.kanji.map(k => k.text)`), included only when non-empty — confirmed by direct inspection that the raw release carries this per-entry (射る/炒る・煎る・熬る/入る for いる's first three senses) but the compact format was discarding it entirely before this field existed. Powers the kanji-spellings-on-homograph-cards feature (Popup content & display below).
- **`jmdict-compact.json` regenerated a third time (2026-07-06), adding `k` and restructuring `g` in the same pass as the previous entries above.** Same reverse-engineered generation script (`scripts/generate-jmdict-compact.js`), re-run against the same raw `jmdict-eng-3.6.2` release, then `fix-jmdict-priority.js` and `scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js` re-run in sequence afterward, unchanged in their own logic apart from the `g[0]?.[0]` fingerprint fix in `fix-jmdict-priority.js`. Validated against the pre-regen backup across all 462,753 index keys: 100% match on `r`/`p`/`c`/`m`/`ki`/`rs` with zero mismatches; every gloss difference (20.8% of keys) is explained by the intended cap bump (strictly additive — every old gloss still present, in order, just with up to one more added per sense), except 0.07% of keys where a gloss that used to get mis-attributed to a later sense (because the old 2-cap hid it in its true earlier sense) now correctly attributes to that earlier sense once the larger cap reaches it — a benign side effect of the same dedup-by-position algorithm, not a regression. `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 11 corpus-validation patterns unchanged (89/38/53/24/336/105/7/8/36/2) after the regen and after the content.js/background.js changes that consume the new fields.
- **Multi-reading display-precision bug fixed (2026-07-05).** The compact format now carries `rs` — the entry's full list of kana readings, added only when there's more than one — alongside the existing single `r` (primary reading, unchanged everywhere else). `background.js`'s `lookupWord` checks whether the word actually being looked up matches one of `rs`'s entries; if so, the returned result's `r` is swapped to that matched reading instead of the entry's primary one, without mutating the shared cached entry object. Fixes くる resolving to 刳る showing えぐる instead of くる. See the regeneration entry below for how `rs` was populated. **Independently re-verified as a general fix, not case-specific (2026-07-05, follow-up check):** the first validation pass was circular (tested the fix against its own trigger condition); redone directly against the raw JMdict release instead — 37,514 unambiguous multi-reading words (84,111 individual readings) cross-checked, 0 missing/0 wrong. In the original (index key, entry)-pair framing, ~46,600 pairs needed the fix dictionary-wide (larger than the initial ~37,000 estimate, likely computed against an earlier snapshot) — 100% now resolve correctly. Spot-checked well beyond くる/えぐる: ふなばし/せんきょう (3-way), たまう/たもう, わたし/ワタシ, わい/ワイ (a genuine unrelated second entry sharing わい correctly left alone), and こう (a 14-way stress case) all resolve to the correct reading.
- **Duplicate-gloss homograph entries fixed (2026-07-05).** `content.js`'s `renderEntries` flags any entry (beyond the first) among the popup's results whose first gloss (`g[0]`) exactly matches one already shown — confirmed via the raw `jmdict-eng` release that real cases (酒/君) genuinely share an identical first gloss, not just a similar one, so this simple check is sufficient without a fuzzier overlap comparison. `archaicTagLabel()` then checks the entry's `m` (sense-level misc tags: arch/obs/dated, priority order) and `ki` (reading-level tags: ok/ik) for a real, sourced reason; if found, the card renders normally with a small labeled badge (e.g. "dated" — confirmed real for 酒's ささ reading). If no such tag exists, the card is visually demoted instead (`.jp-immersion-popup-entry-demoted`, reduced opacity/size) rather than shown as an equal-weight duplicate. Validated against this session's live-confirmed 私/あたし case: あたし carries no arch/obs/dated tag (only register tags, `fem`/`uk`) and is correctly demoted, not falsely tagged; わらわ (also "I; me") does carry `arch` and correctly shows the "archaic" badge instead. **Verb-type-implied archaism, built (2026-07-06, later round), with a same-day follow-up fix after live re-testing.** `isArchaicVerbType()`/`archaicTagLabel()` (`content.js`) now treat any `v4*`/`v2*` (Yodan/Nidan) POS code as an implicit `arch` signal, checked as a fallback AFTER the explicit `m`/`ki` checks so an entry with its own real tag is never overridden. Confirmed by inspecting the full `posTags` map that literally every `v4*`/`v2*` code is JMdict-labeled "(archaic)" — not assumed from a couple of samples. **Live re-testing caught a real gap the same day:** 呉る (くる homograph) and こかす (倒す homograph) both still showed no badge despite being genuinely `v2r-s`/`v4s`-tagged, because `archaicTagLabel()` was only ever CALLED for entries already flagged `isDuplicate` (sharing a first gloss with an earlier card in the same popup) — and neither 呉る nor こかす shares its first gloss with anything else shown alongside it, so the check never ran at all. Fixed by calling `archaicTagLabel()` unconditionally for every rendered entry, not just duplicates; `demoted` styling still only applies to genuine duplicates lacking a real tag, so an ordinary non-duplicate entry with no archaic signal stays a normal, undemoted card exactly as before. Re-validated against both reported cases post-fix (correctly showing "archaic" now) and against the full corpus — `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 11 patterns unchanged throughout, since this is popup-render-only, not a tokenization/grouping change. **Second same-day follow-up: `m`/`ki` were themselves whole-entry unions, not aligned to the specific sense/reading actually shown — fixed by making both `jmdict-compact.json` fields reflect only the primary sense/reading.** Once `archaicTagLabel()` started running unconditionally (the fix above), a deeper pre-existing flaw surfaced immediately: こと's entry has `arch` on its SECOND sense ("stringed instrument") while its primary first sense ("koto, 13-stringed zither") isn't archaic at all, and 君 has the same shape (`obs` on its third sense, "he/she", not its primary "you/buddy" sense) — the old `m` (a deduped union across all 3 included senses) wrongly tagged the whole common-word card "archaic"/"obsolete" from a LATER, less-relevant sense. じゃない showed the reading-side version of the same bug: its own primary reading carries no misc/reading tags at all, but its alternate ぢゃない reading is tagged `ik`, and the old `ki` (a union across every reading) leaked that onto the card even though ぢゃない was never the reading being displayed. Regenerated `jmdict-compact.json` again the same day: `m` is now just the first included sense's own misc tags, `ki` just the primary reading's (`w.kana[0]`) own tags — validated 100% match on every other field, with `m`/`ki` themselves only ever narrowing (8,414 and 16,587 index keys respectively), never gaining a tag. Re-confirmed the original 2026-07-05 validation cases (あたし demoted correctly, わらわ still "archaic", 酒's ささ reading still "dated") are unaffected, since those all carry their tag on a single included sense/reading with no union to begin with. Known residual gap, not built: if a user clicks a specific alternate reading (via the `rs`-swap multi-reading-precision mechanism) that's itself archaic/dated while the entry's primary reading isn't, the badge won't reflect that specific reading — no real case of this has turned up, so not built pre-emptively.
- **`jmdict-compact.json` regenerated from scratch (2026-07-05), replacing incremental patching.** The original 2026-06-24 generation script was never saved to this repo; a same-day-earlier attempt to fix a TUBELEX scoring bug (子: ね ranking above こ) surfaced that the file's accumulated multi-run history meant a tied array-position tiebreak no longer reliably reflected true JMdict order for entries a now-fixed bug had once mis-scored. Rebuilt `scripts/generate-jmdict-compact.js` by reverse-engineering the exact algorithm against the current file and a freshly-downloaded raw `jmdict-eng-3.6.2` release, then validating statistically before trusting it: 99.96%+ exact match on `r`/`g`/`p` and 99.98%+ on `c` across all 217k+ entries, with the small residual traced to genuine version drift between raw-release snapshots (different gloss text entirely, not a capping/ordering difference) rather than an algorithm error. `fix-jmdict-priority.js` (kana-primary/`kp` tagging) and `scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js` (frequency scoring/reordering, including the 2026-07-05 POS-gate fix) both still run as separate passes afterward, unchanged — this script only rebuilds the base `r`/`g`/`p`/`c`/index/`posTags` data plus the two new fields (`rs`, `m`/`ki`, see above) needed for the multi-reading and duplicate-gloss fixes. Re-running the full pipeline changed the top-ranked entry for 82 of 17,512 multi-entry index keys (0.47%) — every sampled change was a legitimate frequency-driven correction (e.g. 軍: いくさ → ぐん, the far more common modern reading), none a regression. File grew from ~30.8MB to ~33.1MB (new fields on ~1 in 6 entries).
- **Word-frequency data (superseded 2026-07-03).** jmdict-simplified's public JSON exposes only a collapsed `common: boolean`, not the underlying priority tier (ichi1/ichi2, newsN, nfXX) — confirmed 2026-07-01 against the raw `jmdict-eng-3.6.2.json` release; `tags` only carries reading-form annotations (ateji, gikun, rK/rk/sK/sk, oK/ok, iK/ik), never priority markers. This gap is now closed for homograph ordering via TUBELEX-JA (BSD-3-Clause, YouTube-subtitle-derived, 337,757 lemmas) — see the next entry. The same TUBELEX data now also powers the real, graduated frequency-rank toggle decided 2026-07-04 (Section 4), which replaces the earlier `common`-flag-based toggle.
- **Homograph/sense ordering via TUBELEX (2026-07-03, two-part fix same day).** `scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js` re-ranks every multi-entry index key by real usage frequency, superseding the earlier rK/sK kanji-rarity heuristic wherever real data exists (that heuristic remains the fallback tiebreak where TUBELEX has no data for either candidate). Needs no raw jmdict-eng re-download: `jmdict-compact.json`'s own index already links every kanji spelling to the same entry object as its kana reading (e.g. `index["こと"]`, `index["事"]`, and `index["琴"]` all point to shared entries), so per-entry frequency is derived by inverting the existing index and taking the max TUBELEX count across all surface forms pointing to each entry — no new field added, file size unchanged (30,772,667 bytes). First pass: 59,703 of 217,625 entries matched a nonzero frequency, 6,624 index keys reordered, fixing こと-style sense ordering (事 "thing/matter": 1,105,160 vs 琴 "zither": 372). Same-day follow-up fixed a cross-reading contamination bug in that first pass: taking the *max* frequency across every key pointing to an entry let a rare sense borrow an unrelated common word's frequency via a shared reading (そうか's "Buddhist temple" sense outranking its own common interjection sense by borrowing 宗家's frequency; だ's own high frequency outranking だ-as-copula under a shared index key). Fixed by checking, ahead of raw frequency: (1) whether a KANA-ONLY key matches the entry's own displayed reading (kanji keys are exempt — excluding them dropped coverage from 59,703 to 9,450 entries, since TUBELEX's lemma field is often in kanji); (2) JMdict's own curated `common` flag, since an automated MeCab-based frequency tool can fail to lemmatize a casual multi-morpheme interjection like そうか as its own token, so "no data" isn't reliably distinguishable from "genuinely rare." Re-validated: 59,011 entries scored, 2,999 keys reordered, `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 6 pattern counts unchanged (89/38/53/24/397/125). Also fixed the 大事なことさ bug (な showing だ+た as equally-weighted cards) as a side effect.
- **いる/居る JMdict ordering (2026-07-01):** fixed using rK/sK ("rarely-used kanji") tags already present in the source — an entry whose kanji forms are all rK/sK-tagged (or has none) is conventionally kana-written, so it ranks first for a kana-only lookup key. Directly fixes 居る ranking above 射る/炒る. Matched back to `jmdict-compact.json` by (reading, first gloss) fingerprint (217,601/217,625 matched). Later reinforced with real usage data by the TUBELEX pass above, which supersedes this heuristic wherever it has data.
- **TUBELEX scoring now gated on POS-category compatibility (2026-07-05).** `scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js` only counts a TUBELEX lemma's frequency toward an entry's score if the lemma's own UniDic POS class (e.g. `助詞`, `名詞`) maps to the same coarse category as at least one of the entry's JMdict POS codes. Fixes cross-category contamination the earlier same-reading-only check couldn't catch: 子's ね-reading entry (zodiac sign, JMdict `n`) was inheriting the sentence-final particle ね's enormous frequency, since TUBELEX aggregates ALL real-world ね usage under one `助詞`-tagged row regardless of JMdict sense. Lenient by design (same lesson as the earlier coverage-collapse mistake): an unmapped TUBELEX category or an entry with no mappable JMdict category is always allowed through. 57,596 of 217,625 entries scored (down from 59,011, as expected — some previously-counted matches were contamination), 519 keys reordered, `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 6 corpus patterns unchanged. The resulting 子 (ね-vs-こ) tie was itself an array-position artifact from `jmdict-compact.json`'s accumulated multi-run history, not true JMdict order — resolved same day by the full regeneration above, which the tie's discovery directly prompted.
- **`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana` and `collapseVowelElongation` moved to `tokenize-utils.js` (2026-07-05), shared by content.js and background.js.** Half-width katakana normalization previously ran only at the background.js lookup layer (fixing click resolution but not the on-screen display); content.js now also normalizes the raw subtitle text before tokenization, so half-width releases (e.g. VCB-Studio) display full-width on screen too. `collapseVowelElongation` is new: a small vowel written directly after its own large-vowel counterpart (おぉ) is a real expressive-writing convention with no corresponding JMdict reading variant (confirmed against the raw release: the real "oh!" entry only lists おお/おう/おー/オー) — used as a lookupWord fallback only, never applied to the displayed text, since the elongated spelling is a deliberate stylistic choice worth keeping visible.
- **Godan potential-form reverse-conjugation fallback (2026-07-05).** `derivePotentialFormBase`/`selectPotentialFormMatches` in `tokenize-utils.js`, used by background.js's `lookupWord` as a last-resort fallback: reverse-maps a word's final e-row kana to its u-row counterpart (輝ける → 輝く) and looks that up instead, filtered to godan-tagged (`v5*`) entries, when the direct lookup fails. Needed because kuromoji doesn't recognize every godan potential form as an inflection at all (輝ける tokenizes as one opaque unknown-word token with zero conjugation signal, unlike て/た/etc. absorption, which always has a kuromoji-provided signal to key off). Prefers an irregular-verb match (`vk`) over a godan one when both exist under the same derived key — confirmed necessary for 来れる (ら抜き of the irregular verb 来る/くる), which without this preference reverse-derived to the same kanji key as きたる, an unrelated godan verb ("next, forthcoming") also spelled 来る. Validated via a new `scripts/batch-test.js` Pattern 9 (8/8 real corpus resolutions confirmed correct after the vk-preference fix).
- **Katakana proper-noun un-suppression (2026-07-05).** `findKatakanaUnsuppressCandidates`/`applyKatakanaUnsuppress` in `tokenize-utils.js` — the mirror image of the existing katakana-name suppression (which un-clicks a word with no JMdict entry): groupTokens' hasProperNoun rule silences an ENTIRE katakana run as soon as any token in it is kuromoji-tagged 固有名詞, without ever checking whether the full run is actually a real word. Confirmed real: チヤホヤ (an ordinary onomatopoeic verb, "to be pampered/fussed over") mis-tags its first half チヤ as 固有名詞, silencing a real word. Existence-only gate on the whole run's surface, same reliability precedent as the original katakana-name check. Validated via a new `scripts/batch-test.js` Pattern 8 (7/7 real corpus resolutions confirmed correct — アンデッド, エルフ ×2, ドワーフ, フフッ, フン, ディー).

**kuromoji.js limitations (found via Phase 4 testing):**

- **`word_type` can't distinguish segmentation errors from correct parses** (confirmed 2026-07-01): both a genuine kuromoji mistake and a correct parse get tagged `"KNOWN"`; length/adjacency heuristics are the only reliable signal for that class of bug.
- **Has no working runtime user-dictionary feature** — confirmed 2026-07-01 that the bundled library's `"USER"` node-type branch is dead code (`// TODO User dictionary`, never implemented by the library's own author). Any future "teach it new words" feature needs either an offline dictionary recompile or an application-level merge mechanism (see Kana-merge below), not a runtime CSV load.
- **Per-token `reading` is not a reliable signal for sense disambiguation** (checked 2026-07-03, not built on). Tested as a candidate anchor for kanji-heteronym sense ordering (一行 = いっこう "party/group" vs いちぎょう "one line"): unreliable both in the reported case (勇者一行 triggers a Viterbi-cost segmentation split with no usable single-token reading at all) and in general (correct for 大分, wrong for 十分/人気, which are missing their alternate readings entirely). Concluded not solid enough to build a fix on; logged as an accepted limitation (Section 8) rather than pursued further.
- **Benchmark lindera-wasm/Vibrato-wasm only if real subtitle-corpus testing shows a quality gap** — not pre-emptive; no gap has surfaced yet (Section 8).
- **Doesn't recognize every godan potential-form conjugation** (confirmed 2026-07-05): 輝ける (potential form of 輝く) tokenizes as a single opaque unknown-word token (`連体詞`, `conjugated_type`/`conjugated_form` both `*`) with zero signal connecting it to 輝く at all — unlike て/た/etc. inflections, there's no kuromoji-provided conjugation info to key a grouping rule off here. Worked around at the JMdict-lookup layer instead (reverse-derive the plain form and look that up) — see Dictionary-data precision above.

**Popup content & display:**

- **Popup POS labels (2026-07-03):** `content.js`'s `POS_LABELS`/`posLabel()`/`formatPosChips()` centralize JMdict POS-code → short-label formatting (e.g. `n` → "Noun", `vs` → "する-verb", all `v5*` codes → "Godan verb"), applied at every popup render site. Cuts kokugo-grammar-school parentheticals ((futsuumeishi), (keiyoushi), etc.) and the redundant "(common)" tag on the default noun case; caps at 3 chips per sense. `n-pr` (proper noun) has a label ready but is currently unreachable — JMdict's proper-noun data lives in the separate JMnedict database, which this project doesn't ingest. **くる/する self-headword exception:** `IRREGULAR_VERB_SELF_CODES` suppresses the "Kuru verb"/"する-verb" chip only when くる/する (or their rare kanji spellings 来る/為る) is the resolved headword itself, since the label would just restate the headword in grammatical-term form — kept for every other case, e.g. a compound where くる/する is a component (持ってくる) or a noun+する pairing (凱旋, tagged `vs` not `vs-i`/`vs-s`/`vs-c` so it's unaffected either way), where the label is real, non-obvious information. Unlike Ichidan/Godan (kept as-is everywhere), くる/する as standalone headwords have no comparable ambiguity to disambiguate.
- **Furigana centering fix (2026-07-03):** `content.css`'s `.jp-immersion-popup-reading` (the `<ruby>` headword element) had `display: block`, which overrides the browser's native ruby layout algorithm responsible for centering `<rt>` over the base text, falling back to plain block stacking instead. Fixed with explicit `ruby-position: over`; the element still participates correctly in its flex-row parent since flex items get their outer display auto-"blockified" regardless of this property. Only fixes centering for the existing group/jukugo-ruby style (whole reading over the whole compound) — mono-ruby (per-kanji distributed furigana) is a separate, deliberately-deferred Phase 6 item.
- **Popup inflection line (2026-07-03, two-part rework same day; extended 2026-07-05).** `content.js`'s `INFLECTION_LABELS`/`describeInflection()` replaced the old "Dictionary form: X (inflected: Y-form)" line with native-morpheme labels (て-form, た-form, etc.), no line at all when there's nothing to show. Noun+plain-copula groupings (凱旋+です) get an explicit `+ です (copula)` label instead of being mislabeled as the noun's own inflection — depends on a new `conjugatedForm` field carried on every Rule-3-path group (display-only, doesn't affect merge/grouping), needed to detect imperative (命令形), which never appears in the `inflections` array at all. `INFLECTION_LABELS` also carries the labels for the newly-mergeable auxiliaries (-ば; られる/れる (passive); せる/させる (causative); -たら/-だら (conditional); contracted てる/てた/てく/てき; なく as ない's connective form). Every label includes a short plain-English parenthetical (て-form → "(connective)", ない-form → "(negative)", etc.); だ/です split into three distinct wordings based on whether the preceding token is 名詞 or 形容詞: noun+plain だ → "(copula)"; noun+polite です → "(polite copula)"; い-adjective+です → "(polite)" only, since です there adds only politeness to an already-complete predicate, no copula-linking work. な (だ's attributive/体言接続 form, e.g. 大事な) is labeled "な (attributive)"; んだ/んです (Rule 0.6's construction) is labeled "んだ (explanatory)" / "んです (explanatory, polite)", and its extended んだろう/んでしょう form (2026-07-05) gets its own "んだろう (explanatory, conjecture)" / "んでしょう (explanatory, conjecture, polite)" label instead of falling through to the raw chain text. い-adjective's conjunctive/adverbial く-form (うまく in うまくなる), which isn't absorbed into anything by any grouping rule, also gets a "く-form (adverbial)" label (2026-07-05). なら (だ's conditional/仮定形 form) still has the identical labeling gap as な originally did — flagged, not fixed, out of scope of what's been asked so far.
- **Small っ (sokuon) is 2 UTF-16 code units**, not 1 (confirmed 2026-07-01) — relevant for any future kana-length heuristic; don't assume "1 mora = 1 character" for small kana (っ/ゃ/ゅ/ょ).
- **だ/です plain-copula vs. conjecture-form (でしょ/だろ):** share the same `basic_form`; the only distinguishing field is `conjugated_form` (`基本形` plain terminal vs. `未然形` imperfective stem+volitional). This is Rule 3's allowlist signal, below.
- **Kanji spellings on homograph cards, built (2026-07-06).** A kana-only lookup that resolves to multiple entries (いる → 居る/入る/要る) previously showed three visually-identical kana headers with no way to tell the cards apart at a glance — each card now shows its kanji spelling(s) (`entry.k`, joined with "・" if more than one) next to the header instead. Fact-finding check confirmed `jmdict-compact.json` was NOT retaining kanji spellings at all (see the `k`-field regeneration entry above), so this needed the regen, not a pure display change. Gated on `NO_KANJI_RE` (`content.js`) — only shown when the CLICKED word itself has no kanji of its own, so a direct kanji click (分かる) doesn't get a redundant kanji-spelling badge repeating what the ruby headword already shows.
- **Numbered gloss display, built (2026-07-06).** Senses render with visible numbering (1., 2., 3.) instead of running together unnumbered, matching the ManabiDojo-style reference layout, via the `g` sense-grouping added to `jmdict-compact.json` (Dictionary-data precision above). POS chips stay a single line per entry (unchanged) rather than repeating per numbered sense — the reference layout's repeated "Verb, Transitive" per sense wasn't part of the approved scope (gloss numbering + cap bump only), and `jmdict-compact.json`'s `p` field is still a whole-entry deduped union, not per-sense data. **Confirmed working as designed in live testing (2026-07-06, later round):** a sense showing fewer than 3 glosses (e.g. こと's sense 1 showing only 2) is expected — some JMdict senses simply don't have 3 glosses to show — not a bug, no change made.
- **れる/られる/せる/させる + ない (or なく) combined inflection label added (2026-07-12).** These absorb ない as a SEPARATE second `inflections` entry (信じられない → `["られ", "ない"]`, the suffix's own 未然形 stem, not the `INFLECTION_LABELS`-keyed dictionary form) — no existing branch matched a 2-part chain, so it fell through to the raw untranslated join ("られない") while every other inflection got a plain-English label. New branch reconstructs the dictionary form from the stem (られ→られる) and combines both labels: "られる/れる (passive) + ない-form (negative)". Scoped to exactly this family+ない/なく shape, not a general chained-inflection composer (see the んだろう/んでしょう precedent above for why chains don't get a synthesized label in general).
- **Kanji-spelling filtering by rK/sK/oK/iK tags, built (2026-07-06, later round) — scope narrowed from the original decision after implementation.** `jmdict-compact.json`'s generation script now excludes rK/sK/oK/iK-tagged kanji forms from `k` at generation time (confirmed real counts in the raw release: sK 14831, rK 5732, iK 604, oK 538) — simpler than the "needs per-form tags" data-model change anticipated when this was approved, since `k` only ever needs to carry the GOOD spellings, not a filterable superset. Fixes both reported bugs: 来る's `k` is now just `["来る"]` (來る, sK, excluded); じゃない's `k` is now omitted entirely (both じゃ無い and ぢゃ無い are sK — correctly resolved to "no kanji spelling shown," not a fallback to showing them anyway, see the caught bug below). **`rs` (alternate kana readings) deliberately NOT touched** — re-examining the original decision found `rs` is never actually displayed anywhere in the popup at all (grepped `content.js`: zero references); it's purely an internal background.js mechanism for resolving to whichever reading the user actually clicked (くる → 刳る's えぐる-vs-くる precision fix). Filtering it would directly contradict the same live-testing round's own reaffirmed "show what was actually on screen as primary" principle (see the display-placement confirmation below) — there's no current display surface for kana-reading filtering to apply to. **Bug caught and fixed during implementation:** the first attempt fell back to the FULL unfiltered kanji list whenever every form was tagged rare, reasoning "a genuinely kanji-only word shouldn't end up with zero spellings" — but real-corpus validation on じゃない showed this reasoning was backwards: an entry where EVERY kanji form is rare/search-only IS exactly the "conventionally kana-only" signal, and the fallback was reintroducing ぢゃ無い, the exact bug being fixed. Removed the fallback; validated the fix now correctly empties `k` for じゃない. **Confirmed desirable side effect:** いる's own "to be" sense (居る, already known rK-tagged from the pre-existing いる sense-ordering fix) also loses its kanji-spelling badge now — the same underlying signal correctly reused for a second purpose, not a separate fix. Validated against the pre-change file: 100% match on every field except `k`, which only ever shrank (52,729 of 462,753 index keys), never grew or changed unexpectedly. `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 11 corpus patterns unchanged.
- **Kanji-spelling display placement — confirmed as-is, no change (2026-07-06, later round).** The clicked surface form always displays as primary (big text), with the kanji spelling shown as a small secondary annotation only when the clicked surface was kana — consistent with how a directly-clicked kanji surface (倒す) already displays kanji-primary/furigana-secondary. One rule ("show what was actually on screen as primary"), not two separate behaviors to maintain — matches the product's "confirm what's on screen" principle over a standard dictionary's kanji-first convention.
- **そうか showing unrelated nouns (層化, 装花), fixed (2026-07-06, later round).** Root cause confirmed as suspected: `そうか` is ALWAYS reached via the phrase-matcher's existence-gated fuse mechanism (kuromoji tokenizes it そう(副詞)+か(助詞), never as one natural token), and fuse-outcome groups never set a `pos` field at all, so background.js's existing `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS` filter silently never activates for any phrase-fused word. **Fix:** `groupTokens`' fuse-outcome branch now sets a sentinel `pos: "__phraseFuse"` (not a real kuromoji tag — a fused span can cover tokens of mixed POS with no single natural category to inherit); `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS["__phraseFuse"]` matches JMdict `exp`/`int` codes, the closest fit for a fused colloquial expression. Corpus-validated precisely (not just spot-checked): of 181 distinct real fuse-outcome words in the 3-show corpus, 84 have no exp/int-tagged entry at all (the existing "never narrow to zero" safety net leaves these untouched, exactly as before) and 11 get correctly narrowed (そうか → just "is that so?", おい → just "hey!", よし → just "alright," etc. — every single dropped entry was a genuinely unrelated homograph noun, none a legitimate lost sense). Confirmed the fix correctly discriminates by context, not a blind override: なんで tokenizes as ONE natural token (pos 副詞) in its common "why?" usage, entirely bypassing this new sentinel and keeping its existing correct adv-tagged filtering; only the separate, genuinely fuse-requiring usage (恩人なんで, functioning like だから) gets `__phraseFuse` and correctly resolves to "because" instead. `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 11 corpus patterns unchanged (this only adds a `pos` field to fuse groups; classification logic itself is untouched).
- **s_inf (sense-info) annotation display, built (2026-07-15).** Raised by a よ popup report: sense 1 showed a bare "hey; you" with no indication it's the sentence-final particle used for certainty/emphasis/contempt/etc. — that qualifier is real JMdict data (`s_inf`), just never carried into the compact format at all (confirmed by reading `generate-jmdict-compact.js` before assuming). The report's own proposed scope (prt/int-primary entries only, reusing `isFunctionWordPrimary()`) was checked against a real sample from the raw release before building, not assumed: prt (62.8%) and int (23.3%) senses do carry a note far more often than content-word POS (nouns 1.5%, verbs 3-6%, adjectives 2-7%), confirming function words need this more — but when a content-word sense DOES have one, it's typically substantive grammatical-construction info (らしい: "after the plain form of a verb or adjective...") or kanji-nuance disambiguation (あう: "逢う is often used for close friends... 遭う may have an undesirable nuance"), not the register/dialect info the report assumed would already be covered by the archaic badge. Built as a universal field instead of POS-scoped: `jmdict-compact.json`'s `si` (new field, parallel array to `g`, one string per visible sense, "" when absent) is populated for ANY entry, regardless of POS. Cost negligible either way (only 6,668 of 252,198 senses in the whole raw release carry a note, ~160KB raw text). Rendered in `content.js` as a muted parenthetical (`.jp-immersion-popup-gloss-note`, 12px/#777, matching the existing POS-line treatment) appended as a child node of the gloss span itself (not a new flex sibling), so long notes (p90 ~47 chars, max 181) wrap as ordinary inline text within the popup's existing 320px max-width/no-max-height layout instead of needing their own truncation handling. Required a full regen pipeline run (`generate-jmdict-compact.js` → `fix-jmdict-priority.js` → `apply-tubelex-frequency.js`, in order) since this touches the base compact-generation script.
- **それもそうか-style multi-word idiomatic dual-view — confirmed no new code needed (2026-07-06).** Fact-finding against the raw JMdict release found それもそうか itself has no headword, but several close variants do (それもそうだ, そういえば, そうだね, そうだよね, そっか). Manually verified all of these already fuse correctly through the EXISTING general phrase-matcher (`findPhraseMatchCandidates`/`classifyAndSelectPhraseMatches`, existing existence-gated multi-token-window mechanism, no changes) — no extension was actually needed. それもそうか itself (no headword) correctly falls through to a two-part fuse instead (それも + そうか), both of which are independently real, correctly-defined JMdict entries — not a broken/misleading split, just a graceful compositional fallback. Nothing built; this was a confirm-not-fix outcome, same shape as the じゃない and archaic-badge findings from the same live-testing round.

**Grouping/merge pipeline (content-script segmentation logic):**

`tokenize-utils.js` runs several post-tokenization passes before rendering; `groupTokens(tokens, fuseSpans)` is the core grouping function. Current rule set (as of 2026-07-05):

- **Rule 0** — Honorific prefix (お弁当 → looks up 弁当). **Now records an inflection note (2026-07-13):** "お (honorific prefix)" / "ご (honorific prefix)", matching Rule 1/3/0.6 (previously the only absorption rule with no note at all). Doesn't fire for お母さん/お客様-style words — those are their own single lexicalized JMdict headwords (お already part of kuromoji's own `basic_form`), never reaching Rule 0.
- **Honorific SUFFIX (さん/さま/くん/ちゃん/様/氏), the mirror of Rule 0 (2026-07-13).** Not a merge rule — the preceding content word already gets its own correct group; this only fixes the suffix token's OWN lookup, which was resolving to unrelated ordinary-noun homographs (さん → 酸/三/讃) since the generic noun-category POS filter can't exclude same-category nouns. See Dictionary-data precision above (`isHonorificSuffix`) for the filtering mechanism; the detection signal here is kuromoji's own uniform 名詞/接尾/人名 tagging, confirmed general across all four honorific suffixes, not word-specific.
- **Trailing emphatic っ (dramatic vocal cutoff — やめろっ, 行けっ) suppressed as a POST-kana-merge pass, not inside `groupTokens` (2026-07-13).** IPADIC has no consistent tag for a bare sokuon after an imperative/conjugated verb form with nothing following it — it reliably emits a bogus standalone 動詞/非自立 token (`basic_form: "く"`), independently clickable and resolving to く's unrelated homographs (句/区/九) before this fix. **Ordering lesson, caught via batch-test regression:** an earlier version suppressed this token from inside `groupTokens` itself, which silently pre-empted kana-merge's own correct handling of ん+っ (a real, distinct JMdict headword, んっ "hm?; huh?") — kana-merge's candidate scan requires two adjacent, un-suppressed hiragana groups, so absorbing っ away before kana-merge ever ran made that merge impossible. Fixed by running `suppressTrailingSokuon` as its own pass AFTER `applyKanaMerges`, so a real merge gets first claim on the token and this only ever fires on a っ that survived unmerged.
- **Rule 0.5** — Pronoun + pluralizing suffix (私たち, 僕たち): merged deterministically, no JMdict check, since the pattern is always grammatical.
- **Rule 0.6** — ん + copula-family (んだ/んです/んだろう/んでしょう): deterministic, looks up のだ directly and keeps the copula chain as the inflection label. Needed because JMdict has a headword for んだ but not んだろう/んでしょう.
- **Rule 1** — Te-form: [動詞]+[て/で] → one group (main verb); the following auxiliary gets its own group, so the learner can look up both. Also handles Rule 2 (contracted te-form auxiliary, 見てる → 見ている; now records the contraction itself as the inflection, see Popup content & display above — previously discarded, so 見てる/してる showed no inflection note at all).
- **Rule 3** — Allowlisted auxiliary absorption: only `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` (た/ない/たい/せる/させる/れる/られる/う) and the plain copula (だ/です, `基本形`) absorb into the preceding word. Everything else tagged 助動詞 (らしい/べき/まい) does not absorb and typically gets picked up correctly by the phrase-matcher instead.
  - **でしょ/だろ exception (2026-07-03):** these share です/だ's `basic_form` but are the conjecture stem (`conjugated_form === "未然形"`, not `基本形`) — でしょう/だろう, including the bare でしょ/だろ colloquial form with no う following, have their own JMdict headword ("probably; I guess"). The group's lookup word is the reconstructed surface, not the copula's own basic_form, so でしょう/だろう resolves to its own definition instead of です/だ's plain "be; is." **Confirmed working as designed in live testing (2026-07-06, later round):** だろ resolves to its own genuine, standalone JMdict headword via this same override, with no inflection note shown — expected, same mechanism and same no-note behavior as じゃない (both resolve directly to a complete headword with no remaining "inflected form of X" relationship left to explain). No action needed.
  - **せる/させる/れる/られる gap fixed (2026-07-03):** these are listed in `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` but were never actually reachable — the absorption check required `pos === "助動詞"`, while kuromoji always tags these `動詞` with `pos_detail_1 === "接尾"` (verb-suffix). Confirmed a pre-existing gap, not a regression; fixed by also accepting that tag combination. Each was previously independently clickable as its own token.
  - **ば conditional absorption added (2026-07-03, new scope):** 助詞 ば now absorbs into a preceding verb/adjective already conjugated to `仮定形` (見れば, 聞けば) — mirrors the たり/だり precedent (surface-form check on a 助詞) with an added conjugation gate, since bare ば has an unrelated real noun sense ("place") that only the conjugation context rules out. Approved via the project's litmus test: two separate clicks is worse than one click on the real conditional-inflected verb, same reasoning that justified て/た merging.
  - **だら/たら past-tense-conditional absorption added (2026-07-05):** verb-past-tense だら (voiced variant of た's own 仮定形 たら, e.g. 読んだら, 組んだら) now also absorbs — it was missing because only `"た"`, not `"だ"`, is in `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX`. Disambiguated from copula だ's own conditional form (なら, 学生なら, which shares the identical `basic_form`/`conjugated_form`) via `conjugated_type` (`特殊・タ` for the verb auxiliary's paradigm vs. `特殊・ダ` for the copula's) — deliberately does not touch なら, a separate already-flagged gap.
  - **な (終助詞) directly followed by a noun is treated as だ's attributive form, not the sentence-final particle (2026-07-05):** kuromoji occasionally mis-tags the attributive な as `助詞/終助詞` instead of `助動詞/体言接続` when the preceding word wasn't recognized as its own token at all (ひとりぼっちな子 — ひとりぼっち fragments into unrelated verb stems in kuromoji's own dictionary, throwing off its parse of な). A genuine sentence-final な never precedes a noun with nothing between them, so this is a safe structural correction, not a hardcoded exception.
  - **の tagged 名詞/非自立 (formal/dummy noun) treated as the nominalizer/explanatory particle, not a real noun (2026-07-12):** kuromoji tags nominalizer の as 助詞/終助詞 after a plain verb dictionary form (かけるの, already handled by the isParticle branch above), but as 名詞/非自立 after an inflected form like ない (なれないのだ) — confirmed via direct tokenizer testing. Left unflagged, this skipped particle filtering entirely and showed の's unrelated noun homographs (野/幅・布/篦) instead of the nominalizer sense. Scoped to `basic_form === "の"` specifically — other genuine 名詞/非自立 formal nouns (こと, もの, とき, わけ, はず) each have their own real headword and must stay untouched.
- **Katakana-only proper-noun runs** merge into one fully inert (`word: null`, no click, no underline) span if any token is tagged 固有名詞, or — for names kuromoji's own tag misses (e.g. ヒンメル/アイゼン, inconsistently tagged 一般) — if a standalone katakana word has no real JMdict entry at all (async existence check). **Un-suppression pass added (2026-07-05):** the hasProperNoun rule above silences the WHOLE run the instant any token is tagged 固有名詞, without ever checking whether the run as a whole is a real word — `findKatakanaUnsuppressCandidates`/`applyKatakanaUnsuppress` run as a later async stage (mirroring the existing katakana-name-suppression check in the opposite direction) and un-suppress a run that turns out to have a real JMdict entry (チヤホヤ, mis-tagged 固有名詞 on its first half チヤ). Kanji-containing names now behave as ordinary clickable words (2026-07-05, implementing the 2026-07-04 decision): kuromoji still tags individual kanji-name tokens 固有名詞, but `content.js`'s click handler no longer silently closes the popup on a lookup miss for these — it shows the normal "no dictionary entry" message like any other word. This only ever affected kanji names in practice, since katakana-only runs never reach the click handler at all (already inert via the rule above, before a click is possible).
- **Numeral runs built (2026-07-05, later session), implementing the 2026-07-05 decision.** `NUMERAL_ONLY_RE` (Arabic digit glyphs, half- or full-width) is checked in `groupTokens` BEFORE the `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` non-match branch, not by extending `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` itself as originally scoped — digits are outside that regex's ranges entirely, and extending it risked leaking numeral tokens into unrelated logic keyed on it (phrase-matching windows, kana-merge, katakana-run detection). Adjacent numeral tokens merge into one surface (kuromoji tokenizes a half-width run as ONE token — `23` → one `"23"` token — but a full-width run digit-by-digit — `２３` → `"２"`+`"３"` — confirmed empirically before building this). `background.js`'s `lookupWord` normalizes ASCII digits to full-width for the index lookup (jmdict-compact.json already carries real entries for 0-9, most round tens/hundreds/thousands, and 10000 under full-width keys — confirmed directly against the file) and falls back to `numberToReading` (`tokenize-utils.js`) — a closed, deterministic Japanese-numeral-construction algorithm (base-10000 grouping via 万/億/兆/京, with the real sound-change irregulars for 百/千: さんびゃく/ろっぴゃく/はっぴゃく/さんぜん/はっせん, and 万/億/兆 keeping an explicit いち where 十/百 drop it) — for every other combination, since that's an unbounded combinatorial space no static dictionary enumerates. `content.js`'s `buildHeadword` was generalized from "show ruby furigana only if the word contains kanji" to "show ruby furigana whenever `word !== reading`" (the kanji check was only ever a proxy for that), so a numeral's reading displays correctly (23 → にじゅうさん) instead of replacing the visible "23" outright. Corpus-validated via a new `scripts/batch-test.js` Pattern 10 (36 real occurrences across the 3-show corpus, all resolved via existing JMdict entries — the synthesis fallback was separately validated offline against 23/123/2024/9999/10001/0).
- **Multi-kanji personal/place-name run fusion built (2026-07-05, later session), implementing the 2026-07-05 decision — approach refined from the original "existence-check fuse" spec after corpus testing.** kuromoji often recognizes only the SURNAME half of a full name as 固有名詞, while the given-name half falls through as ordinary kanji it happens to also know as unrelated common words (伊地知虹夏 → 伊地知(固有名詞) + 虹("rainbow", 一般) + 夏("summer", 一般)) — confirmed this is the real shape of the reported bug, not literally 後藤 splitting (後藤 already tokenizes as one kuromoji token in every context tested; "後藤-style" was evidently a descriptive label, not a literal repro). Anchored on a 固有名詞-tagged kanji token and extended via `extendsNameRun`/`findNameRunEnd` (`tokenize-utils.js`): a further 固有名詞-tagged token extends at any length, but a `一般`-tagged token only extends when it's a single kanji character — the signature of kuromoji being forced into character-by-character fallback because it doesn't recognize the combination as a word at all, which is what actually distinguishes an unrecognized given-name fragment (虹, 夏) from a real compound noun kuromoji already knows as one word (高校 in 下北沢高校, "Shimokitazawa High School"). **This length gate is why the fix deviates from the original spec:** an unconditional existence-check fuse (fuse only if the whole span has a JMdict entry) would NOT have fixed the primary reported case at all, since 伊地知虹夏 itself has no JMdict entry — the fix instead always fuses a detected run into one lookup unit, using existence only to decide what's displayed (a real coincidental hit like 胡桃 shows normally; a miss shows one honest "no dictionary entry" message instead of two disconnected wrong ones, matching the already-decided kanji-proper-noun UX). Corpus-validated via a new `scripts/batch-test.js` Pattern 11, called directly on raw tokens (not inferred from final grouped output, since the general phrase-matcher's own noun+noun fuse — 王都, etc. — can independently produce the identical group shape and would double-count work this rule didn't do): exactly 2 real firings across the 3-show corpus — 伊地知虹夏 (no entry, the intended fix) and 王都 (has entry, a harmless overlap with the phrase-matcher's own noun+noun fuse, same correct result either way). **Known accepted false-positive risk:** kuromoji's tags can't distinguish this from an ordinary place-name + noun compound with no dictionary entry of its own — not eliminated, just not observed in this corpus; the single-kanji-only extension gate specifically exists to keep this risk low (it correctly kept 下北沢高校 split into its two real, correctly-defined parts during testing, rather than sweeping it into one no-entry unit).

**Phrase-matching** (`findPhraseMatchCandidates`/`classifyAndSelectPhraseMatches`/`applyPhraseMatches`) runs on **raw kuromoji tokens**, not `groupTokens`' output — required because 帰ったら tokenizes as one combined 帰っ+たら auxiliary token, so Rule 3 would already absorb it before any post-hoc scan on grouped output could see it. Implemented via a `fuseSpans` parameter threaded into `groupTokens` (fuse-outcome spans finalize before Rules 0–3 run on those positions) plus per-group `tokenStart`/`tokenEnd` tracking. Every accepted match resolves to one of two outcomes, selected together in one overlap pass (longest span wins):
- **fuse** — replaces individual tokens with one clickable unit (からといって, じゃない, んだ, にもかかわらず, ひとりぼっち). Gated on existence + (5+ characters, or every source token tagged 名詞, or a function-POS match, or two adjacent ≤2-character 動詞/形容詞 tokens — the signal for a kuromoji segmentation error like ぼっ+ち). A "baseline redundancy" filter rejects any span `groupTokens` would already produce correctly on its own (prevents the いた→板 "board" collision). **Extended to boundary-alignment, not just exact-match, rejection (2026-07-05):** a candidate span is now also rejected if either boundary falls in the *middle* of an existing baseline group instead of exactly on its edge — confirmed real for し+て+い (交換していた), where baseline grouping already produces して (Rule 1) + いた (Rule 3) as two separate groups, but the raw 3-token span してい cut across that boundary and coincidentally matched an unrelated real word ("designation"). Same fix also caught いいん in いいんだろう (the span ended inside Rule 0.6's ん+だろ+う group instead of at its edge).
- **dual-view** — attaches as a secondary "Also, as a set phrase" note without touching the individual tokens, when **both boundary tokens are genuine content words** (動詞/名詞/形容詞/副詞/連体詞) worth their own click regardless (もの…なる). If either boundary is a particle/auxiliary/copula-contraction, the match fully fuses instead. と (助詞) is an explicit exception, forced to dual-view even next to a negative verb (探さないと), since it retains a common, unrelated job (plain reported-speech/conditional と) a full fuse would wrongly override. **Pure noun+noun exception (2026-07-03):** a span where every token is 名詞 (e.g. 王都 = 王+都, no intervening particle) always fuses instead, even though both boundary tokens independently pass the content-word check — dual-view exists for cases like もの…なる where the boundary words stay independently meaningful across an intervening particle, not for two nouns directly adjacent forming an ordinary compound. Previously any all-noun span was wrongly classified dual-view first, burying the compound's own definition (e.g. 王都) under its first half's (王, "king"). **時 carve-out added (2026-07-05):** the dependent noun 時 (as in 遠足の時に) is now forced to dual-view the same way と is, since it retains a common, unrelated job as an ordinary noun far more often than it's the idiomatic とき-adverb/conjunction sense that gives 時に its JMdict `conj`/`adv` tag — a full fuse was replacing 時's own correct "time" gloss with the idiom's "by the way; sometimes" gloss. **外 carve-out added (2026-07-12):** same shape — 外(そと, "outside") + location に retains its common, unrelated ordinary-noun meaning far more often than the idiomatic 外に(ほかに) "else; in addition; besides" reading, which was winning the fuse gate on a coincidence: real JMdict tags that idiom's 外に kanji spelling `rK` (rare — real usage almost always writes 他に), but the compact-file index isn't filtered by that tag (only the display-only `k` field is), so the collision still existed underneath.

**Kana-merge** (`findKanaMergeCandidates`/`applyKanaMerges`) fixes a single word kuromoji fragmented (ただいま！→た+だ+いま), triggered by trailing hard punctuation or an embedded elongation mark (〜) against a hiragana run. The elongation branch needs only JMdict existence; the punctuation branch also requires a function-word POS match (prt/exp/conj/int/aux-v) — needed after real corpus false positives (はいい→"dethronement", あったか→"warm").

Both mechanisms only trigger an async background round-trip (`CHECK_KANA_MERGES`) when candidates actually exist; the common case (neither present) stays synchronous. Any future tightening/loosening needs empirical validation via `scripts/batch-test.js` (Patterns 5/6/7), not a one-off test sentence.

**Other Phase 4 fixes:**

- **Module export guard (2026-06-30):** `typeof module !== "undefined"` isn't a safe Node-only check in content scripts (`module` can exist in some browser/bundler contexts); changed to `typeof process !== "undefined"` in `tokenize-utils.js` and `subtitle-parser.js`.
- **Particle POS filtering (2026-06-30):** grammatical particles (は/が/に/で, etc.) have JMdict entries tagged `"prt"`; homophone common-noun entries (に→荷, は→歯, な→菜) lack that tag. An `isParticle` flag threaded content.js → background.js, filtered to `r.p?.includes("prt")`, gives correct grammatical definitions instead of misleading homophone nouns.
- **Half-width katakana (2026-06-30, root cause found 2026-07-01):** some subtitle releases (VCB-Studio) encode katakana in the half-width range (U+FF66–FF9F); `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` originally covered only full-width, leaving these non-clickable (fixed 2026-06-30 by adding the `ｦ-ﾟ` range). That fix made them *clickable* but not *resolvable* — `jmdict-compact.json`'s index is full-width-only, so every half-width click had silently shown "no entry" since Phase 3. Fixed 2026-07-01 with `normalizeHalfwidthKatakana()` applied at the single point every lookup passes through (the JMdict-index-lookup layer in `background.js`).
- **Manual subtitle upload, built (2026-07-06):** `buildUploadControl()` in `content.js` — a file input (`.srt`/`.ass`/`.ssa`) reading the local file via `FileReader.readAsText`, then feeding it into the existing `parseSrt`/`parseAss` (`subtitle-parser.js`, built in Phase 2 for Jimaku-sourced files) — no new parsing logic, just a new input path. `subtitle-parser.js` is now also loaded as a content script (`manifest.json`), not just imported into the background service worker, so `content.js` can call it directly without a message round-trip. Always visible (not just shown on a Jimaku failure), since a per-episode override is a real, expected case even when Jimaku succeeds. `init()`'s cue-rendering `timeupdate` listener is now attached once, unconditionally, reading from the shared `cues` variable — both the Jimaku-fetch success handler and the upload handler just reassign `cues`, rather than each attaching its own listener. Uploading forces an immediate re-render via a synthetic `timeupdate` dispatch, so a paused video reflects the new file right away instead of waiting for the next real timeupdate. Answers "shows with zero Jimaku subtitles" (Section 8) as well as the per-episode override need.

_This section will continue to grow as later-phase testing surfaces more segmentation/sync edge cases._

### Phase 4.5: Jimaku integration

No technical architecture locked in yet beyond the decisions below — this section will populate as design/build work begins. Design decisions so far (2026-07-05, planning session):

- **Show/episode auto-detection is a hard dependency for the rest of this phase.** Must be built and validated as its own standalone step first: every later step (ID-based Jimaku matching, ranked subtitle selection, auto-select, the switcher panel, per-show-per-season memory) depends on reliably knowing what show/episode is currently playing with zero user input. **Built 2026-07-15 — see the entry below; the "ID-based Jimaku matching" framing here is superseded, plain title matching turned out to be sufficient.**
