- **Jimaku search-by-title mismatch, fix decided (2026-07-04), moved here from Phase 6 (2026-07-05) — SUPERSEDED 2026-07-15, see the entry below and Decisions Log.** ~~Resolve via TMDB/AniList ID from Crunchyroll metadata, chosen over a fuzzy-search fallback despite more build effort, for reliability.~~ Real page inspection found Crunchyroll exposes no external ID at all (not TMDB, not AniList, not MAL) anywhere on the watch page — this decision assumed data that doesn't exist. Superseded by direct title matching, validated as sufficient in practice (see below) rather than assumed risky.
- **Show/episode auto-detection, built (2026-07-15).** `content.js`'s `detectShowEpisode()` parses Crunchyroll's own schema.org JSON-LD (a `TVEpisode` block already present on the page for SEO/rich-results purposes — confirmed via a real DevTools console dump on a logged-in watch page, since the page itself is behind a Cloudflare bot challenge and can't be fetched directly for inspection) for `partOfSeries.name` (series title), `partOfSeason.seasonNumber`, and `episodeNumber`. No external ID (TMDB/AniList/MAL) is present anywhere on the page — confirmed by grepping the full page HTML for all of those, zero hits — so the 2026-07-04 "resolve via TMDB/AniList ID" plan is superseded; would have required a THIRD-PARTY title-search hop (query TMDB/AniList's own API by title to get an ID) that reintroduces the exact same fuzzy-title-matching problem it was meant to avoid, just at a different vendor, for a benefit that direct verification below shows isn't actually needed. Verified directly against the real Jimaku API (not assumed) that plain title matching is sufficient: querying Jimaku's search with the exact title Crunchyroll exposes ("Witch Hat Atelier") returns exactly one match (Jimaku id 11793, `anilist_id: 147105`, own JP-romanized `name` "Tongari Boushi no Atelier"), matched via `english_name` — confirming Jimaku already indexes English titles as an alias, not just JP/romanized names, and that `fetchSubtitles`'s existing exact-`name`/`english_name`-match preference (background.js, built pre-Phase-4.5) already does the right thing once fed a real title. `seasonNumber` is now threaded into the Jimaku query as of the 2026-07-17 entry-selection fix below — used to pick the right Jimaku ENTRY for a multi-season show, a different problem from the still-unsolved per-FILE restart-numbering issue within a single entry (Naruto: Shippuuden's S07E01-style Jimaku file tagging, Decisions Log 2026-07-06). **`partOfSeason.name` is read and threaded alongside it as of 2026-07-27, and takes priority over the number** — Crunchyroll's `seasonNumber` turned out to be a positional index into its own season list (which counts OVA/special collections as seasons), not the anime's season, so it is wrong for any show with a non-season collection listed before the season being watched. `background.js`'s `resolveTextFiles` matches the season NAME against Jimaku's entry names first, then falls back to a season number parsed out of that name, then to Crunchyroll's number. Name matching tries three title forms (whole name; series title + season name; series-prefix-plus-season-suffix), because Crunchyroll and Jimaku don't agree on how much of the franchise title a season name repeats — this is the only tier that resolves ARC-named seasons at all (Demon Slayer, Attack on Titan's Final Season, Dr. STONE), none of which carry a season number on either side. Season-marker parsing itself was validated end to end against live Jimaku search results for 17 show/season combinations spanning all four naming conventions, all resolving via a genuine season match rather than falling through to the title fallback; the 2026-07-29 name-matching tiers were validated the same way across 12 further multi-season franchises. `courSiblingEntries` then covers seasons Jimaku splits across cours: when the chosen entry has no files for the requested episode, same-season siblings are retried, which is what makes Re:Zero S2 episodes 14–25 resolvable at all (its "2nd Season" entry holds none of them). Whether the `name` field exists at all is an open assumption — a missing one is logged loudly and degrades to the previous number-only behaviour. See Decisions Log and Open Questions. Returns `null` (not a guessed fallback) when no parseable `TVEpisode` block is found, routing to the existing "use manual upload" message instead of querying Jimaku with a wrong/empty title. **Validated end-to-end (2026-07-15, same day):** user reloaded the extension and the Witch Hat Atelier ep 1 page, subtitles appeared — confirms the full content-script flow (detection → `FETCH_SUBTITLES` → cues rendering) works on the live page, not just the standalone parse/API tests. **SPA-navigation gap found same day, fixed in two attempts.** Clicking to the next episode left the previous episode's subtitles showing (confirmed via live testing — Crunchyroll does NOT reload the page between episodes), since the original once-per-load detection never re-ran. **First attempt** wrapped `history.pushState`/`replaceState` to dispatch a custom `jp-immersion-locationchange` event on any real pathname change (plus a `popstate` listener) — shipped, then a second live test showed it still didn't work. **Root cause (confirmed, not guessed):** content scripts run in an isolated JS world with its own separate `history` object wrapping the shared browsing-context state — reassigning `history.pushState` from the isolated world has no effect on Crunchyroll's own (main-world) calls to its own unpatched reference. Navigation STATE is correctly shared across both worlds; only FUNCTION OVERRIDES fail to cross the boundary. **Second attempt (current):** polls `location.pathname` directly every 1s instead of trying to intercept whichever world changed it — sidesteps the isolation problem entirely since reading state works fine cross-world, only patching functions doesn't. `popstate` kept as a faster supplementary trigger for genuine back/forward navigation (a real browser-dispatched event, not a JS function call, so it legitimately crosses the world boundary) but polling is now the primary, reliable mechanism. The detect+fetch logic is still a reusable `loadSubtitles()` function shared between `init()` and the navigation listener — unchanged from the first attempt, only the trigger mechanism changed. Also fixed a related latent bug caught while touching this: `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY` was computed once at module load, so a saved offset would have silently leaked across episodes once SPA navigation was in play — now recomputed alongside the subtitle reload. **Re-validated live (2026-07-15, same day):** user confirmed episode-to-episode navigation now correctly refreshes subtitles. See Decisions Log.
- **"Best subtitle" selection algorithm, built (2026-07-15).** `background.js`'s `rankFiles()` (renamed 2026-07-15 from a single-winner `selectPreferredFile()` once the switcher panel below needed the full ranked list, not just the top pick) — sorts every candidate by a hardcoded uploader-preference list (`PREFERRED_UPLOADERS = ["SubsPlease", "Haruhana", "VCB-Studio"]`), matched by filename-substring bracket tag (e.g. `[Haruhana]`), preferred uploaders first in list order, everything else keeping Jimaku's own original relative order after. `rankFiles(files)[0]` is `fetchSubtitles`'s actual auto-pick. Real-signal ranking was ruled out first, not skipped: checked directly against real API responses (`/entries/search`, `/entries/{id}`, `/entries/{id}/files`) and confirmed no view/download/favorite count exists anywhere, file or entry level. List seeded from uploaders actually seen working well in this project's own live-testing (not outside general reputation) — user confirmed seeding from testing-so-far and refining later, rather than researching a fuller list now. `fileHint` still runs FIRST and can override the top rank — it solves a different axis (same-uploader language-track disambiguation, e.g. Witch Hat Atelier's Haruhana release having both a CHS+JPN dual-track file and a JPN-only one) that uploader ranking alone can't resolve, since both files share the same bracket tag. Doesn't attempt season-level disambiguation for shows with per-season restart-numbered Jimaku entries (Naruto: Shippuuden's S07E01 case) — a separate, harder problem needing its own filename-pattern design against real multi-season data, deliberately not solved in this pass. Unit-tested against the real 8-file candidate list Witch Hat Atelier ep 1 actually returns (confirmed correct behavior — prefers SubsPlease over an unmatched uploader, falls back to first file when nothing matches, correctly preserves relative order among non-preferred candidates). Auto-select-on-load is a natural consequence of this + the pre-existing always-automatic `FETCH_SUBTITLES` call (no separate work needed — there was never a manual step to begin with).
- **Manual switcher panel, built (2026-07-15).** `content.js`'s `buildSwitcherPanel()` (creates an empty `#jp-immersion-switcher` container, positioned/styled like the existing offset/upload controls) + `renderSwitcherOptions(panel, files, selectedUrl)` (populates a single labeled `<select>` from `FETCH_SUBTITLES`'s response — every ranked candidate as an option, the one actually playing pre-selected). Not Jimaku-embedded, not a separate `LIST_SUBTITLE_FILES` round trip — `background.js`'s `FETCH_SUBTITLES` response was extended to carry the full `files` list and `selectedUrl` alongside `cues`, since `fetchSubtitles` was already computing all of that internally; a second message would have doubled the Jimaku search+files-list API calls for data already in hand. Picking a different option sends a new `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` message (`url`/`name` of the chosen file) that downloads and parses just that one file via a `fetchAndParseFile()` helper shared with the auto-load path; a failed pick reverts the dropdown to the last known-good selection rather than leaving it showing a choice that silently didn't take. `renderSwitcherOptions` is called from `loadSubtitles()` (both initial page load and every SPA episode change via the fix above), and hides itself (`display: none`) rather than showing an empty box when there's nothing to list yet (no candidates, or the auto-fetch failed). Deliberately rough: a native `<select>`, no custom dropdown styling, matching the build-order scope's explicit "no visual polish (deferred to Phase 6)." Unit-tested (DOM-stub) against the real Witch Hat Atelier ep 1 file list — panel builds, populates with correct pre-selection, sends the right message on pick, updates `cues` on success, reverts on failure. **Confirmed working live by the user (2026-07-16).**
- **Per-show-per-season uploader preference memory + sticky fallback, built together (2026-07-16).** `content.js`'s `uploaderPrefKey(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)` derives a storage key from the SAME `detectShowEpisode()` fields already used elsewhere — deliberately independent of offset memory's own per-pathname key (a separate, unrelated mechanism this doesn't touch or migrate). `extractUploaderTag(filename)` pulls a release group's bracket tag from the start of a filename (e.g. `[Haruhana]`) — the only place uploader identity exists at all (no structured field in Jimaku's API, confirmed 2026-07-15); returns `null` for unbracketed direct-source files (Netflix/Amazon rips), and a pick landing on one of those simply doesn't persist a preference, since there's no reusable identity to save. The switcher panel's `change` handler saves the picked file's tag to `chrome.storage.local` on a successful switch. `loadSubtitles()` reads that saved preference (if any) before every `FETCH_SUBTITLES` call and passes it through as a new `preferredUploader` param, rather than fetching the default file first and separately swapping afterward — one Jimaku round trip either way, not two. `background.js`'s `rankFiles(files, preferredUploader)` gives the preference top priority by prepending it to the priority list (de-duped against the hardcoded default) rather than treating it as a special case elsewhere. This design gets the "sticky fallback" requirement for free, not as separate code: when no candidate file for a given episode actually matches the saved preference, every file's rank falls through to exactly where the plain default list would have put it, and nothing is ever written back to storage from the ranking function — a single episode's gap literally cannot corrupt the saved show+season preference, since the code path that could do that never executes. Unit-tested against 4 scenarios (preference overriding the default ranking; sticky fallback when the preferred uploader is absent this episode; unchanged behavior with no preference set; correct top-rank when the preference happens to coincide with an existing default-list entry) — **not yet exercised live in-browser.**
- **Jimaku per-season offset/uploader memory, moved here from Phase 6 (2026-07-05).** Cache the chosen Jimaku file/uploader + computed offset keyed per show and season; auto-apply on any new episode of an already-configured show+season, no re-prompt. Today's per-episode manual override (Phase 4) stays available for the rare episode needing a different offset, without overwriting the show-level default. Viewable/clearable by the user. **Sticky fallback (2026-07-05):** if the saved preferred uploader has no file for a given episode, silently fall through to the "best"-ranked option for that one episode only — the saved show+season preference is never cleared or overwritten by a single episode's gap.
- **Multi-season Jimaku entry resolution, reworked (2026-07-26).** Jimaku indexes each season as a separate entry with a free-text name and no structured season field, so the right entry has to be identified from the name. `background.js`'s `parseSeasonMarker()` recognises all four conventions in live use — `"2nd Season"`, `"Season 2"`, roman numerals (`"Overlord IV"`), and a bare trailing number (`"…Shukufuku wo! 3"`) — strips a trailing `"Part N"`/`"Cour N"` first as a cour marker rather than a season, and bounds the bare-number form to 2–20 so `"Mob Psycho 100"` and `"Jujutsu Kaisen 0"` aren't misread. `stripSeasonSuffix()`/`entrySeasonNumber()` are thin wrappers over it, and `resolveTextFiles()` still requires the season-stripped base title to equal what Crunchyroll reported before accepting a season match — the parse proposes, the title match decides. The resolved entry name is returned to `content.js` and shown in the switcher panel label, and a season fallback logs a console warning. Does NOT address the separate per-FILE restart-numbering problem within a single entry (Naruto: Shippuuden's `S07E01` tagging) — different axis, still open.
- **Non-episodic content — films, OVAs, specials, compilations (2026-07-31).** These have no `partOfSeason` block on Crunchyroll's page at all, so every season-matching tier above is inapplicable and the old code fell through to the plain title match, i.e. the franchise's season-1 entry, silently. Three additions. `searchQueryLadder()` re-runs a zero-result Jimaku search on progressively broader queries — the segment after the last colon, then trailing words dropped one at a time, capped at six rungs and only reached when the rung before it found nothing; measured against the live API, this recovers Mugen Train, Infinity Castle, The Last Attack and Re:Zero's OVAs, all of which returned zero under Crunchyroll's own spelling. `matchEntryByFullTitle()` then identifies the entry punctuation-insensitively (`looseTitle()` collapses everything non-alphanumeric, so `"Demon Slayer: Kimetsu no Yaiba - The Movie: Mugen Train"` and `"Demon Slayer -Kimetsu no Yaiba- The Movie: Mugen Train"` compare equal), trusts Jimaku's own index when a full-title search returns exactly one entry, and tests containment in ONE direction only — an entry name contained *in* Crunchyroll's title is the franchise, not the film. When nothing identifies the entry, `resolveTextFiles` returns `confident: false` plus the candidate list, and the switcher panel renders an entry picker (`FETCH_ENTRY_FILES`) rather than presenting a guess as a result. Entry resolution is logged on EVERY load now, not only on a detected mismatch. A file list empty for the requested episode also falls back to the entry's unfiltered file list, but only for non-episodic content, where Jimaku's per-file numbering routinely doesn't line up.

- **Which content counts as non-episodic (2026-08-01, revising the entry above).** The 2026-07-31 build asked that question as "did Crunchyroll publish a `partOfSeason` block?", which is wrong in both directions — Crunchyroll lists OVA/OAD collections *as* seasons, and a film has no block but was still run through the season match with a fabricated `wantedSeason` of 1. It is now asked of the season NAME. `nonEpisodicClass(name)` classifies it into one of five side formats (OVA/OAD/ONA as one class, film, special, picture drama, recap) and `isEpisodic` gates three things that previously keyed off the block's presence: the season-number match (suppressed for side formats, so an OAD can't take a numbered TV season's entry, and so a film's log line can't claim `matched by season 1`), the confidence calculation, and the unfiltered-file fallback (which now reaches OVA collections Crunchyroll publishes as seasons — Re:Zero's OVAs previously hard-errored on four unnumbered files). A new tier, `matchEntryByNonEpisodicClass()`, matches Crunchyroll's format against the format in a Jimaku entry's own name, requiring the entry to belong to the franchise and the match to be unique; this is what connects Crunchyroll's "OAD" to Jimaku's "Attack on Titan OVA", which neither the season name nor the season number can. Ambiguity degrades to the entry picker rather than guessing. The classifier was checked against 37 real season names for both over- and under-firing, including traps like "Mugen Train Arc" — a TV season named after a film, whose film entry sits beside it in the same search results.

- **The unresolved state — nothing matched, nothing loaded (2026-08-01).** When no tier matches, `resolveTextFiles` returns `{unresolved: true, candidates}` **before any file request**, and `fetchSubtitles` passes it back as a normal response rather than an error (an error message replaces the subtitle box and takes the switcher panel with it, and the entry picker is the whole point of this state). `content.js` renders the picker with a distinct banner — "no subtitles were loaded — pick one" rather than "check these are the right subtitles" — and `renderSwitcherOptions` no longer hides the panel when there are no files, provided there's a picker to show; otherwise the dead end would be permanent. Picking from the dropdown clears the unresolved wording. Playback is untouched throughout.

- **Which Crunchyroll field the season name comes from (confirmed against a live page, 2026-08-01).** `content.js`'s `detectShowEpisode()` reads `partOfSeason.name` and `partOfSeason.seasonNumber` out of the page's **schema.org JSON-LD `TVEpisode` block** — that is the only season source this extension reads. It does *not* read Crunchyroll's v2 CMS API (`/content/v2/cms/seasons/{id}/episodes`), whose equivalent fields are `season_title` and `season_number`. **The two surfaces were confirmed to agree**, on a real Attack on Titan OAD episode page: JSON-LD carries `"name": "Attack on Titan OADs"` and `"seasonNumber": 66`, the same values the CMS API reports. So the OAD fixtures reflect real runtime data, not a plausible guess. Worth keeping in mind that the format classifier depends on the name being present: with no season name an OAD has only its season number to go on, and Crunchyroll's numbers for side-format content are not sequential — 66 here. Nothing in resolution assumes a small or sequential season number (an unmatchable one simply fails to match, which is correct), but the classifier is the only thing that routes OADs correctly, so a missing `partOfSeason.name` would degrade them to an unidentified load. The existing "no `partOfSeason.name` on this page" warning already reports that case.

- **Entry-resolution test suite (`scripts/test-entry-resolution.js`, 2026-08-01).** Runs the real `resolveTextFiles` end to end: `background.js` is loaded whole into a `vm` sandbox with `fetch`, `console`, `chrome` and `importScripts` stubbed, so the query ladder, file lookup, cour-sibling retry, unfiltered fallback, archive filter and logging are all exercised — no browser, no network, no AnkiConnect. **82 cases as of 2026-08-13**, each asserting the selected entry, the exact console lines, and the UI state (`confident` / warning + candidates / unresolved / thrown error). The unresolved case additionally asserts that **no file request was made at all** — "no entry returned" would still pass if the function had already pulled season 1's files down. Logging is asserted unconditionally — resolving without emitting the entry line fails the case. All fixture data is real, captured from the live Jimaku API; the single constructed value (an empty `?episode=24` on entry 712, the only way to reach the cour retry) is labelled as such in the file. `test-season-resolution.js` remains alongside it for helper-level coverage.

- **Entry resolution — current state, and how to re-verify it (2026-08-13).** Eight defects were fixed across 2026-08-11/12, four of which silently served the wrong episode; the Decisions Log for those dates carries the reasoning. What is true now: the resolver identifies an entry through seven ordered tiers (season name, the content's own title, format class, a lone search hit, season number, exact title, exact entry name) and **refuses rather than guessing** when none match — a later season whose name says it is not season 1 will not fall back to the bare-franchise entry, and a film or stage play nothing names resolves to nothing. Within an entry it asks Jimaku for the episode, then falls through four retries in order: resolving locally from filenames when Jimaku's answer disagrees with itself, a cour sibling that is checked to cover the requested episode, an offset read from Jimaku's own index, and an offset derived from a second numbering population inside the entry. Non-episodic content skips episode numbers entirely and matches on the work's own title, then on its numbered part. **Verification, in the order it is worth running:** `scripts/test-entry-resolution.js` (offline, seconds); `scripts/audit-resolution.js <capture>` against live Jimaku in `--only` batches of ~150 episodes (~1.5s/episode, and larger batches exceed a 600s foreground limit); the six known-bug shows (`--only "tokyo ghoul,slimes,mushoku,shangri,dress-up,kimetsu no yaiba"`) after **any** resolver change, which is what caught a fix regressing a clean show on 2026-08-11; and the `--background` proof, where `fixtures/known-bugs-2026-08-02.json` against the resolver from commit `6ff83e8` must still report 9 proven defects and against current code 0. Never run the audit beside `test-render-pipeline.js` or `test-merged-audio-span.js --live` — it saturates Jimaku's rate limit and their failures then read as regressions. Last full pass 2026-08-13: 2,526 episodes, 0 COLLISION / 0 DUPLICATE / 0 EMPTY / 0 request failures.

- **What resolution deliberately cannot do (2026-08-13).** Four standing limitations, all of which end in the entry picker or manual upload rather than a wrong load. **No lexical bridge:** Crunchyroll's "Root A" and Jimaku's "√A" share no characters, and Detective Conan is indexed as "Case Closed" — nothing derives one from the other, so these decline (Detective Conan accounts for ~580 refusals in a full audit, correctly). **Generically named later seasons:** where Crunchyroll says "Season 2" and Jimaku names the season after an arc, nothing in Crunchyroll's page connects them, so the user picks once per season and it is remembered. **Content Jimaku does not hold:** Attack on Titan's Chronicle has no entry at all, and a lone search result there is a *different* film, so the picker says so and points at manual upload. **Two works sharing one entry with Japanese-only filenames:** Haikyu!!'s OVAs cannot be told apart from Crunchyroll's English titles, so a short list is offered instead of one file. Closing the last two would need Japanese↔English title mapping, which is its own scope.

- **The picked Jimaku entry is remembered per season (2026-08-13).** `content.js`'s `entryPrefKey(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)` stores the entry id chosen in the switcher, keyed exactly like the uploader preference beside it, and `loadSubtitles()` passes it as `preferredEntryId`. `background.js`'s `filesFromRememberedEntry()` then fetches that entry's files directly instead of resolving. It is only reachable after a manual pick, so it can never override a working automatic answer; any failure to use it (deleted entry, renamed, nothing for this episode) falls back to ordinary resolution rather than throwing; and the picker stays on screen while a remembered entry is in use, so a wrong pick is correctable.

- **UI for this phase ships rough/functional only, no visual polish (2026-07-05)** — polish is deferred to Phase 6, alongside the dictionary-popup UI/UX redesign already scheduled there.

_Manual subtitle file upload fallback, originally scoped as lower-priority Phase 4.5 work, was instead pulled forward and built in Phase 4 (2026-07-06) — see Phase 4 above._

### Phase 5: Anki export

- **Anki export architecture (decided 2026-07-02):** AnkiConnect, local HTTP server at `127.0.0.1:8765`, called via `fetch()` from the background service worker — same shape as the existing Jimaku calls, consistent with the no-backend approach. Requires Anki desktop + AnkiConnect running at capture time — needs a clear "Anki isn't open" state with retry, not a silent failure. **Capture UX:** "Add to Anki" button on the existing word-click popup, sends instantly, undo/edit-last-card available after (no pre-send edit step). **Core card fields:** target word, reading, gloss, full subtitle sentence with the target word marked. **Metadata toggles:** opt-in, off-by-default, shared by the popup and the card as one toggle set — POS label, JLPT level (source externally if jmdict-simplified doesn't carry the tag itself — decided 2026-07-04, toggle stays either way), and a frequency-rank badge (decided 2026-07-04: 3 text tiers — Common / Uncommon / Rare — no raw numbers shown, and no badge at all when TUBELEX has no data for the word, rather than defaulting an unranked word to "Rare"; replaces the earlier common-word flag/placeholder; built on the TUBELEX usage-frequency data already integrated for homograph ordering in Phase 4 above). Sentence translation and audio both shipped later in Phase 5 (2026-07-23 and 2026-07-22); the screenshot field was dropped from the roadmap entirely on 2026-07-17, confirmed DRM-blocked rather than deferred.
- **Frequency-rank badge, built end-to-end (2026-07-19).** `jmdict-compact.json` entries now persist an `fr` field ("common"/"uncommon"/"rare", omitted entirely when there's no signal) and a raw JMdict `id` (ent_seq, added for cross-referencing against data the compact format doesn't itself carry — also sets up the future JLPT phase's likely need for the same kind of lookup). Tier thresholds (score >= 500 → common, >= 20 → uncommon, >= 1 → rare) are absolute TUBELEX occurrence counts, chosen from real distribution data and boundary-word spot-checks, not picked blind — the "Strict" scheme was chosen specifically because its common/uncommon boundary words (悉く, ちょくちょく) read as more solidly common than looser schemes' borderline picks (カッと, ぎょっと); a percentile-based framing was considered and shelved (in practice nearly identical results to the absolute scheme, given how Zipfian the real distribution is) — could revisit if TUBELEX coverage changes later. **Orphaned-entry fallback:** confirmed 7,737 entries JMdict's own editors mark `common:true` have literally zero TUBELEX data (TUBELEX's tokenizer doesn't lemmatize multi-morpheme expressions like でしょう/かもしれない/おかね as single units at all, unrelated to real frequency — an initial "fragments under kuromoji" detection proxy was tested and rejected, since 61%+ of ALL content-word entries fragment under kuromoji regardless of TUBELEX coverage, no correlation). `scripts/build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` fills this gap two ways, corpus taking priority: (1) real occurrence counts from the same 3-episode batch-test corpus, relative-ranked within the orphaned set; (2) for the rest, the raw EDRDG `JMdict_e.xml`'s news/ichi/spec/gai/nf priority tags (NOT present in jmdict-simplified's JSON, confirmed 2026-07-01) via a scoped regex extraction — not a switch to XML as the primary source, just this one supplementary lookup. nf-priority data is newspaper-frequency-derived, the same formal-writing bias TUBELEX was chosen over BCCWJ specifically to avoid (confirmed via spot-check: nf01-ranked orphaned entries included 委員会/外務省/共産党/総選挙/自民党) — mitigated by a bounded political/institutional keyword check (kanji substrings like 党/省/議会/選挙 plus gloss keywords like "ministry"/"election") that demotes matching entries one tier. Output cached in `scripts/orphaned-tier-overrides.json` (checked in, ~180KB), consumed by `apply-tubelex-frequency.js`. **A real bug caught during testing:** the raw per-entry TUBELEX score (used for index ordering, unchanged) trusts any kanji-key match unconditionally by design, which let obscure alternate readings sharing a kanji with a common word (僕's archaic やつがれ/やつこ readings) inherit ぼく's huge score and nearly got tagged "Common" — existing index-ORDER logic was already immune (common flag beats score in that tiebreak) but the new tier LABEL wasn't. Fixed by moving tier assignment to run after the index re-sort and only trusting a kanji key's score for an entry if the re-sort actually ranked that entry first for that exact kanji spelling. **Full regen pipeline order:** `generate-jmdict-compact.js` → `fix-jmdict-priority.js` → `build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` → `apply-tubelex-frequency.js` (must run last among these four — see its own header). Popup badge (`content.js`) and an optional Anki "Frequency" field (`background.js`, same opt-in-toggle pattern as POS) both gated behind a new `metaShowFreq` toggle (`popup.html`/`popup.js`), replacing the old always-on common-word badge entirely, per the 2026-07-04 decision. Visual/color treatment for the three tiers is still unstyled (plain neutral badge, matching the old common-word badge's CSS) — deliberately deferred, see Section 7.
- **JLPT-level tag, built end-to-end (2026-07-22).** Data source: `stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab`'s `original_data/*.csv` (packaged in this repo as `scripts/jlpt-data/`, CC BY-SA 4.0, attribution not yet surfaced anywhere user-facing — tracked as a Phase 7 packaging item, see Section 8), itself a conversion of Jonathan Waller's JLPT Resources vocabulary list — the same underlying list Jisho.org uses for its own JLPT tags. Chosen over the elzup/jlpt-word-list lineage (same underlying Waller data, re-licensed MIT downstream, no JMdict-seq keys) specifically because every row already carries the exact JMdict `ent_seq`, matching `jmdict-compact.json`'s own `id` field — a direct ID join, not the fuzzy kanji/reading matching TUBELEX needed (and which caused a real cross-reading contamination bug there). `scripts/apply-jlpt-level.js` adds a `jlpt` field ("N5".."N1") to 7,909 of 218,859 entries; ~450 of the ~8,300 source rows share a jmdict_seq with a row on a DIFFERENT level list (confirmed via inspection, not a parsing bug: Waller's list distinguishes different SPELLINGS of the same JMdict entry by level, e.g. 会う/あう is N5 but the same entry's 遭う spelling is N2 — a distinction that can't survive a per-entry, not per-spelling, join), resolved by keeping the easier level. No ordering dependency on the TUBELEX/orphaned-override scripts — appended last in the regen pipeline by convention only. Popup badge, optional Anki "JLPT" field, and the `metaShowJlpt` opt-in toggle (graduated from its disabled "Coming soon" placeholder) all shipped, same pattern as frequency. Source data's own caveat carried forward deliberately: no official JLPT vocabulary list has existed since 2010, so this is a community "educated guess," not authoritative — matches the existing opt-in/off-by-default-only rule for this field.
- **Audio Anki field + "last looked-up word" chip, built 2026-07-22, first live-testing round done same day (two real bugs found and fixed — clip cut off mid-sentence on an early click, chip/popup vanishing on fullscreen toggle — see Decisions Log; fixes themselves not yet re-verified live).** Full design consolidated in the Decisions Log (2026-07-22 entries) after the user's own written analysis rejected a per-click rewind-and-recapture approach. **Capture mechanism** (`audio-capture.js`, a new content script loaded before `content.js`): a `ScriptProcessorNode` (chosen over `AudioWorkletNode` — functionally equivalent for this non-real-time use case, avoids a separate worklet module file + manifest `web_accessible_resources` entry + cross-thread chunked message passing) taps the SAME `createMediaElementSource` graph proven DRM-viable in the 2026-07-17 feasibility test, continuously filling a 45-second mono Float32 ring buffer; the tap is a parallel, silent-output path alongside the unchanged direct `source -> destination` link, so the graph is only ever added to, never disconnected (confirmed 2026-07-17: disconnecting silences playback until reload). Buffer position and subtitle-cue-boundary timestamps are both recorded on `audioCtx.currentTime`, not `video.currentTime` or wall-clock `performance.now()` — that clock keeps advancing in real time regardless of video pause/seek, avoiding any need to reconcile buffer position against a video timeline that can jump discontinuously between when a cue was seen and when the card is actually committed. A cue's exact `[audioStart, audioEnd]` (±200ms padding) comes from a `cueTimeline` maintained in lockstep with `content.js`'s own `lastText`, not from click time (click routinely happens after the line has already passed). **Three guards keep the recorded timings and the buffer describing the same audio (all added 2026-07-31, each from its own live-pass failure).** Each capture graph carries a generation number and writes only while it is current, so a graph superseded by a `<video>` swap goes quiet instead of interleaving its samples with the new one's — it still cannot be disconnected. The timeline carries an epoch, bumped on seek (`noteSeek`) and on SPA navigation (`resetCaptureForNavigation`, which also clears the buffer): a capture spanning either resolves to no audio rather than to whatever now occupies those positions, widening never reaches across one, and clips are capped at 20 seconds regardless. For a MERGED sentence the clip's edges are interpolated from the display window back to the sentence's own subtitle timings, because a display window spans every cue visible at that instant and is routinely wider than the sentence on a stacking provider. **The Anki send is two-phase (2026-07-31):** the note is written with every text field immediately and the audio attached by a follow-up `updateNoteFields` when the clip is ready, so nothing waits on a split sentence's second half — which in turn let the wait grow from 8s to 20s instead of shrinking. Export format is WAV (a direct fit for the ring buffer's raw-PCM storage, no extra encoding step) over webm/opus (smaller, but needs a second encoding pipeline bolted on for a benefit that doesn't matter much for local Anki storage). **Chip UI:** rather than a separately-built component, `chipifyPopup()` (`content.js`) repurposes the SAME popup DOM element/listeners the word-click popup already uses — the popup still auto-dismisses instantly on the next cue (unchanged), but if it had already finished loading real content, it's restyled/repositioned into a small chip (anchored to the `<video>` element's own live bounding rect, recomputed on resize/fullscreenchange — not a fixed viewport coordinate, the same class of bug as the 2026-07-17 stray-subtitle-box issue) instead of being discarded. Default state shows a minimal word+reading label; hovering reveals the exact same body content (definition, "+ Anki" button included) via a CSS-only show/hide toggle on a wrapping `.jp-immersion-popup-body` div — reading and committing stay the same interaction, not two. Single-slot (a new lookup immediately evicts any showing chip via `removeChip()`), 15-second lifetime after the popup would otherwise have closed. The sliced clip is computed fresh at "+ Anki" click time (not eagerly at popup-render time), since the captured cue's `audioEnd` may not be finalized yet when the popup first renders. **Anki wiring** (`background.js`): a new "Audio" field on the note type (no opt-in toggle, unlike POS/Frequency/JLPT — silently empty whenever content.js couldn't produce a clip), populated via AnkiConnect's `audio` note param (which stores the file and writes the `[sound:...]` reference itself, not written directly into `fields`), placed on the FRONT template so it plays as part of the recall prompt and still shows on the back via `{{FrontSide}}`. **Loudness normalization added 2026-07-26** after live testing found exported clip loudness tracking Crunchyroll's own volume slider at every level (not just at full mute): `normalizeLoudness()` scales each sliced clip to a fixed RMS target before WAV encoding, with a peak ceiling capping the gain below clipping and a max-gain limit so a near-silent capture isn't amplified into hiss. This is a correction on the captured samples, not a different tap point — `createMediaElementSource()` taps after the media element's own volume attenuation per the Web Audio spec, and `captureStream()` (the one tap that would bypass it) is already confirmed DRM-blocked. A clip below the silence floor exports no audio at all rather than silence or amplified noise. Unit-tested against synthetic signals: the same tone captured at player volumes 1.0, 0.5, 0.2, 0.05 and 0.01 exports at an identical output RMS, loud captures are turned *down* to the same target rather than only quiet ones boosted, and no input amplitude produces a clipping peak. See Decisions Log. **Explicitly out of this build:** a separate "settings panel" consolidating the existing subtitle-offset controls, manual upload, and Jimaku switcher panel — raised in the same design discussion but independent scope, not started. See Section 8.

- **Audio capture reworked against the second live pass (2026-08-15).** Five defects, one root cause between three of them. **The capture clock is now the ring buffer's own write count** (`captureNow()`, samples written ÷ sample rate), not `audioCtx.currentTime`, and the processor does not write while the `<video>` is paused or seeking. A paused media element still feeds its graph, so the old clock kept advancing while the buffer filled with silence — a cue left open across a pause therefore measured the pause as part of the line, which is the reported "44.29 seconds of nothing, then audio at the very end". On the new clock a pause takes zero space: the audio either side of it is adjacent, exactly as the buffer stores it. The same change makes the buffer hold 45 seconds of real audio rather than 45 seconds of a pause. **Padding is asymmetric** — 0.2s lead-in, 0.6s tail — because subtitle END timestamps are routinely authored a little before the speech finishes, which is the "the very end of the sentence is usually omitted" report; and the slice now WAITS (bounded, 2s) for that tail to have actually been recorded before cutting, since a cue's end is noticed by the next `timeupdate` and the buffer holds almost nothing past it at that instant. Deliberately not `outputLatency` compensation: that shifts both edges together, and its sign can't be verified from here. **A capture interrupted by a seek or an episode change is now flushed rather than voided** (`flushPendingCaptures`, called from `noteSeek` before the epoch bump): at the moment of the jump the buffer still holds the line the user clicked, so it is sliced then, giving a short but correct clip instead of the empty audio field the live pass reported. A flushed clip is capped at the line's own subtitle window + 0.25s, because SPA navigation is noticed by a 1s poll and the next episode's audio can already have started arriving. **Discontinuities are recorded** (`noteDiscontinuity`, one per seek/navigation/`<video>` swap) and the edit buffer's padding is clamped to the nearest one either side, so pulling the trim handles outward after skipping around can no longer play two unrelated moments spliced together.

- **The retained edit buffer survives an episode change (2026-08-15).** It is an independent copy of PCM, so navigation only invalidates the ability to RE-CUT it, not the samples themselves. `sealRetainedClip()` tops it up one last time and drops its bounds; `clearRetainedClip()` is now only for a note that has been deleted. This is the fix for the editor saying "Audio for this card is no longer available to edit" the moment the user moved on, for a clip sitting complete in memory. A re-cut is also now only ever accepted when it is MORE complete than what is held (`isMoreComplete`) — re-cutting a clip whose start had aged out of the 45-second window would otherwise return a shorter buffer with its markers shifted to fit, i.e. the same card's audio apparently starting somewhere else. Ownership is tracked by a token issued at click time (`retainedClipToken`), not by "the buffer belongs to the newest note": the buffer is only cut when the capture RESOLVES, so for as long as one was in flight the newest note pointed at the PREVIOUS card's audio, which is what the live pass reported as the preview playing the previously-captured clip.

- **Offset memory is per provider as well as per episode (2026-08-15).** Timing drift is a property of the release, not of the episode, so an adjustment writes two keys: `offset:<pathname>` and `offsetProvider:<series>:<season>:<releaseTag>`. Reads prefer the episode's own value and fall back to the provider's, so a one-off correction stays a one-off while a systematic one carries forward by itself. The release tag comes from `extractUploaderTag` — the same bracket tag the uploader preference uses — so a direct-source rip with no tag simply gets no provider key and behaves exactly as before. `offsetTouchedThisEpisode` guards the fallback against overwriting a correction the user made by hand before the file list arrived.

- **English caption pipeline (`caption-url-sniffer.js`, built 2026-07-23, hardened through 2026-07-27).** Crunchyroll exposes no reachable caption text in the DOM — six rounds of live fact-finding ruled that out (Decisions Log 2026-07-23) — so the English track is taken from the signed `.ass` URL inside Crunchyroll's own `play` API response. A MAIN-world content script at `document_start` overrides `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send` and recognises that response by its JSON SHAPE (a `subtitles` or `captions` object with locale keys each carrying a `url`), never by request path, so Crunchyroll renaming the endpoint doesn't break it. Locale matching prefers `en-US`, then any `en*`. The URL crosses into the isolated world by `postMessage`; `background.js` performs the actual fetch (content scripts can't fetch cross-origin) behind a `*.crunchyrollcdn.com` hostname allowlist, since any script on the page could forge that message. The real signed host was confirmed live as `vod-fy-mod.crunchyrollcdn.com`, which the pre-existing allowlist already covered.
  Two rules keep the cues alive, both from real failures. **The sniffer caches the last URL found and replays it on a handshake** from `content.js`, because it runs at `document_start` and `content.js` at `document_idle`, so a `play` response resolving in that window was otherwise lost outright with no retry. **Cues are cleared ONLY on episode change** (`resetEnglishCaptions`), never from `loadSubtitles` — that function also runs on initial load and on every Jimaku retry, and clearing there destroyed a correctly-loaded 317-cue set before it could ever render. Each replayed URL is stamped with the pathname it was captured on (2026-07-27) so a replay of the current episode's file is accepted while a replay of the previous episode's is discarded.
  Diagnostics are unconditional rather than debug-gated, because this feature once survived a full live-testing round looking correct while being entirely non-functional (the English on screen was coming from a dual-language Jimaku file). The sniffer logs locale, format and host on a hit and warns with the available locale keys when the shape matches but no English entry exists; `background.js` names a rejected hostname; `content.js` logs the loaded cue count; and `window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` reports whether the fetch/XHR overrides are seeing page traffic at all — distinguishing "never saw the response" (e.g. a request made from a Worker, whose separate global scope these overrides never touch) from "saw it, didn't recognise it".

- **Dual-language subtitle stripping (2026-07-26).** `subtitle-parser.js`'s `parseAss()` now retains each Dialogue line's `Style`, and `stripDualLanguageCues()` classifies every style by the majority script of its own lines (kana is the discriminator — kanji is shared with Chinese) and drops the non-Japanese ones wholesale. `.srt` has no styles, so it falls back to a conservative per-line rule gated on a high proportion of Latin-only lines. Applied in `background.js`'s `fetchAndParseFile()` only, never to `fetchEnglishSubtitles()`, so the on-screen Japanese box, the Anki `Sentence` field and the audio-capture cue boundaries are all cleaned from one place. Fails open: a file with no majority-Japanese style is returned untouched. **Validated against 27 real Jimaku files across three shows, with zero Japanese lines lost in any of them:** the reported `[SubsPlease] … _ja-en.ass` drops from 317 English cues to 0 while keeping all 317 Japanese; the Chinese dual-language releases (Nekomoe kissaten, Haruhana) lose their Chinese track and keep credits intact; and Japanese-only files carrying a few incidental Latin lines (`erai-raws`, the Netflix `ja[cc]` rip) are correctly left untouched.

- **"Edit last card" — in-page edit panel (2026-07-30).**
  *Trigger surfaces:* the popup success-row "Edit" button next to Undo, shown immediately after capture; and the persistent "Edit last card (word)" control in the top-left tool column, which appears after the first capture in a session, survives episode changes, clears on full page reload, and tracks the note's `nid` in memory only — never persisted to disk.
  *Chip, two-state discovery surface:* `showCaptureChip()` builds it once export completes. **State 1 (expanded)** runs for `chipExpandedMs()`, which scales with captured content length, and shows the JP sentence with the captured word's span highlighted (by recorded OFFSET, not text search — the same word can occur twice), reading, gloss, EN translation, and inline Edit/Undo. **State 2 (collapsed)** shrinks to the word label for the remainder of the chip's existing ~15s lifetime, with hover re-expanding to the same content; hover expansion is CSS-only. Undo works in both states — the note is fully written by the time the chip exists. `mountChip()` gives it the same single-slot lifecycle, video-anchored positioning and fullscreen re-parenting the lookup chip already had.
  *Edit panel:* `openEditPanel(nid)` calls `notesInfo` fresh on every open and never trusts values cached from capture, since the note may have been hand-edited in Anki since. Sentence (JP) and Translation (EN) are freeform textareas. Word/Reading/Gloss are not directly editable — a summary row plus "Change word", which re-renders the stored sentence through `buildGroupsForText` (the same pipeline the subtitle line uses) with the current token marked, and a pick cascades Word/Reading/Gloss/POS/Frequency/JLPT together as a pending preview. POS/Frequency/JLPT/Source are read-only and move only as a side effect of that pick. Audio is a full trim editor over the retained PCM buffer (rebuilt 2026-08-15, see below); adjustments are staged, not written until Save. Tags, note type and deletion are absent by design and routed to "Open in Anki".
  *Save/Cancel/errors:* Save is disabled until something is dirty, then fires ONE `updateNoteFields` carrying every changed field plus a media write if the audio moved. Replacing audio blanks `Audio` in the same call, or AnkiConnect appends a second `[sound:…]` and the card plays both clips; the superseded media file is deleted only AFTER a successful update, and a failure to delete it never fails the edit. Cancel writes nothing. A failed save discards no pending edits and stays retryable. A note deleted since capture is an explicit, distinct, non-retryable message rather than a silent no-op. An unavailable audio buffer degrades the audio section alone, leaving the rest of the panel usable.
  *Audio buffer:* `retainClipForEditing()` (audio-capture.js) keeps raw, un-normalized PCM over the same bounds that were exported plus `AUDIO_EDIT_PAD_SECONDS` on each side, so a clip can be extended as well as tightened. It is retained twice — immediately and again after the padding window — because no post-roll exists at the moment a clip is finalized. `audioBufferNoteId` (content.js) records which note the buffer belongs to, so editing any older note degrades instead of silently trimming a different clip, and `forgetAddedNote()` releases the buffer when its note is deleted. **Reworked 2026-08-15** — see the Phase 5 architecture entries above for the capture clock, the seal-on-navigation lifetime, the discontinuity clamp and the capture token that now governs all of this.

  *Audio trim editor (rebuilt 2026-08-15):* the model is an NLE's, not a DAW's — scrub a playhead over the waveform, listen, then set the in or out point AT the playhead. It carries a playhead (click or drag anywhere on the waveform to move it, and playback follows a scrub made while playing), a transport that behaves (play/pause/resume, loop the selection, and playback muting the episode underneath rather than pausing it), a ruler labelled relative to the CAPTURED clip's start so the pre-roll reads as negative, dimming outside the selection, dashed markers where the clip sat as captured, and keyboard control (space, I/O, L, arrows to nudge with Shift for coarse, Home/End). `Snap to speech` walks outward from the loudest bucket inside the current selection to the first sustained quiet stretch on each side — outward, so it extends a clip that starts late as readily as it trims one that starts early. The whole padded buffer is encoded once and played through one `<audio>` element rather than re-encoding per play: that is what makes the playhead possible, since the element's own `currentTime` IS a position in the buffer. It also **watches its own padding**: the trailing pad only exists once the episode has played it, so a panel opened two seconds after a capture has two seconds of post-roll, and the editor now re-reads and rebuilds in place (carrying the in-progress edit across, never mid-playback) until the pad is full or the buffer is sealed. That is the fix for the right-hand pad "always" being short while the left was always exactly 3s — the left is complete immediately by definition, and the editor only ever read the buffer once. While a capture is still in flight the section says so and waits on it, instead of declaring the audio unavailable or showing the previous card's clip.
  *Escape hatch:* `editAnkiNote` is unchanged (`guiEditNote`, falling back to `guiBrowse` with a `nid:` query only on an unimplemented-action error) but is now reachable solely from the panel's "Open in Anki" control.

- **Subtitle display filter chain (`cueDisplayText`, current as of 2026-07-29).** One function defines what a raw cue looks like on screen, called both per-cue per-tick by `updateJapaneseCue` and by the Anki sentence merge, so the two cannot disagree. Order: fansub markup is stripped FIRST, then the stage-direction test (which only matches a parenthetical that is the whole line, so any surviving prefix would defeat it), then speaker prefixes, inline furigana, and half-width→full-width katakana normalization. The markup rule is defined by CLASS, not enumeration: `\p{Extended_Pictographic}` plus the contiguous U+2600–27BF span (Miscellaneous Symbols + Dingbats, the two Unicode blocks dedicated to decorative symbols — this is what catches all four music notes, both continuation arrows and the off-screen-speech marker), plus a deliberately curated bracket list (`《》⸨⸩`). That last one is forced rather than lazy: Unicode classifies those identically to `「」『』（）`, so no property can separate a monologue marker from a real quote. Exempt by design: `‼`/`⁉` (punctuation, not decoration, despite Unicode calling them pictographic), geometric shapes (Japanese censors a character with `○`), and unit signs (`°℃№`). Measured over 49,592 real corpus cues from 48 files.

- **English/Japanese cue pairing and split-sentence Anki merge (2026-07-26).** `content.js`'s `pairEnglishCues(window)` is the single source of truth for which English cues translate a given Japanese cue window, shared by the on-screen English line (`updateEnglishCue`) and the Anki merge so the two cannot disagree. `updateEnglishCue` is called unconditionally from `handleTimeUpdate` rather than living inside the Japanese half: sitting there, behind that half's `if (text === lastText) return`, it was unreachable on any tick where the Japanese line hadn't just changed, so an English cue starting or ending mid-Japanese-line appeared late, lingered, or never rendered. Simulated at the browser's real ~4Hz tick rate over deliberately misaligned boundaries, the old arrangement showed the wrong English on 14 of 29 ticks and the current one on 0 of 29. It uses midpoint containment in both directions — no tuned threshold. **`dropBackgroundEnglishCues` (2026-08-15)** then removes a top-positioned cue when a bottom-positioned one competes with it for the same moment: Crunchyroll moves the secondary of two simultaneous lines to the top of the frame with an `\an8` override, and `parseAss` now records that alignment (`assAlignment`), which is the only thing in the file that tells an unrelated background line from the translation of the line being spoken. Relative, never absolute — a lone top-positioned cue is dialogue placed away from a speaker at the bottom of frame and survives untouched. Applied inside `pairEnglishCues`, so the on-screen line and the Anki `Translation` field make the same decision by construction; it also un-blocks the sentence merge, which refuses to merge when a window pairs with several English cues and a stray background line was enough to count as several. `buildMergedAnkiSentence()` uses it at click time to rejoin a sentence split across consecutive Japanese cues **for the card only**, splicing the displayed line into the merged string verbatim so the clicked word's recorded character offset stays valid (shifted by `offsetShift`). `audio-capture.js`'s `sliceClipWavWhenReady()` takes optional `mergeStart`/`mergeEnd` — subtitle-file timestamps spanning the merged group — to widen the clip to the same span, waiting bounded for a second cue still playing and keeping the widened start if it never finishes. It matches those against each timeline entry's own recorded cue window (`markCueBoundary` stores `jpStart`/`jpEnd` alongside the audio timestamps) rather than by index, since the timeline also records empty entries for the gaps between lines. **Matched by display TEXT until 2026-07-27**, which live testing found broken — see the Decisions Log entry for why text is not a usable key here. `updateEnglishCue` pairs against `renderedJpWindow` (the window whose text is actually painted) rather than `activeJpWindow` (whatever the clock says should be showing), so the English line cannot appear ahead of the Japanese one it belongs to on a cold start; `renderCue` takes a completion callback to drive that. `cueDisplayText()` is the single definition of the display filter chain, called both per-cue per-tick by `updateJapaneseCue` and by the merge — fansub markup is stripped *before* the stage-direction test, since that test only matches a parenthetical that is the whole line. Two filters were added 2026-08-15: `ASS_OVERRIDE_RE` (a safety net for `{\anN}`-style tags, whose primary strip now also happens in `parseSrt` — Jimaku is full of .srt files converted from .ass with the tags left in), and `SENTENCE_PERIOD_RE`, which replaces 。 with a space and collapses the result, per the user's request; only 。, never 、 or ？／！. `japaneseDisplayAt(fileTime)` (2026-07-29) answers "which Japanese cues are on screen at this moment, and what do they render to" for ANY moment, not just now, which is what lets `bridgedEnglishText()` inspect the cues either side of a gap: the English line is held across a gap shorter than `JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` when — and only when — both flanking cues pair to the same English text, so a split sentence's English no longer blinks as the Japanese line changes beneath it. Display-only; the Anki merge is unaffected.

- **Subtitle display layout — one flex stack, not two viewport anchors (2026-07-26).** Both subtitle boxes are children of a single bottom-anchored `#jp-immersion-subtitle-stack` (`content.css`), created in `content.js`'s `init()`: Japanese (`#jp-immersion-subtitle`, clickable) first, English (`#jp-immersion-subtitle-en`, plain text) second. Neither box carries its own `position: fixed` anchor any more — that earlier arrangement is what let a multi-line English caption grow up into the Japanese box and paint over it. The stack is what gets re-parented on `fullscreenchange` and hidden on SPA navigation away from a watch page (previously only the Japanese box was hidden, leaving the English one stranded on non-watch pages). The two boxes remain independently driven: Japanese cues match offset-adjusted `video.currentTime`, English cues match raw `video.currentTime`, in `updateJapaneseCue()` and `updateEnglishCue()` respectively — both called unconditionally from `handleTimeUpdate` on every tick, which is what keeps them in sync. See Decisions Log.

### Phase 6–7: Differentiation, polish & launch prep

- **Mono-ruby (per-kanji distributed furigana), designed but deliberately deferred (identified 2026-07-03).** Requested during Phase 4 live testing for readability — the current furigana centering fix (Phase 4 above) only handles group/jukugo-ruby (whole reading centered over the whole compound), not per-kanji distribution (e.g. おう over 王 and と over 都 within 王都). Needs a new data source (KANJIDIC, JMdict's sister project, for per-kanji on'yomi/kun'yomi) plus an alignment algorithm to match a compound's reading against its characters' possible readings, and won't work at all for jukujikun words (大人=おとな) where the reading doesn't decompose per-kanji. A naive even-split heuristic was rejected: it would produce wrong splits most of the time (王都: おう is 2 mora, と is 1 — not a 50/50 split), actively misleading a learner rather than helping. Not built as a quick fix for this reason.
- **Jimaku auto-offset-detection, decided but not yet built (2026-07-05).** Align subtitle offset automatically against Crunchyroll's own native subtitle track timing, replacing the first-watch manual nudge (today's per-episode manual offset tool, Phase 4, stays available as an override). Closes the last open Jimaku-import UX gap vs. ManabiDojo (Section 3.3).

(Jimaku per-season offset/uploader memory and the Jimaku search-by-title-mismatch fix moved to Phase 4.5, 2026-07-05 — see above.)

No technical architecture locked in yet for furigana-only mode, appearance controls, auto-pause, onboarding, or packaging — this section will populate as design/build work begins on each. (English subtitle display moved to Phase 5, 2026-07-23 — see Section 6.)

---

## 6. Build Order

Each phase lists what's **done** and what **remains**, remaining items in priority order. Anything gated on another task or deprioritized says so explicitly.

**Phase 0 — Finalize spec** ✅ Complete (this doc).

**Phase 1 — Environment & extension skeleton** ✅ Complete. Basic Manifest V3 skeleton injecting into the Crunchyroll page, verified on a real watch page.

**Phase 2 — Subtitle pipeline proof of concept** ✅ Complete. Query Jimaku for one show, fetch a subtitle file, parse it, display raw Japanese text synced to video playback time (no clicking/dictionary). Verified end-to-end against Witch Hat Atelier ep. 1.

**Phase 3 — Word parsing & dictionary lookup** ✅ Complete.
- Segment parsed subtitle text into clickable words; click/hover → JMdict popup. Verified against Witch Hat Atelier ep 1.
- Merged auxiliary-verb tokens (た/ない/etc.) into the preceding word, fixing wrong lookups on inflected words (生まれた was splitting into 生まれ + unrelated noun 田) — the origin of what's now Rule 3 (see Section 5, Phase 4, Grouping/merge pipeline).
- Popup enrichment: furigana (ruby markup), inflection line, POS labels, common-word badge — each substantially reworked in Phase 4 (see Section 5, Phase 4, Popup content & display).

**Phase 4 — Sync tooling & multi-show validation** ✅ Complete (closed 2026-07-15, user's explicit call — see below)

*Done:*
- Offset adjustment UI + hotkeys: ±0.1s buttons, reset, Alt+←/→/0, offset persisted per-episode URL in `chrome.storage.local`
- Batch-testing script (`scripts/batch-test.js`), now reporting 7 corpus-validation patterns
- Multi-show manual testing: Bocchi the Rock! ep 1 (8 bugs found & fixed), Frieren (confirmed working), One Piece ep 894 (no forced-subtitle layer)
- Jimaku entry-selection bug fixed (exact-match preference over `entries[0]`) — see Decisions Log 2026-07-01
- Full grouping-pipeline rebuild (katakana-name inertness, half-width-katakana resolution, speaker-prefix/inline-furigana stripping, Rule 3 rewrite, Rule 0.6, phrase-matcher restructure, dual-view mechanism) — see Section 5 and Decisions Log
- First live-browser pass (Frieren ep 1) fixed でしょう/だろう resolution, 王都-style compound ordering, and 凱旋+です mislabeling — see Section 5 (Grouping/merge pipeline; Popup content & display) and Decisions Log
- こと-style JMdict sense-ordering resolved via TUBELEX — see Section 5 (Homograph/sense ordering via TUBELEX)
- Popup content-decisions spec implemented (POS chip labels, inflection-line rework) — see Section 5 (Popup content & display)
- Follow-up fixes: bare だろ/でしょ resolution, れる/られる/せる/させる/ば merging (pre-existing gap, not a regression), ば conditional absorption added as new scope — see Section 5 (Grouping/merge pipeline) and Decisions Log
- Full corpus validation run (all 3 shows) — caught and fixed a でしょう/だろう regression missed by hand-picked tests (Pattern 2 lookup misses 43→38) — see Decisions Log 2026-07-03
- Further live-testing round: furigana centering, Kuru/Suru chip suppression, TUBELEX cross-contamination fix, full inflection-label descriptor pass — see Section 5; kanji-heteronym disambiguation, この先 dual-view primacy, and duplicate-gloss homographs investigated and logged rather than fixed at the time — see Section 8 and Decisions Log
- **Live re-test started (2026-07-05), Bocchi the Rock! ep 1 pass complete.** Offset-reset hotkey fixed (⌥+0 produced "º" on Mac, not "0" — see Section 5). Full QA pass surfaced 26 items, evaluated and mostly fixed same session — see Section 5 (Grouping/merge pipeline; Popup content & display; dictionary-data precision) and Decisions Log for the complete breakdown. Frieren and One Piece passes still remaining (see below).
- **`jmdict-compact.json` regenerated from scratch** (superseding incremental patching), **kanji-proper-noun un-suppression, multi-reading display-precision fix, and duplicate-gloss archaic-tag/demotion fix** all built and validated same session (2026-07-05, later pass) — see Section 5 (Dictionary-data precision; Grouping/merge pipeline) and Decisions Log. 子's ね/こ ordering now correct; お's interjection ranking accepted as a permanent limitation instead (see Decisions Log and Section 8 Resolved).
- **Numerals/numbers made clickable, 後藤-style multi-kanji name-run fusion, and fansub-provider markup filter all built and corpus-validated (2026-07-05, later pass)**; colloquial-contraction handling (どした-class cases) investigated and accepted as a permanent limitation, no reliable general mechanism found — see Section 5 (Grouping/merge pipeline) and Decisions Log for all four. `scripts/batch-test.js` now reports 11 corpus-validation patterns.
- **Frieren live-testing round started (2026-07-06).** じゃない's inflection note and the archaic/dated-tag-vs-frequency-badge interaction both confirmed as already-correct existing behavior, no fix needed; karaoke-style word highlighting considered and declined (same shape of rejection as the mono-ruby naive-heuristic call) — see Decisions Log for all three.
- **Kanji spellings on homograph cards, numbered gloss display + gloss-cap bump, それもそうか-style dual-view (confirmed no new code needed), and manual subtitle upload fallback all built/resolved (2026-07-06)** — required regenerating `jmdict-compact.json` (new `k` field, `g` restructured by sense), validated 100% match on all non-gloss fields across 462,753 index keys; `scripts/batch-test.js`'s 11 patterns unchanged — see Section 5 (Dictionary-data precision; Popup content & display) and Decisions Log for all four
- **Second Frieren live-testing round (2026-07-06)** — numbered-gloss structure, kanji-spelling placement, and だろ's inflection note all confirmed working as designed; two real bugs found and fixed (kanji-spelling rK/sK/oK/iK filtering, そうか showing unrelated nouns via a new `__phraseFuse` POS sentinel); verb-type-implied archaism also built — all corpus-validated with zero regressions — see Section 5 (Popup content & display; Dictionary-data precision) and Decisions Log for all six findings
- **Live-testing show swapped from Naruto: Shippuuden to Witch Hat Atelier (2026-07-12, see Decisions Log).** First Witch Hat Atelier live-testing round (2026-07-12): six confirmed bugs fixed (よ/なあ sense-selection and per-reading-kana-restriction gaps, の mis-tagged as a noun after ない, 外に false-fusing to an idiom, て resolving through quotative って's entry, られない's missing inflection label) and two fact-finding items resolved as correct-as-is (ただ's kanji spellings, 編み's absence confirmed a genuine JMdict gap) — all verified directly against a downloaded raw JMdict release, not assumption. See Section 5 (Dictionary-data precision; Grouping/merge pipeline; Popup content & display) and Decisions Log.
- **s_inf (sense-info) annotation display built (2026-07-15)** — outside the live-testing rotation, from a standalone report on よ's popup. Built as a universal field (any POS), not scoped to prt/int as originally proposed, after sampling the raw release showed content-word notes are often substantive (grammar constraints, kanji-nuance disambiguation), not just redundant register info. Required a full 3-script regen pipeline run. See Section 5 (Popup content & display) and Decisions Log.
- **Second Witch Hat Atelier live-testing round (2026-07-13).** A real regression caught before shipping: `apply-tubelex-frequency.js` hadn't been re-run after the previous round's `jmdict-compact.json` regen, reverting こと/僕/くる ordering — fixed and now documented as a required regen-pipeline step (see Dictionary-data precision above). Three further confirmed bugs fixed (trailing emphatic っ independently clickable, honorific suffixes さん/さま/くん/ちゃん resolving to unrelated nouns, Rule 0 honorific-prefix missing an inflection note) plus the previously-open 3-sense-cap question resolved with a data-driven fix (raised to 8 for particle/interjection-primary entries only, verified against a full raw-release scan first). Four items confirmed correct as-is with no changes (よ's single JMdict headword, お in lexicalized words, て staying separate in te-form, 他にも chunking). See Section 5 and Decisions Log.
- **Live re-test formally closed (2026-07-15), user's explicit call.** Three shows, several rounds each, deep coverage of grouping/dictionary/popup bugs (see all entries above). Not exhaustive — the user acknowledged more live-testing may surface issues later — but sufficient to move forward and build the rest of the extension rather than keep testing indefinitely. Any future live-testing bugs get logged and fixed as they come up, same as any other phase, rather than gating further build work on a dedicated testing pass.

*Remaining:* none — accepted residual edge cases and conditional items below are not scheduled work.
- Accepted residual edge cases, not scheduled work: があん (rare interjection collision), たの-vs-のか tie-break, occasional harmless dual-view "noise"; 入る (いる/はいる ordering), まま (まんま/にゅうぼ ordering), お (interjection-sense ranking), and colloquial-contraction handling (どした-class cases) — all confirmed permanent limitations, no frequency data or reliable general mechanism exists for any of these — see Decisions Log
- Conditional, not scheduled: lindera-wasm/Vibrato-wasm benchmark, only if real testing shows a kuromoji quality gap; forced-subtitle overlay test, only if a confirmed-forced-subtitle Crunchyroll title turns up — none has so far, don't actively hunt for one

**Phase 4.5 — Jimaku integration** (new phase, created 2026-07-05 — see Decisions Log; absorbs two items previously scheduled in Phase 6, pushing Phase 5 later in the overall order)

*Done:*
- Show/episode auto-detection built and validated end-to-end (2026-07-15): `content.js`'s `detectShowEpisode()` reads Crunchyroll's own schema.org JSON-LD for series title/season/episode number, replacing the old hardcoded `SHOW_QUERY`/`EPISODE` constants. Real fact-finding (a live DevTools dump, since the page itself resists direct fetching) found Crunchyroll exposes no TMDB/AniList/MAL ID at all, superseding the original "resolve via ID" plan from 2026-07-04 — verified plain title matching against the real Jimaku API is sufficient instead. Confirmed live: reloaded extension + page, subtitles appeared. See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Decisions Log.
- Jimaku popularity-signal fact-finding check done (2026-07-15): confirmed via real API responses (not just docs) that no per-file or per-entry popularity/quality signal exists at all. Ranking algorithm below now has its answer — hardcoded uploader-preference-list fallback is the only path, not a conditional choice. See Decisions Log.
- "Best subtitle" selection algorithm built (2026-07-15): `background.js`'s `rankFiles()` (renamed from an earlier single-winner `selectPreferredFile()` on 2026-07-15 when the switcher panel needed the full ranked list, not just the top pick), hardcoded uploader-preference list matched by filename-substring, seeded from testing so far (SubsPlease/Haruhana/VCB-Studio) per the user's explicit choice to seed-now-refine-later rather than research a fuller list first. Unit-tested against the real Witch Hat Atelier ep 1 candidate list. Auto-select-on-load falls out of this for free (there was never a manual step). See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Decisions Log.
- SPA episode-navigation fix, two attempts (2026-07-15): first attempt (`history.pushState`/`replaceState` wrapper) shipped, then failed a second live test — root-caused to content scripts running in an isolated JS world, where reassigning `history.pushState` has no effect on Crunchyroll's own main-world calls to its own unpatched reference. Second attempt polls `location.pathname` directly every 1s instead (state reads correctly cross-world even though function overrides don't), with `popstate` kept as a faster supplementary trigger for real back/forward navigation. Also fixed a related latent bug: the offset storage key was computed once at module load and would have leaked across episodes once SPA navigation was in play. Re-validated live — user confirmed episode navigation now correctly refreshes subtitles. See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Decisions Log.
- Manual switcher panel built (2026-07-15): `content.js`'s `buildSwitcherPanel()`/`renderSwitcherOptions()`, a single labeled `<select>` listing every candidate file for the current show+episode, ranked per `rankFiles()`, pre-selected to whichever one is actually playing. `background.js`'s `FETCH_SUBTITLES` response was extended to include the full ranked `files` list and `selectedUrl` alongside `cues` (rather than adding a separate `LIST_SUBTITLE_FILES` message), avoiding a redundant second Jimaku round trip for data already computed internally. A new `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` message downloads/parses a user-picked file on demand; a failed pick reverts the dropdown rather than leaving it showing a selection that silently didn't take. Refreshed on every `loadSubtitles()` call (initial load and SPA episode change alike), so it always reflects the current episode's real candidates. Rough/functional only per the build-order scope — no visual polish, a native `<select>` not a custom dropdown. Unit-tested (DOM-stub) against the real Witch Hat Atelier ep 1 file list; **confirmed working live by the user (2026-07-16).** See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Decisions Log.
- Per-show-per-season uploader preference memory + sticky fallback, both built together (2026-07-16): a manual switcher-panel pick now saves its uploader tag (extracted from the filename's bracket prefix, e.g. `[Haruhana]`) keyed on `(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)`, and `loadSubtitles()` reads that saved preference before every fetch, threading it into `FETCH_SUBTITLES` as a new `preferredUploader` param. `background.js`'s `rankFiles()` gives a preference top priority in the SAME ranking pass rather than a two-step "auto-load then swap" flow — one Jimaku round trip either way. The sticky-fallback requirement falls out of this for free: a preference with no matching file in a given episode's candidate list just falls through to wherever it'd rank under the plain default list, with nothing written back to storage, so a single episode's gap can't corrupt the saved preference. A pick landing on an unbracketed direct-source file (Netflix/Amazon rip, no extractable uploader identity) doesn't persist anything — applies for that one episode only, same as before this feature existed. Unit-tested (not yet exercised live) against 4 real scenarios: preference overriding the default ranking, sticky fallback when absent this episode, unchanged behavior with no preference set, and correct top-rank when the preference happens to already be in the default list. See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Decisions Log.
- Seven more bugs found and fixed in one extended live-testing session (2026-07-17), started by testing the uploader-memory feature against Frieren season 2 and Witch Hat Atelier. Full technical detail for each is in the Decisions Log (search "2026-07-17"); summarized here with final confirmation status:
  1. Jimaku apostrophe-search + multi-season entry-selection (`resolveTextFiles` now strips apostrophes and matches entries by parsed season suffix, not just `entries[0]`). **Confirmed live:** season 2 loads season 2's actual dialogue, not season 1's.
  2. Missing forced re-render on the auto-fetch and switcher-panel-pick paths (`video.dispatchEvent(new Event("timeupdate"))`, matching a fix the manual-upload path already had). **Confirmed live:** mid-episode switching applies immediately in both play and pause states.
  3. SPA-navigation detection retry (2 retries, 500ms apart) for "couldn't detect the show/episode." **Confirmed live:** episode-to-episode navigation shows subtitles immediately, no reload, across several episodes.
  4. Show-to-show navigation left subtitles permanently blank until a reload — `video` promoted to module scope, re-queried/rebound on every SPA navigation instead of captured once at `init()`. **Confirmed live:** switching Frieren → Witch Hat Atelier → Frieren now works immediately both directions.
  5. Stale-identity detection guard (SPA-navigation re-detection that still matches the pre-navigation episode identity is treated as stale, retried like a null detection). Bundled into the same overall fix as items 6/7 below for the "stuck on Haruhana" report — see there for final confirmation.
  6. `FILE_HINT` ("[JPN]") was searching ALL candidate files instead of only the top-ranked pick's own uploader, so it silently overrode both default ranking and any saved preference on Witch Hat Atelier specifically (the one show it happens to match) — confirmed via direct Jimaku API calls, not assumed. Scoped the hint search to the top pick's own bracket tag. **Confirmed live, combined with item 5:** sticky-fallback re-tested two ways (SubsPlease preference falling back to Haruhana on an episode without it, then correctly returning to SubsPlease; Haruhana preference falling back to NanakoRaws on one gap episode, then correctly returning to Haruhana on every other episode) — both passed.
  7. "Couldn't detect the show/episode" was appearing on non-watch pages (homepage, search) — an unrelated `<video>` element there (autoplay banner) satisfied the old video-only check. Added an `isWatchPage()` gate (`/watch/` in the URL) required alongside it. **Confirmed live:** error no longer appears on homepage/search.
- An eighth bug, found from a live screenshot the same session: a small dark rounded box was visible wherever `#jp-immersion-subtitle` happened to sit on screen (its position is fixed to the viewport, not the video) during any gap between subtitle lines — reported once near the usual subtitle position and once over Crunchyroll's own episode-list text. Root-caused (not assumed) after the user's own evidence ruled out a "native Crunchyroll element" theory: `renderCue` cleared the box to empty text but never hid it, and CSS padding/background/border-radius render even on an empty div; `pointer-events: none` (a deliberate, unrelated design choice, so the box never blocks clicking the video) is why it couldn't be selected via Inspect Element, which is what had pointed away from our own code initially. Fixed by hiding the element (`display: none`) specifically when there's no active line, and restoring it wherever real content is shown. Not yet re-confirmed live.

This closes out every currently-known Phase 4.5 build-order item, and the oval-artifact fix was confirmed live the same day. **Phase 4.5 formally closed (2026-07-17), user's explicit call** — see Decisions Log. Project moves to Phase 5.

(Manual subtitle file upload fallback, originally scoped as lower-priority Phase 4.5 work, was instead pulled forward and built in Phase 4, 2026-07-06 — see above.)

**Phase 5 — Anki export** (core-loop completion; moved out of the old "differentiation layer" 2026-07-02 — see Section 4; now follows Phase 4.5, pushed later 2026-07-05 to accommodate it — see Decisions Log)

*Done:*
- DRM feasibility test run live (2026-07-17) against 2 different shows, identical results both times: `canvas.drawImage()` doesn't throw but paints an all-black frame ("blank frame" — sampled pixel data confirmed all-zero); `video.captureStream()` throws outright with an explicit, unambiguous browser error — `NotSupportedError: Stream capture not supported with EME`, naming the DRM mechanism directly, not a guess. **Screenshot capture is confirmed blocked** by both independent methods agreeing — per the pre-decided rule (Decisions Log 2026-07-02), dropped from the roadmap entirely, not deferred.
