- Audio-capture follow-up test run live (2026-07-17): `AudioContext.createMediaElementSource()` connecting without an error wasn't trusted as proof on its own (DRM can mute silently rather than reject the call) — an `AnalyserNode` signal-level measurement over 3 seconds of actual dialogue confirmed real, non-silent audio data (max deviation 34, average ~7.9 across 363 samples, well above the silence baseline). **Audio capture is confirmed viable.** Real side-finding worth remembering for the eventual build: calling `createMediaElementSource()` claims the video element's audio output permanently for that page — if the connection is ever dropped without being explicitly rerouted back to the speakers, the episode goes silent for the rest of the page session (confirmed by accident: the first test script's own 500ms-then-disconnect "cleanup" did exactly this, requiring a page reload to recover). Any real audio-capture implementation needs to keep the graph connected through to `destination` the whole time, not just during a capture window.
- `chrome.desktopCapture` screenshot workaround considered and rejected without testing (2026-07-17) — see Decisions Log.
- AnkiConnect wiring built and confirmed live (2026-07-17): `background.js`'s `invokeAnkiConnect(action, params)` POSTs to `http://127.0.0.1:8765` with AnkiConnect's `{action, version, params}` protocol, throwing on either a network-level failure (Anki not open / AnkiConnect not installed — a distinct message from an AnkiConnect-level `error` field, so the eventual UI can tell "Anki isn't running" apart from "Anki rejected this specific request") or a non-null `error` in the response. `manifest.json` needed `http://127.0.0.1:8765/*` added to `host_permissions`. Confirmed a real, near-universal gotcha for any browser-extension-to-AnkiConnect integration, not Crunchyroll-specific: AnkiConnect's `webCorsOriginList` config doesn't trust extension origins by default, and needs the user to add `"*"` (or the specific `chrome-extension://` origin) before any request succeeds — walked the user through the config edit, including a JSON-syntax slip from a stray leftover config line, before the connection actually went through. Tested via `invokeAnkiConnect("version")` from the background service worker's own console (no throwaway UI needed) — returned `6` correctly.
- Core card fields built and confirmed live (2026-07-17): a dedicated "Japanese Immersion" deck + note type (Word/Reading/Gloss/Sentence fields), deliberately separate from the user's own "Kaishi 1.5k" study deck (their explicit call) and not reusing Anki's stock "Basic" type (only 2 fields, not enough to keep the four pieces independently stylable). `background.js`'s `ensureAnkiSetup()` creates both idempotently (checks `deckNames`/`modelNames` first) rather than assuming a one-time setup already ran, and `addAnkiNote({word, reading, gloss, sentenceHtml})` wraps `addNote` — the caller supplies `sentenceHtml` with the target word already wrapped in `<b>`, this function does no text processing of its own. Card direction is sentence-first per the user's explicit choice: front shows the sentence with the target word bolded (green via CSS), back reveals word/reading/gloss below a divider — tested with a real sample note, user confirmed the rendered card matches the design exactly.
- "Add to Anki" button built and confirmed live (2026-07-17), including the exact-offset bolding logic (user specifically tested a word appearing twice in one line — correct occurrence bolded, not the first one by default), Undo (confirmed the card actually disappears from Anki, not just the UI), and the "Anki isn't open" case (user closed Anki and confirmed the button correctly shows "Failed — retry" rather than a silent failure or a crash) — no separate error-state UI needed, the existing failure path already covers it. Per-card in the word-click popup (not once per popup), so a homograph with multiple cards shown lets the user pick exactly which reading/sense to save. Instant send on click, per the 2026-07-02 capture-UX decision — no separate confirm step. `content.js`'s `renderGroups` now tracks each clickable span's exact character offset within the rendered line (`renderGroups`/`onWordClick`/`buildSentenceHtml`), so the target word gets bolded in the Sentence field at its EXACT position — not a substring search, which could bold the wrong occurrence if the same word appears twice in one line. The sentence context is captured synchronously at click time, before the async dictionary-lookup round trip, since `lastText` is module-scope and the video may advance to a new line before the popup (and its "+ Anki" button) actually finishes rendering. Gloss field matches the popup's own numbered-sense display exactly, not a separately-formatted summary. Success replaces the button with inline "Added to Anki" + an Undo button (calls AnkiConnect's `deleteNotes`) — no separate toast component, since the popup card is already the transient surface a toast would otherwise duplicate. **Scoped down at the time:** "edit last card" was deliberately left out of this pass as a separable follow-up; it was built on 2026-07-29 and redesigned as an in-page editor on 2026-07-30.

- POS opt-in metadata toggle built and confirmed live (2026-07-17), including the backward-compatibility case (the earlier test card, created before the POS field existed on the note type, still opens fine in Anki's Browse window with the field simply empty) — the one fully-ready piece of the "opt-in metadata toggles" item, split out from frequency-rank/JLPT once those turned out to need much bigger prerequisite work (see below). A new "Extra info" section in the toolbar popup (`popup.html`/`popup.js`) holds all three toggles as one shared checkbox group (frequency/JLPT rendered disabled with a "Coming soon" hint, not hidden — so the eventual toggle set is visually stable rather than growing checkboxes in later sessions), backed by `chrome.storage.local` (`metaShowPos`), off by default. `background.js`'s `LOOKUP_WORD` handler now bundles the current toggle value into the same round-trip response content.js already waits on (no separate storage read needed). Gates ONLY the visible POS-chip line in the popup — `p` (the POS code array) is still read unconditionally everywhere else in `renderEntries` (formatPosChips's own computation, archaicTagLabel's internal archaic-verb-type check), since those aren't "showing POS metadata" in the sense the toggle means. `content.js`'s `renderGroups`-tracked exact-offset sentence-capture work from the "Add to Anki" button build now also threads a `pos` field through — sent as an empty string when the toggle is off (not omitted), so the Anki note type always has the field but a card only shows it when the user actually opted in. `background.js`'s `ensureAnkiSetup()` extended to add the new field to an ALREADY-EXISTING model (via `modelFieldAdd`) rather than only handling brand-new models, since the user's own note type was created in the previous session's step before this field existed — and now always reapplies the current card template on every call, not just when a field was added, so template-only changes (like the new conditional `{{#POS}}...{{/POS}}` line) also reach an existing install.

- **Frequency-rank badge built end-to-end (2026-07-19)** — full technical detail in Section 5 (Phase 5) above. Popup badge, opt-in `metaShowFreq` toggle, and optional Anki "Frequency" field all shipped; replaces the old always-on common-word badge entirely. Real corpus/nf-priority tiering for the 7,737 JMdict-common-but-TUBELEX-unscored entries, with a formal-register-bias mitigation — see Decisions Log. **Confirmed live the same day:** user found and verified real words landing in all three tiers (common/uncommon/rare), in both the popup badge and the Anki card's Frequency field. Initial confusion (待つ's badge looked unchanged from the old common-word badge) was expected, not a bug — same green-pill styling was kept deliberately (visual/color design for the 3 tiers still deferred) and 待つ happens to land in "common," the same tier the old binary flag would've shown; resolved by testing an uncommon/rare word instead.
- **JLPT-level tag built end-to-end (2026-07-22)** — full technical detail in Section 5 (Phase 5) above. Data source chosen (stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab, direct JMdict-id join), `scripts/apply-jlpt-level.js` built, popup badge + opt-in `metaShowJlpt` toggle + optional Anki "JLPT" field all shipped. Batch-test corpus validation shows zero regressions (all 11 patterns unchanged) and spot-checks (食べる→N5, 経済→N4, 憂鬱→N1) look correct. **Not yet live-tested in the browser** — same as every UI feature in this project, needs the user's pass on a real Crunchyroll page.
- **Audio Anki field + "last looked-up word" chip built (2026-07-22)** — full technical detail in Section 5 (Phase 5) above; design decided via the user's own written analysis (continuous rolling-buffer capture over rewind-and-recapture, cue-timestamp slicing, 15s chip lifetime, WAV encoding — all logged in the Decisions Log). `audio-capture.js` (new content script), chip logic in `content.js` (`chipifyPopup`/`removeChip`/`positionChip`), Anki "Audio" field wiring in `background.js`. **Two real bugs found in first live-testing round (2026-07-23), both fixed and re-verified live the same day** — early-click cutoff and fullscreen re-parenting, see Decisions Log. Still untested: 20–30 min continuous capture stability, SPA navigation during capture.
- **Show/episode opt-in metadata toggle built end-to-end (2026-07-23)** — full technical detail in Decisions Log. Fourth "Extra info" toggle (`metaShowSource`), Anki-only (no popup badge). Batch-test corpus validation unaffected (all 11 patterns unchanged). **Not yet live-tested.**
- **English subtitle dual-display + Anki "Translation" field built end-to-end (2026-07-23)** — English captions taken from Crunchyroll's own `play` API response after six rounds of live fact-finding ruled out reading the rendered text from the DOM, rendered in a new non-clickable box below the Japanese line, plus a back-only Anki `Translation` field. Corpus validation unaffected.

- **First live-testing round on the English subtitle/Translation build — four fixes (2026-07-26).** JP/EN desync, the two boxes swapping vertical position on long captions, exported audio tracking Crunchyroll's volume slider, and the `Translation` field appearing on the card front — plus two defects found while fixing them (raw newlines reaching Anki's HTML fields, and template updates silently no-oping on a renamed card). The Translation report turned out to be a dual-language Jimaku file feeding English into the `Sentence` field, which also revealed that the English caption pipeline had never actually run; three further defects in it were found and fixed the same day.
- **Second live-testing round on the English subtitle build — two new bugs fixed (2026-07-26, Frieren ep 7).** The English caption pipeline worked end to end for the first time. Fixed: embedded English/Chinese in dual-language provider files (a general mechanism, no per-provider special case), and JP/EN display timing, by pairing English to the Japanese cue window instead of matching it against the clock independently. Confirmed working and left alone: vertical order, preserved line breaks, and the `Translation` field.
- **Wrong-season Jimaku entry selection fixed (2026-07-26, reported as recurring).** KonoSuba season 3 was loading season 1's files, because season parsing required the literal word "season" and Jimaku marks that show's seasons with a bare trailing number. Parsing now covers all four naming conventions Jimaku uses, with a cour-marker exclusion and a bounded bare-number rule. The resolved entry name is now shown in the switcher panel, since the file names alone look equally correct whichever season they came from — which is why this went unnoticed repeatedly.
- **English layering fix + split-sentence rejoining for Anki (2026-07-26).** Two English lines could stack at once; the tuned overlap threshold was replaced with parameter-free midpoint containment. The mirror case — one English sentence spanning two Japanese cues, 12.4% of cues — is now rejoined for the Anki card only, with the audio clip widened to match.
- **Third live-testing round — seven bugs found and fixed (2026-07-27).** Re:Zero seasons resolving one off, two separate show-switch failures (no subtitles at all; English lost until reload), `《` and emoji-prefixed stage directions surviving the filters, the first subtitle after a load or alt-tab appearing in English before Japanese, and split-sentence Anki audio capturing only the first half. Episode-to-episode navigation confirmed working; the long-session check was not reached.
- **Generality pass over the 2026-07-27 fixes (2026-07-29, user's request).** Both audited fixes were still case-specific; defining them by class instead fixed a further 514 subtitle lines across 49,592 real corpus cues (`♬` music lines chief among them) and five more seasons across three shows, and added a cour-split retry for seasons whose back half Jimaku files separately.
- **English subtitle visibility bridged across split-sentence cue gaps (2026-07-29, to the user's written spec).** The English box no longer blinks when a `timeupdate` tick lands in the sub-second gap between a split sentence's two Japanese cues; it is held across gaps under `JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` when both flanking cues pair to the same English text.
- **"Edit last card" built (2026-07-29), then redesigned as an in-page editor (2026-07-30).** The last unbuilt Phase 5 item. Now a capture-verification chip, an in-page edit panel (freeform sentence/translation, word changed by dictionary re-lookup, read-only derived fields) and a waveform audio trim editor over retained PCM; Anki's own editor is demoted to an "Open in Anki" escape hatch. Required extracting `renderCue`'s dictionary pipeline into `buildGroupsForText` so the panel re-tokenizes through it rather than a copy.

- **First full live-test pass run (2026-07-31)** — a written checklist covering every Phase 5 "needs testing" item; ~20 findings, several outside its scope. Three fix batches landed the same day (edit panel and chip, audio capture, non-episodic content), all offline-verified only.
- **Edit-panel and chip fixes (2026-07-31).** Sentence corruption when changing the target word, the chip showing the wrong English line, overlapping and orphaned preview playback, panel keystrokes reaching Crunchyroll's player shortcuts, and a longer chip expand window for merged sentences.
- **Audio-capture fixes (2026-07-31).** Ring-buffer corruption after a `<video>` swap, wrong-episode audio after SPA navigation, seek desync and its 22–40s clips, and merged-clip edges over-capturing on stacking providers.
- **Anki send split into two phases (2026-07-31).** The card is written immediately; its audio field is filled in when the clip is ready, so no capture waits on a split sentence's second half.
- **Non-episodic content supported (2026-07-31).** Films, OVAs, specials and compilations now resolve to their own Jimaku entry via a widening query ladder and punctuation-insensitive matching, and an entry that can't be identified offers a picker instead of silently serving season 1's subtitles.

- **Entry resolution put under a fixture-based test suite, and three bugs it found fixed (2026-08-01).** `scripts/test-entry-resolution.js` drives the real `resolveTextFiles` end to end against a mocked Jimaku API, asserting the entry, the exact console lines and the resulting UI state on every case. Five of its thirteen cases failed on the code as shipped; all three underlying bugs shared one root cause — using "did Crunchyroll publish a season block?" as the test for episodic TV. See Decisions Log.
- **The no-valid-entry state loads nothing (2026-08-01, user's call).** A title that matches no Jimaku entry no longer falls back to the first search result behind a warning banner — nothing is fetched and nothing loads until the user picks an entry or uses manual upload, since a missed banner can put wrong-show sentences into permanent Anki cards. Playback is unaffected. AoT OAD fixtures updated to Crunchyroll's real captured metadata ("Attack on Titan OADs", season 66), since confirmed to be what JSON-LD carries on a live OAD page too; the classifier already handled it.
- **All six 2026-08-02 issues fixed (2026-08-02).** Renamed seasons on a split franchise; non-integer episode codes; over-narrowing; season 1 taking season 2's entry via a mismatched field pair; an episode-title search widening the pool with unrelated shows; and episodes unreachable through the number asked, resolved by deriving the offset from Jimaku's own index after the filename-based method was measured and rejected.
- **Five of six 2026-08-02 issues fixed (2026-08-02).** Renamed seasons on a split franchise; non-integer episode codes; over-narrowing; season 1 taking season 2's entry via a mismatched field pair; and an episode-title search widening the pool with unrelated shows. The sixth (episodes unreachable through the number asked) is diagnosed in "remaining" above.
- **Resolution audit built and two more defects fixed (2026-08-04).** `scripts/audit-resolution.js` checks actual file lists per episode against live Jimaku — the thing no previous sweep did — and is proven against pre-fix code on all six 2026-08-02 bugs before being trusted on anything new. Found and fixed the `(English Dub)` season-name miss (577 episodes' worth of seasons) and entries being dumped wholesale when only the series title matched. Two of its own defects fixed in the process (cached failures, over-firing NARROWED). Capture archived at `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-04.json`; known-bug capture at `fixtures/known-bugs-2026-08-02.json`.
- **Three fixes from the 2026-08-02 report (2026-08-02).** Renamed seasons on a split franchise no longer fall back to season 1's entry; a non-integer episode code (`14.5`, `EEX`) no longer picks a numbered file; narrowing no longer discards valid alternates for the same work. Adds `scripts/sweep-resolution.js`, which tests resolution end to end — something no previous sweep did. See Decisions Log.
- **Entry resolution verified live on 2026-08-01, but NOT closed** — the 2026-08-02 pass found six further issues on shows outside both sweeps, three still open. The 2026-08-01 "closed" claim rested on a sweep that never tested resolution. Section A passed in full on a real browser pass: One Piece episode 1156, Naruto episodes 1/33/144 through three separate triggers, Chronicle loading nothing, AoT OADs 4–8 with the wrong-episode file gone. That closes the area opened by the 2026-07-31 report — six rounds of fixes, 61 offline fixtures, two catalogue sweeps over ~7,200 episodes, and the checklist is retained as this area's regression script rather than as outstanding work.
- **Second catalogue sweep, franchise-heavy (2026-08-01)** — 45 series, 75 seasons, 2,385 episodes across Gundam, Fate, Dragon Ball and Detective Conan. Zero classification misses. Fixed detection discarding a usable episode code when `episode_number` was absent; found that `season_number` isn't unique within a series, and that the CMS shows the same code-vs-number split as JSON-LD. See Decisions Log.
- **Catalogue-wide sweep (2026-08-01)** — 30 series, 134 seasons, 4,829 episodes collected in one pass and swept against the real resolver. Found and fixed the "Special Edition" misclassification; closed the Crunchyroll-numbering open question with evidence; confirmed no pagination gaps and that `season_number` is unbounded. Capture archived at `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01.json`. See Decisions Log.
- **Long-franchise episode numbering resolved locally (2026-08-01).** Jimaku's `?episode=` filter collides across seasons on Naruto: Shippuuden — three distinct failure shapes measured, including one that answers confidently with the wrong episode. The episode is now resolved from the entry's own filenames, validated across all 500 numbered files under both possible Crunchyroll numbering conventions (0 failures either way). See Decisions Log.
- **Jimaku's `?episode=` filter skipped for non-episodic content (2026-08-01).** Five consecutive OADs were loading a file whose uploader release tag ("OAD2", "OAD3") coincided with Crunchyroll's episode number, because Jimaku's server-side filter parses those as episode numbers and a non-empty result was trusted outright. The general case of a problem first worked around per-show in 2026-07-06. Also fixes the sibling cache clobbering itself on every navigation. See Decisions Log.
- **Sibling-episode capture rebuilt on Crunchyroll's real adjacent-episode lookups (2026-08-01).** The batched season-episode-list response the first attempt watched for does not exist. Replaced with detection of `previous_episode`/`next_episode` responses, accumulating titles per season in `chrome.storage.local` across visits, and pinned by a new `scripts/test-sibling-sniffer.js` running the real script against the verbatim captured JSON. See Decisions Log.
- **Franchise-film disambiguation (2026-08-01, from the first service-worker-console live pass).** Three films failed three ways — Mugen Train silently loading Infinity Castle's subtitles, Infinity Castle refusing for the wrong reason, The Last Attack falling through to the TV series — all from the resolver never reading the episode's own title. It now does, searches on it, matches on it, and refuses to guess at a film it can't name. Also narrows the non-episodic file listing by episode title. See Decisions Log.
- **Dead resolver code removed (2026-08-01).** The computed `confident` flag and the "using X as a guess" warning it gated were only reachable through the `entries[0]` fallback, which the change above deleted. The switcher's second warning variant was checked and kept — it is still reached from the entry picker's own change handler. See Decisions Log.

- **Whole capture audited, fast pass and long tail (2026-08-11)** — 2,526 episodes, 0 request failures; reduced the outstanding defects to five root causes.
- **All five root causes fixed (2026-08-11/12)** — the arc-titled cour split, the audit's own false positives, a stage play and a two-part recap, later seasons playing season 1, and an entry carrying two numberings.
- **Three further defects found by verifying those five, and fixed (2026-08-12)** — a cour used without checking it covers the episode, a cour answering with its own Nth file alongside episode N, and a special whose entry holds one unnumbered file.
- **Section A of the live checklist passed in full (2026-08-13, user's pass)** — two faults no offline run could show, both fixed: Chronicle hid the entry picker, and its lone candidate was a different film offered as the answer.
- **The picked Jimaku entry is remembered per season (2026-08-13)**, so a season the resolver refuses to guess costs one pick rather than one per episode.
- **Section B of the live checklist run, and everything it found fixed (2026-08-15, user's pass).** Split-sentence audio, ordinary capture and the skip-then-capture clip length all passed. Five audio defects fixed from what it found: the capture clock now counts written samples so a pause can't inflate a clip; padding is asymmetric (0.2s / 0.6s) and the slice waits for the tail to exist; an interrupted capture is flushed rather than voided; the retained edit buffer is sealed rather than dropped on navigation, so the editor keeps working after an episode change; and the buffer's padding is clamped to the nearest discontinuity so extending a trim can't splice two moments together.
- **The audio trim editor rebuilt (2026-08-15)** — playhead, scrubbing, play/pause/resume, loop, in/out points, a time ruler, keyboard control, snap-to-speech, and a watcher that keeps filling in the right-hand padding while the panel is open. Also fixes the editor opening on the PREVIOUS card's clip while a capture is still in flight.
- **Six further live-pass reports fixed the same day (2026-08-15):** `{\anN}` override tags left in converted .srt files, the Japanese full stop 。, the first subtitle after a refresh being unclickable, offset memory not carrying between episodes of the same release, an unrelated background English line landing in the `Translation` field, and an extension reload leaving dead panels on the page with no explanation.

*Remaining, in priority order:*

1. **Re-test group B and run groups C–D** — group B was run on 2026-08-15 and everything it found is fixed, so it needs one more pass to confirm the fixes; C (two-phase Anki send) and D (edit panel) have still never been re-run since 2026-07-31. The rebuilt trim editor is entirely unexercised in a browser.
2. **The 20–30 minute continuous-session check**, which no pass has reached yet.
3. **Subtitle-text filtering group** — stage directions and speaker attributions that aren't the whole line, and `((`/`))` pairs. Reported 2026-07-31, still not fixed; the punctuation half of that report is now done (2026-08-15, 。 and `{\anN}` tags).
4. **NanakoRaws line-break shape** — one sentence rendering across four lines; needs a real file before changing how stacked cues are joined for display.

Entry resolution is **closed** as of 2026-08-13 — verified live and offline-clean; see Section 5 (Phase 4.5) for its current state and how to re-verify it.

*Live-test checklist (written 2026-08-01, Section A last rewritten 2026-08-12 for the resolution fixes).* **Section A passed in full on 2026-08-13 and is retained as a regression script; groups B–D are still offline-verified only** and are what a browser has yet to confirm. Ordered by value, not by where the bug was found. **Groups B–D have not been re-run since 2026-07-31 and are unchanged by the entry-resolution work.** Anki must be open with AnkiConnect running, and the console filtered to `jp-immersion` — several of these are only observable there. **Read the SERVICE WORKER console, not the page console** (`chrome://extensions` → the extension's "service worker" link): resolver logging runs in the background worker, so the page console shows none of it. Three live rounds were spent reading the wrong console before this was noticed on 2026-08-01.

**A. Entry resolution — ✅ PASSED IN FULL, live, 2026-08-13.** Run against the eight defects fixed on 2026-08-11/12, four of which had been silently serving the wrong episode. Found two faults no offline run could have shown (Chronicle's hidden picker, and its lone wrong-film candidate), both fixed the same day. **Kept below as this area's regression script, not as outstanding work** — re-run it if `resolveTextFiles`, the sibling sniffer or the episode-number extraction changes.

Reload the extension first (`chrome://extensions` → reload). **Read the SERVICE WORKER console** (`chrome://extensions` → the extension's "service worker" link), not the page console — resolver logging runs in the background worker, and three live rounds were lost to reading the wrong one. Filter to `jp-immersion`. A load producing **no** `Jimaku entry …` line at all is a failure however right the screen looks.

*A1 — must load, and load the RIGHT episode. These are the 2026-08-12 fixes; each was serving wrong content.*
1. **Dr. STONE SCIENCE FUTURE episode 13** — expect `…Cour 3 … covers episodes 25–37, not 13 — looking further`, then Cour 2. The subtitle must match 同じ月を見て, **not** 未来を唆るもの (episode 37, the old bug).
2. **SPY x FAMILY season 1 episode 13** — expect `dropped 7 of its 13 file(s) that state a different episode`. Subtitles must be PROJECT APPLE, not FIRST CONTACT (episode 25).
3. **Mushoku Tensei season 1 episode 12** — same shape, expect `dropped 6 of its 14`. (The figure was written as 4 before `[23]`-style bracket numbering was recognised; six is correct.)
4. **MHA: Vigilantes season 2 episode 6** — expect `carries a second numbering running 13 ahead — episode 6 is its 19`. Previously loaded nothing. Also try episodes 9–13.
5. **Haikyu!! TO THE TOP episode 14** — expect `continues from 14 (Jimaku splits this season across cours under an arc title)`. Previously failed outright; 12 of 25 episodes were dead.
6. **My Hero Academia "I am a Hero too"** — expect `holds one file and it names no episode`. Previously loaded nothing.
7. **Fruits Basket season 2 episode 1** — expect `also searched "Fruits Basket", which adds 3 entries`, entry **3850**. Previously played season 1.
8. **Chainsaw Man — The Compilation, both episodes** — each must get its OWN part (`then to the 2 numbered part 1/2 of it`), not the same four files.

*A2 — must load NOTHING and offer the picker. Any subtitles here is the failure.*
9. **Dr. STONE Season 2 or 3** — expect `its own name says it is not season 1 — declining to fall back`. Previously played season 1, silently.
10. **Chainsaw Man The Stage** — a stage play; expect no entry identified.
11. **Attack on Titan: Chronicle** and **Tokyo Ghoul: Root A** — both should decline, with the amber "pick one" banner and the video playing normally.

*A3 — must be UNCHANGED. The regression guards; all of them worked before and must still.*
12. **Naruto: Shippuuden episodes 1, 33 and 144** — the highest-value check. A later-season rule nearly blanked this show entirely; each episode uses a different trigger, and 144 was once unreachable.
13. **One Piece, any recent episode** — console must name the episode Crunchyroll displays (e.g. `episode 1156`, not `episode 1`), and show **no** `episode filter is unusable` line.
14. **Frieren season 2, episode 9** — must still offer its full file list; its files legitimately mix `09` and `37` numbering and both are the same episode.
15. **My Dress-Up Darling season 2, and an ordinary TV season** (KonoSuba S3, Demon Slayer's Entertainment District Arc) — plain `matched by …` lines, no retry lines at all.

*A4 — diagnostics, wherever they appear.* `Jimaku has nothing indexed under "…"` is the query ladder working; on ordinary TV it is a bug. `also searched this title's own name "…"` should appear **only** on films/OVAs/specials. `Jimaku's own episode filter is unusable …` should appear **only** on long franchises whose files carry 第N話 — on Frieren or One Piece it means the trigger is over-firing. **If anything resolves wrongly**, copy the full `jp-immersion` output plus the page's JSON-LD (`JSON.parse(document.querySelector('script[type="application/ld+json"]').textContent)`) — rounds have been lost to fixtures built on guessed metadata.

*A5 — sibling capture.* On any watch page run `__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` in the **page** console (that script is page-world). Expect `siblingEpisodesSeen` ≥ 1. It reads Crunchyroll's own adjacent-episode lookups, so it fires on load with no navigation; titles accumulate across visits.

**B. Audio capture — run 2026-08-15; items 4 and 5 passed, the rest were rewritten from what it found.**
1. **Interrupted capture (SPA navigation).** Capture a word, then immediately navigate to the next episode before the chip appears. It must now have audio — the line up to the moment you navigated — and that audio must be from the episode you captured in, never the next one. (Until 2026-08-15 this correctly but unhelpfully produced an empty audio field every time.) Repeat with a seek instead of a navigation.
2. **Pause, then capture.** Pause mid-line for 30+ seconds, resume, and capture immediately. The clip must be the length of the line. The failure was a 44-second clip of silence with the audio at the very end; a console line reporting a clip trimmed to 20s now means something is still wrong and is worth reporting with what you'd just done.
3. **Skip around, then extend.** Skip forward and back a few times, capture, open the edit panel and drag both handles all the way out. Every part of the padding must be continuous with the line — no audio spliced in from wherever you jumped from. The padding is deliberately clamped at a jump, so a short pad here is correct behaviour, not a bug.
4. **Sentence end.** Capture several ordinary lines and listen to the last syllable on each card. The tail should be complete; the report was that the very end was consistently omitted. If it still clips, say roughly how much — the pad is one named constant.
5. **Waveform corruption.** Switch shows entirely (the case that swaps the `<video>` element), then capture and open the edit panel. The waveform should be clean, with no discontinuities, without a page refresh.
6. **Split-sentence audio, on a `[NanakoRaws]` file** — passed on 2026-08-15, kept as a regression guard. Capture from the first half and then from the second; both clips should cover the whole sentence, neither should run into the following line, and both should be near-instant.
7. **Ordinary single-cue capture** — passed 2026-08-15, kept as the regression guard on all of the above.

**C. The two-phase Anki send — new behaviour, not just a fix.**
1. Capture a word and watch the card in Anki's browser: it should appear **immediately** with every text field, and its `Audio` field should fill in a moment later. A card that never gains audio is a real failure; a card that gains it a few seconds late is the design.
2. **Undo during the gap.** Capture, then hit Undo before the audio lands. The note must stay deleted — no audio write should resurrect or recreate it.
3. **Capture two words in quick succession**, undoing neither. Both cards must end up with their own audio; the second capture must not strand the first.

**D. Edit panel.**
1. **Change word** — the fix for the reported corruption. Capture a card, open the panel, change the target word, save, and check the Japanese sentence in Anki is **character-for-character unchanged**, with only the bolding moved. Then reopen the panel and confirm it still reads correctly.
2. **Count the notes** while doing D1 — this is the unexplained half of that report. One capture plus one word change must leave exactly **one** note in Anki, not two.
3. **Typing.** Type a space and the letter "m" into both text boxes. The characters must appear; the video must not pause or mute.
4. **The rebuilt trim editor (2026-08-15) — entirely untested in a browser.** Click the waveform to move the playhead, press Space to play from it, press Space again to pause, and confirm the episode's own sound is muted while a preview plays and restored after. Then: drag a handle during playback; press I and O to set the edges at the playhead; turn Loop on and off; press Snap to speech and check it lands on the line rather than on room tone. Close the panel mid-playback — the audio must stop immediately.
5. **Open the panel IMMEDIATELY after capturing.** Two things to watch, both fixed on 2026-08-15 and neither verified: while the line is still playing the panel should say it is still capturing rather than showing anything, and the waveform that then appears must be THIS card's, not the previous card's. Leave the panel open and watch the right-hand padding grow to its full 3 seconds on its own.
6. **Editing after changing episode.** Capture a card, move to the next episode, then open the edit panel from the persistent control. The audio must still be editable — this said "no longer available to edit" until 2026-08-15.
7. **Extending the clip** — still never confirmed end to end. Drag the end handle outward and confirm you hear the extra audio on the *saved* card in Anki, not just in the preview.
8. **Anki closed mid-edit** — also never tested. Open the panel, make edits, quit Anki, hit Save: expect an error and your edits still sitting there. Reopen Anki, save again, confirm it lands.
9. **Chip timing** — a long merged sentence should now stay readable, and its English should be **the captured line's**, not the next one's. A single-word capture shouldn't feel like it lingers.

**E. The 2026-08-15 non-audio fixes — all six new, none seen in a browser.**
1. **First subtitle after a refresh.** Refresh the page mid-episode and click a word in the very first line that appears. It must be clickable; it used to render as plain text for its whole time on screen because kuromoji was still loading.
2. **Offset memory.** Adjust the offset on one episode, then go to the next episode of the same show and same release. It should start at the adjusted value. Then switch to a DIFFERENT release group in the switcher — that one should start from its own value (or 0), not inherit. An adjustment made before the file list loads must not be overwritten when it arrives.
3. **`{\anN}` tags and 。.** Watch a provider that showed either. No `{\an8}` anywhere on screen or in a captured `Sentence`; no 。 either, with a line holding two sentences reading as one space-separated line rather than two run together. `、`, `？` and `！` must all survive.
4. **Background English lines.** Find a moment with two English lines on screen at once and capture from the Japanese one. The `Translation` field must hold only the spoken line's translation. If the stray line is still there, note the show and timestamp — the rule keys off the caption file's own positioning, and whether Crunchyroll uses it on this content is the unverified half.
5. **Extension reload.** Reload the extension at `chrome://extensions` with a watch page open. Every panel should disappear and be replaced by one banner offering a Reload button; clicking it must bring everything back working, audio capture included.
6. **Nothing regressed.** Ordinary word lookup, capture and the English line, on a show that was working before.

Open, not scheduled: sentence-only capture with no target word — deliberately still not built. Its own deferral reason is "revisit once there's usage data from the popup-based flow" (2026-07-04), and that usage data doesn't exist yet, so building it now would be guessing at a capture paradigm rather than answering a known need. Left for the user to schedule.

2. Mono-ruby (per-kanji distributed furigana) — deliberately deferred, not a quick fix; full reasoning and rejected naive-heuristic approach in Section 5

4. Auto-pause on new subtitle line

Raised in the 2026-07-22 audio/chip design discussion as independent scope; exact priority within this list not yet decided, appended here for now.

## 7. Decisions Log

Settled decisions, ordered by date, so they don't get re-debated. Each entry is a genuine choice between real alternatives (test: "what did you choose this over?" has a real answer) — findings, constraints, or verified facts with no alternative live in Section 5 instead. Where a decision was later revised, it's consolidated into one entry showing the final state.

**2026-06-22 — Browser extension, not a desktop app.** Simpler payments, distribution, cross-platform story.

**2026-06-22 — Universal Jimaku import from day one, no curated show list.** Sync/offset tooling treated as must-have, not a later add-on.

**2026-06-22 — V1 platform scope is Crunchyroll only.**

**2026-06-23 — Stay client-side; kuromoji.js locked in as the v1 morphological analyzer.** Rejected matching ManabiDojo's server-side stack (Ichiran/Ichimoe + CaboCha) — that would take on the same backend-cost structure implicated in their own reliability complaints and undercut our pricing positioning. kuromoji.js is pure JS, no WASM toolchain, and shares MeCab/IPADIC lineage with Ichiran itself. Benchmark lindera-wasm/Vibrato-wasm only if real corpus testing shows a quality gap.

**2026-06-23 — jmdict-simplified (JSON) instead of parsing raw EDRDG XML directly.** The raw format's DTD entities are awkward to parse in JS.

**2026-06-23 — CLAUDE.md ↔ project-plan.md sync process defined.** Read Decisions Log/Open Questions at session start; update both files and scan for decisions/open-question changes at session end.

**2026-06-23 — Jimaku API key stored in `chrome.storage.local` via a toolbar popup**, not in any config file.

**2026-06-24 — Fixed auxiliary-token segmentation with a targeted grouping rule**, not a tokenizer swap. Merging 助動詞 tokens into the preceding word was a grouping/boundary problem, not a tagging-accuracy problem, so pulling forward the lindera/Vibrato benchmark wouldn't have helped.

**2026-06-24 — Regenerated `jmdict-compact.json` (27MB → ~29MB) to retain POS codes, common-flag, and a posTags map**, to support new popup features (POS labels, common badge). Low-cost since the source was already downloaded.

**2026-06-30 — Te-form auxiliary chains split into two clickable units** (main-verb+て / auxiliary) rather than merged into one. Lets the learner look up both the verb meaning and the auxiliary's nuance separately.

**2026-06-30 — Extracted shared `tokenize-utils.js`** rather than duplicating `groupTokens`/`JAPANESE_WORD_RE` in the batch-test script, to avoid two implementations silently diverging.

**2026-06-30 — Made particles clickable with JMdict POS-code "prt" filtering**, rather than suppressing them entirely (frustrating) or showing all matching entries (misleading homophone nouns).

**2026-06-30 — Simplified Rule 1 (te-form merge) to always merge [動詞]+[て/で]** regardless of the following token, removing a constraint that blocked correct merges for terminal/non-auxiliary uses (乗り遅れて, 止まっていい).

**2026-06-30 — Stage-direction filter (`STAGE_RE`) added to content.js itself**, not left in the batch script only, since lines like （ドアの開く音） are never useful to the learner.

**2026-06-30 — Positioning pivot: lean into differentiation instead of feature parity.** After direct review of ManabiDojo's site, confirmed several planned "differentiators" (romaji, hotkeys, POS/inflection, furigana) are already parity or behind theirs. Rejected chasing feature-for-feature parity (AI breakdown, wordbook/quiz, multi-platform) as infeasible for a solo project; leaned into Crunchyroll-only depth, an AI-free architecture as a stance, and a structurally lower cost base instead — see Section 3.

**2026-06-30 — Anki export moved from excluded to in-scope**, instead of building an in-house wordbook/SRS/quiz system. The target user already has SRS history in Anki; feeding that system fits the core "capture and keep watching" goal better than a second, competing system would.

**2026-06-30 — Reaffirmed AI grammar/sentence explanations stay excluded, on stronger grounds.** Originally excluded as "expensive to replicate"; now excluded because it actively works against the product's purpose (breaks immersion, competes with a more-trusted resource like Bunpro) regardless of how it's built. Rejected an opt-in BYOK version for the same reason — the issue isn't technical feasibility.

**2026-06-30 — Subscription + lifetime-purchase pricing, replacing the original flat one-time-price plan.** Matches standard practice (WaniKani, Bunpro, ManabiDojo). The user's explicit preference, since the market has normalized subscription+lifetime enough that a flat one-time price would read as unfamiliar rather than a selling point.

**2026-07-01 — Jimaku entry-selection fixed via exact-match preference, not hardcoded IDs.** Prefers an exact case-insensitive name/english_name match over `entries[0]`. Rejected hardcoding known-good entry IDs per tested show — doesn't generalize to a real user's search.

**2026-07-01 — Kuromoji "user dictionary" plan abandoned; replaced with a JMdict-anchored merge mechanism.** The library has no working runtime user-dictionary feature at all (dead code, confirmed). Rejected (1) recompiling the binary IPADIC dictionary offline — too heavy for an open-ended, never-"complete" problem (anime slang is an unbounded long tail); (2) a hardcoded broken-word list — same reason, and root-causing ただいま！/か〜くれんぼ showed they were a Viterbi cost/punctuation-adjacency issue, not missing vocabulary. Built `findKanaMergeCandidates`/`applyKanaMerges` instead: self-generalizes to any show without per-word maintenance.

**2026-07-01 — Kana-merge gating settled on a function-POS-code filter, not the `common` flag or no gate at all.** `common` rejected real fixes like かくれんぼ (not flagged common despite being ordinary). No gate at all produced real false positives at 1257-line-corpus scale (はいい→"dethronement", あったか→"warm", etc.) that all matched only a content-word POS, while every correct match matched a function-word POS.

**2026-07-01 — General phrase-matcher built per explicit design mandate**, not hardcoded phrase-by-phrase. Went through three corpus-driven hardening rounds: existence-only → function-POS gate (rejected false positives like にし→"west"); gate wrongly rejected legit compounds (文化祭) → two-path rule (all-名詞 spans need only existence, mixed spans need the gate); that wrongly rejected ひとりぼっち (mis-tagged by the pre-existing ぼっち segmentation bug) → accept any 3+-group span on existence alone, since every false positive found was 2-group and every real phrase was 3+.

**2026-07-01 — Pronoun+suffix compounds (私たち, 僕たち) handled by a deterministic Rule 0.5**, not the general phrase-matcher, since `pn` isn't a function-word POS code (would fail the matcher's own gate) and the pattern is always grammatical regardless of that specific pronoun's JMdict headword status.

**2026-07-01 — Rule 3 (助動詞 absorption) narrowed to an explicit allowlist, in two passes.** First pass: restricted to verb/adjective-preceding tokens, excluding nouns (fixed 僧侶だろ directly, freed んだ for the phrase-matcher). Second pass, same day: rewritten from "any 助動詞 not excluded" to a positive allowlist (`PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` + plain-copula check via `conjugated_form`), so でしょう/だろう/らしい/べき/まい correctly stay separate. Rejected special-casing each construction individually in favor of one structural rule, per the user's "does this carry independent meaning" test applied uniformly.

**2026-07-01 — いる/居る ordering fixed with kanji-rarity (rK/sK) tags, not a priority/frequency resort.** jmdict-simplified's public JSON doesn't expose priority-tier data at all — confirmed by direct inspection of the raw release.

**2026-07-01 — Three smaller live-testing fixes, each user-directed:** (1) suppress the popup entirely for proper-noun-tagged words with no real JMdict entry, rather than adding JMnedict name translations (deferred, not rejected — narrowed to katakana-only runs 2026-07-04, and the JMnedict deferral itself hardened to a full decline the same day, see below for both); (2) strip speaker-name prefixes ((Name)/Name:) from the display entirely, rather than normalizing both conventions to one style; (3) added a kuromoji-POS-to-JMdict-POS-category definition filter, reusing the particle-filter pattern — confirmed it only helps cross-category collisions (こと's particle sense vs. noun senses), not same-category ones.

**2026-07-01 — こと-style sense-ordering deferred to a dedicated future session**, not solved this session. The ideal domain-specific frequency list no longer exists on GitHub; the Leeds Corpus alternative has an unclear/NOASSERTION license (a risk this project has otherwise avoided). TUBELEX (BSD-3-Clause) looks viable but wasn't investigated further — rejected integrating it in a rushed tail-end addition in favor of giving it its own focused, corpus-validated session. (Superseded 2026-07-03 — see below.)

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Katakana-name detection uses JMdict existence, not kuromoji's 固有名詞 tag alone.** The tag proved inconsistent (レン/ハイター tagged correctly; equally-fictional ヒンメル/アイゼン tagged as ordinary nouns) — existence-check is the reliable fallback for what the tag misses.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Half-width katakana normalization centralized at the JMdict-index-lookup layer** (`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana()` in `background.js`), not at each candidate-generation call site, so every consumer (word clicks, kana-merge, phrase-merge, katakana-name check) benefits from one fix instead of needing it repeated four times.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Phrase-matching restructured to run on raw kuromoji tokens instead of `groupTokens`' output**, per explicit design requirement (帰ったら tokenizes as one combined auxiliary token, so Rule 3 would absorb it before any post-hoc scan on grouped output could see a separate たら candidate). Implemented via a `fuseSpans` parameter threaded into `groupTokens` plus per-group token-range tracking.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Dual-view mechanism added: a matched phrase can attach as a secondary note instead of replacing the individual tokens**, when both boundary tokens are genuine content words worth their own click. Rejected applying dual-view universally — じゃ/か/ら/だ don't carry useful standalone meaning the way もの/なる do, so exposing them again would undo the point of the Rule 3 allowlist work.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — と (助詞) carved out as an always-dual-view exception**, even adjacent to a negative verb (探さないと). Rejected treating it like じゃ/か/ら/だ (always-fuse) — と retains a genuinely common, unrelated job (plain reported-speech/conditional と) far more often than the ないと "have to" idiom; a full fuse would mis-gloss ordinary quotation as an obligation.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Added Rule 0.6 (ん + copula-family) as its own deterministic rule**, not left to the phrase-matcher. JMdict has a headword for んだ but not んだろう/んでしょう, so the matcher's exact-string-anchoring genuinely can't reach the extended forms no matter how it's tuned.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — Added a "baseline redundancy" filter to phrase-matching**: reject any candidate span `groupTokens` already produces correctly on its own. The alternative (accept the resulting noise as residual, like があん) was rejected because this specific collision (い+た vs. 板 "board") is far higher-frequency — it appears in nearly every past-progressive-tense scene.

**2026-07-01 (second round) — `hasSuspiciousFragment` signal tightened to "two adjacent ≤2-character 動詞/形容詞 tokens," in two corrections.** First version (any single 1-character verb/adjective token) was too broad, wrongly catching normal conjugation stems (し for する, い for いる) and causing new collisions. Tightening to "exactly 1 character" then missed ぼっ itself, since it's 2 UTF-16 code units (ぼ + small っ), not 1.

**2026-07-02 — AnkiConnect chosen over offline .apkg/CSV export**, superseding an earlier same-session provisional pick of offline export. Offline export breaks the "capture and keep watching" loop (best case: queue locally, stop watching later, manually import — the same failure mode that excluded the built-in wordbook); building both at once would double the design/testing surface. AnkiConnect is architecturally consistent with the no-backend approach (a `fetch()` to a local server, same shape as Jimaku calls) and a reasonable ask of a user who already has SRS habits (it's Anki's most-installed add-on). Known limitation to design around: requires Anki desktop running at capture time.

**2026-07-02 — Anki card core fields: target word, reading, gloss, full subtitle sentence (target word marked)** — not word-only, and not the full audio/screenshot version. Word-only loses the sentence context that gives real value over a standalone SRS system. Full sentence-mining with audio/screenshot deferred pending the DRM feasibility test, not rejected outright.

**2026-07-02 — Metadata toggles (POS, common-word, JLPT, frequency-placeholder) share one toggle set across the popup and the card**, not separate settings per surface — avoids the popup and the card silently disagreeing about what's shown for the same word. (Toggle *composition* changed 2026-07-04 — common-word/placeholder replaced by a real frequency-rank badge, see below — but this entry's architecture decision, one shared toggle set, still stands unchanged.)

**2026-07-02 — JLPT tagging scope reversal is narrow, not a full reopening.** In scope only as an opt-in, off-by-default toggle — not an always-visible label/filter/sort. The original exclusion targeted active mid-episode teaching breaking immersion (same reasoning as the AI-explanation exclusion); passive, opt-in metadata the user deliberately enables doesn't do that. Does not reopen the AI-explanation or wordbook/SRS exclusions, which stand for unrelated reasons. If jmdict-simplified doesn't carry JLPT tags at all, sourcing them is a separate, unscoped build task. (Sourcing question resolved 2026-07-04 — see below.)

**2026-07-02 — Frequency-marker toggle ships as a UI placeholder now; underlying logic explicitly not built yet.** Same root cause as the こと sense-ordering gap: jmdict-simplified only exposes a collapsed `common: boolean`. Shipping the placeholder now keeps the toggle set's shape stable while gating the real logic behind the same future dedicated session already planned for こと. Drop the separate common-word toggle once frequency ships for real, not before. (Superseded 2026-07-04 — see below.)

**2026-07-02 — Sentence translation on the Anki card deferred, not rejected. (Built 2026-07-23.)** Deferred at the time pending Phase 6's English-subtitle pipeline, which was itself pulled into Phase 5 on 2026-07-23. Low-cost to defer: if the learner already has the word's meaning from the card, a full sentence translation is mainly a comprehension backstop on a later review, not core value.

**2026-07-02 — Audio/screenshot Anki fields left undecided, gated on a not-yet-run feasibility test**, rather than assuming they're blocked from general DRM/EME research already done this session. That research (Widevine + `canvas.drawImage()`/`captureStream()`) was general, not confirmed against Crunchyroll's actual player — the project's own standard (per the forced-subtitle precedent) is to verify a platform limitation hands-on before closing it out.

**2026-07-02 — Anki capture lives in the existing popup, not a separate gesture.** "Add to Anki" button on the existing word-click popup, sends instantly, undo/edit available after — not a separate line-level hotkey/gesture, and not a pre-send edit step. The separate-gesture idea is speculative demand with no confirmed evidence, carried forward as an open question instead of built. Mirrors a proven pattern (Yomitan, which ManabiDojo's own users are already pairing their tool with). A pre-send edit modal was rejected because it reintroduces the "stop and interact with a form" friction the instant-send/undo pattern exists to avoid.

**2026-07-02 — Anki export moved from the differentiation layer into the core-loop must-have list** (Section 4), reflecting that it's the endpoint of the "capture and keep watching" loop, not an add-on feature.

**2026-07-02 — Jimaku per-season offset/uploader memory pushed to Phase 6, not scheduled into Phase 4/5.** Lower priority than the differentiation-layer work already committed to those phases; a quality-of-life item on top of an already-working manual offset tool, better designed once there's a real, sustained user base to design the memory feature around. Not a "we don't need this" — stays a known, real UX shortfall vs. ManabiDojo. (Un-deferred 2026-07-04 — see below.)

**2026-07-03 — こと-style sense ordering un-deferred and solved via TUBELEX.** Re-verified the candidate first rather than assuming the 07-01 pick was still right: confirmed directly against the actual data (not just docs), BSD-3-Clause license, and its lemma field distinguishes exact kanji spellings (事 vs 琴), not just readings — exactly the missing signal. Rejected re-touching the raw jmdict-eng release: `jmdict-compact.json`'s own index already links every kanji spelling to the same entry as its kana reading, so per-entry frequency scores could be derived entirely from data already in the repo plus the TUBELEX file. Built as a general re-ranking of every multi-entry index key (`scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js`), not a こと-specific patch.

**2026-07-03 — 王都-style compound-noun misclassification fixed by excluding pure noun+noun spans from dual-view classification, not by changing the fuse-eligibility gate itself.** The fuse gate already would have accepted these spans — the bug was `isDualViewMatch` claiming them first. Confirmed the fix doesn't regress the case dual-view exists for (もの…なる): that span always has a particle between its boundary tokens, so it's never all-名詞.

**2026-07-03 — でしょう/だろう fixed with a direct override inside Rule 3's own group-construction step, not by relaxing the phrase-matcher's baseline-redundancy filter.** The redundancy filter's job (skip re-checking a span groupTokens already handles correctly) is still valid in general; でしょう/だろう was a case where the baseline grouping's *span* was already right but its *word* wasn't, which the filter can't distinguish. Fixing it at the source (mirroring how Rule 0.6 already special-cases the ん-prefixed んでしょう/んだろう construction) is more robust than patching the filter.

**2026-07-03 — Noun+plain-copula groupings (凱旋+です) relabeled as an explicit "+ です (copula)" attachment, not suppressed entirely.** Both options were on the table; relabeling keeps the copula visible as real information rather than silently dropping it.

**2026-07-03 — Popup content-decisions spec (POS chip labels, inflection-line rework) deliberately scoped to popup text/labeling only, no grouping changes**, even after discovering along the way that られる/れる, せる/させる, and ば weren't merging into the preceding verb at all — flagged as a separate gap rather than silently expanding scope inside a content-only task. Confirmed empirically (no git history existed to check "was it different before") that this was a pre-existing gap from when the allowlist was first written, not a regression: `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` listed these but Rule 3's absorption check required `pos === "助動詞"`, while kuromoji always tags them `動詞`/`接尾`. Fixed as its own follow-up once flagged, by additionally accepting that tag combination.

**2026-07-03 — ば conditional absorption added, a genuinely new scope decision, approved via the project's own litmus test.** Unlike れる/られる/せる/させる, ば was never in the `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` allowlist at all — its absence wasn't a bug but a real "should we build this" question. Ran it through the litmus test directly: leaving ば as the one pure-inflection auxiliary not merged was an inconsistency with why て/た merging was already worth doing, not a deliberate line. Gated on `conjugated_form === "仮定形"` so it doesn't misfire on ば's unrelated "place" noun sense.

**2026-07-03 — でしょう/だろう fix extended to the bare conjecture stem (no う), after a user question prompted a re-check for generality.** The original fix only overrode the lookup word once う had actually been absorbed; colloquial そうだろ/そうでしょ (a real, common spoken form, not a truncation) still fell through to だ/です's own definition. Simplified to always use the reconstructed surface rather than gating on whether う was present.

**2026-07-03 — Fixed a real regression in the でしょう/だろう fix, caught by the user-run `scripts/batch-test.js` corpus validation.** `isConjectureCopula`'s gate (`conjugated_form !== "基本形"`) was broader than intended — でした/だった (past-tense です/だ) tokenize with `conjugated_form` values that are also "not 基本形" but aren't the conjecture stem, so the fix wrongly routed their surface string through as the lookup word (no JMdict headword of its own), turning a silent wrong-entry bug into a new lookup miss. Narrowed to the exact signal originally intended (`conjugated_form === "未然形"`), confirmed via a before/after corpus diff (Pattern 2 lookup misses: 43 → 38, all other patterns unchanged). This is the reason the user's own corpus validation mattered — hand-picked test sentences never happened to include a past-tense copula case.

**2026-07-03 — Multi-reading display-precision issue investigated, diagnosis corrected mid-investigation, then deliberately not fixed this session.** Initial hypothesis (an index-corruption bug affecting ~2,500–10,500 entries) was wrong — verified via the raw `jmdict-eng` release that every sampled case was a real multi-reading word, not a data error. Rejected a string-similarity threshold as a tightening tool: it doesn't actually separate the two categories (a confirmed-legitimate case, でけえ/でかい, scored *lower* similarity than the confirmed-bug case that started the investigation, くる/えぐる). Rejected building the entry-splitting-by-reading fix now — user's call, based on the common-flag breakdown (~11.2% of cases involve a common entry) — logged as a known limitation instead, to prioritize later against real-world frequency of occurrence rather than guessing now. (Rescheduled to active work — see later entry below.)

**2026-07-03 — Kanji-heteronym sense-context disambiguation (一行 いっこう vs いちぎょう) rejected as an anchor-to-kuromoji-reading fix, per a check requested before building anything.** Tested kuromoji's per-token `reading` output across several heteronym pairs before deciding: unreliable both in the specific reported case (a segmentation quirk leaves no usable single-token reading at all) and in general (correct for 大分, wrong for 十分/人気). Explicitly rejected pursuing real sentence-level disambiguation to solve this properly, on the same grounds as the AI-grammar-explanation exclusion (meaningfully bigger lift, cuts against the no-AI/no-backend architecture) — logged as an accepted limitation instead. (Reverified and reasoning refined 2026-07-04 — see below.)

**2026-07-04 — Frequency-rank badge graduates from placeholder to a real, built feature; common-word toggle dropped in its place.** Closes a genuine parity gap vs. ManabiDojo's color-coded frequency badges (Section 3.1), following the plan already set on 2026-07-02: the TUBELEX usage-frequency data built for homograph ordering (Section 5) is repurposed to power a user-facing graduated frequency tier, replacing the binary common-word flag rather than sitting alongside it. Rejected building this as an addition to the existing common-word toggle — a graduated signal supersedes a binary one outright, and keeping both would just be redundant UI. (Tier design finalized in a follow-up decision below.)

**2026-07-04 — Kanji ON/KUN reading display rejected as a feature, including as an opt-in toggle.** ManabiDojo has this (Section 3.1), but it fails the product's own litmus test differently than a simple missing feature: ON/KUN readings are kanji-level pedagogy (how to read a character in isolation), not confirmation of the specific word/inflection in context — that's the job of a kanji-SRS tool (e.g. WaniKani) the target user already has and trusts. Making it opt-in doesn't change this, since the objection isn't intrusiveness — it's the same reasoning that excludes AI grammar explanations and the built-in wordbook regardless of how unobtrusively they're offered.

**2026-07-04 — Names dictionary (JMnedict integration) and custom dictionary URL buttons / choice of dictionary source both declined, not just deferred.** Both are real ManabiDojo parity items (Section 3.1) but neither was judged worth building: a dedicated names database adds a second data source and ingestion pipeline for a problem the kanji-name fix below already addresses well enough, and custom dictionary buttons/source-switching cut against the "nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary" positioning (Section 1) — there's one dictionary, not a menu of them. Not tracked as an Open Question or a UX gap to close in Section 3.3, since the user has decided against building either, not merely postponed the decision.

**2026-07-04 — Kanji-containing proper nouns made clickable again, narrowing the 2026-07-01 popup-suppression fix to katakana-only name runs.** The 2026-07-01 fix ("suppress the popup entirely for proper-noun-tagged words with no real JMdict entry") applied uniformly to any 固有名詞-tagged word, katakana or kanji. Reconsidered: kanji names carry real character-level meaning (readings, kanji-level glosses) useful to a learner even without a dedicated name entry, unlike arbitrary katakana transliterations (ヒンメル, アイゼン) which have no meaningful decomposition to show. Kanji-containing names are now excluded from the suppression rule and behave as ordinary clickable words; katakana-only runs remain fully inert (see Section 5), since that inertness targets exactly the case — transliterated fantasy/foreign names — that still has nothing useful to show. Rejected building a JMnedict names dictionary as the fix instead — the actual problem was over-suppression, not missing name-specific data (see the Names-dictionary decision above). (Implemented 2026-07-05 — see below.)

**2026-07-04 — Duplicate-gloss homograph entries get a real JMdict-sourced "(archaic)"-style tag where one exists, demotion as the fallback only.** Original call was demotion alone (mirroring dual-view); refined after reconsidering that JMdict already carries real rarity data for exactly this case — `re_inf` reading-level tags (`ok` = out-dated reading, same tag family as the existing rK/sK kanji-rarity fix) and `misc` sense-level tags (`arch`, `obs`, `dated`). Rejected demotion-only as the final design: it visually deprioritizes the duplicate without ever telling the user why, where a sourced tag (closer to what Jisho shows) is strictly more informative for the same UI cost. Demotion remains the fallback for any duplicate that turns out not to carry one of these tags — not every rare-looking duplicate is formally "archaic" in JMdict's own data, and that needs verifying against the raw release per-case, not assumed. May require adding `re_inf`/`misc` to `jmdict-compact.json` if not already present, the same kind of low-cost regeneration as the 2026-06-24 POS-code addition. (Implemented 2026-07-05 — see below.)

**2026-07-04 — Multi-reading display-precision fix (くる/えぐる-style) rescheduled from backlog to active work.** Supersedes the earlier same-day call to defer this pending real-world frequency-of-occurrence data. User's call: fix it now rather than wait — scheduled into Phase 4/5 (see Build Order). Still requires restructuring compact JMdict entries to support more than one reading per entry, not a data prune (Section 5); the ~37,000 affected (index key, entry) pairs and ~11.2% common-entry overlap already scoped stand as the starting reference for the fix.

**2026-07-04 — JLPT data will be sourced externally if jmdict-simplified doesn't carry it, rather than dropping the toggle.** Closes the standing 2026-07-02 open question. The toggle stays either way — if jmdict-simplified has JLPT tags, use them directly; if not, this becomes an explicit, scoped build task to find and integrate an external JLPT-level source (candidate not yet chosen).

**2026-07-04 — Frequency-rank badge tier design locked in.** 3 text tiers (Common / Uncommon / Rare), no raw numbers, no badge shown when there's no TUBELEX data for a word. Rejected 4 tiers split as Common/Very Common at the top — that split doesn't change what the learner does with either label (both mean "don't dwell"), so it's a distinction without a difference; a 4th tier would be more useful at the *bottom* (e.g. "Very Rare") if ever added, not the top. Rejected showing the raw TUBELEX frequency number — meaningless without context, forces interpretation mid-episode, works against "confirm and keep watching." Rejected defaulting an unranked (no-data) word to "Rare" — same reasoning already applied to the そうか/TUBELEX scoring fix: absence of data isn't evidence of rarity. Exact numeric thresholds per tier are an implementation detail, ideally percentile-based against the real TUBELEX distribution rather than fixed counts — not decided here. Visual treatment (color-coding, iconography) is explicitly out of scope for this decision — bundled into whatever future task designs the Anki/popup card visuals generally.

**2026-07-04 — Jimaku search-by-title mismatch resolved via TMDB/AniList ID resolution from Crunchyroll metadata, over a fuzzy-search fallback.** More build effort than a fuzzy-match layer on Jimaku's own search, but more reliable — an ID-based lookup doesn't depend on how close Crunchyroll's English title happens to render to Jimaku's JP/romanized indexing, which a fuzzy match would.

**2026-07-04 — Romaji subtitle-display toggle excluded, not merely deprioritized.** A subtitle-level romaji layer is a total-beginner feature — it exists for learners who can't read kana yet, which the target user, by definition, already can. Same shape of exclusion as kanji ON/KUN readings (see above): not a priority call, this isn't the product's job for this persona, so it doesn't get built even as a low-priority extra.

**2026-07-04 — Kana-only subtitle-display mode also excluded, for a sharper reason than romaji.** Considered as a possibly-more-useful alternative to romaji, then rejected: kana-only doesn't just fail to help this persona, it actively works against furigana mode, which keeps kanji visible and adds the reading on top — reinforcing kanji the target user likely already studies elsewhere. Stripping the kanji out entirely (what kana-only would do) is a step backward for that goal, not a lighter version of the same feature.

**2026-07-04 — Jimaku per-season offset/uploader memory un-deferred, moved to active Phase 6 work with a concrete design**, superseding the 2026-07-02 "defer until a real user base exists" call. Reconsidered scope: a show's uploader/offset choice is stable for its whole run in practice (not a per-episode judgment call), which removes most of the edge-case design risk that motivated deferring it. Design: cache the chosen file/uploader + computed offset keyed per show (and season, since a new season may reasonably use a different uploader); auto-apply on any new episode of an already-configured show, no re-prompt; keep today's per-episode manual override available for the rare episode needing a different offset, without overwriting the show-level default; viewable/clearable.

**2026-07-04 — Manual subtitle file upload fallback (.srt/.ass) scheduled into its own Phase 4 priority slot, gating the start of Phase 5.** Caught as a real scheduling gap: it's committed must-have scope (Section 4) that had never actually been assigned to a phase. Chosen over bundling it into general Phase 4 work or pushing it to Phase 6 — the user's call was that it should ship on its own, before Anki export work begins, rather than compete for priority with either the current bug-fix backlog or the later UX-polish phase. No new parsing work needed — `subtitle-parser.js` already handles `.srt`/`.ass` from Phase 2; this is the user-facing upload flow only.

**2026-07-04 — Sentence-only capture reconsidered and kept deferred, with the reasoning corrected.** The original "speculative demand, no confirmed evidence" framing was too dismissive — reviewing it again surfaced a real use case (a sentence with no confusing vocabulary can still be worth saving for other reasons), genuinely distinct from what target-word capture covers. Rejected building it now anyway: it needs its own capture paradigm (a line-level trigger, and a design decision for what fills the word/reading/gloss fields with no target word), not a toggle on the existing flow, and the user's own expectation is it will come up less often than ordinary target-word capture. Real post-Phase-5 usage of the word-click flow is a better signal than guessing now.

**2026-07-04 — Kanji-heteronym disambiguation reverified before accepting it permanently; reasoning refined, conclusion unchanged.** The original 2026-07-03 entry correctly ruled out one approach (anchoring to kuromoji's own reading signal — confirmed unreliable) but its "logged as an accepted limitation" framing understated that a second approach was never actually evaluated: a small hardcoded lookup per heteronym pair (e.g. "一行 preceded by 勇者/私たち-type words → いっこう, otherwise いちぎょう"), which is technically buildable without AI or a backend. Re-evaluated and still rejected — not because it's impossible, but because it doesn't scale: every ambiguous heteronym pair would need its own hand-curated collocate list, maintained indefinitely as new cases surface, the same whack-a-mole pattern already rejected once for kana-merge (hardcoded broken-word list, superseded by the general `findKanaMergeCandidates` mechanism). Only one confirmed real occurrence of this problem exists so far (一行), which doesn't justify that maintenance burden. Accepted as a permanent limitation on scope/maintenance grounds, not technical impossibility.

**2026-07-04 — この先 dual-view primacy severity assessed as low before accepting it permanently.** Checked for other reported instances of this exact pattern (demonstrative + noun competing with a lexicalized idiom) — none exist in testing so far; a hypothetical parallel (そのうち, "eventually" vs その+うち compositionally) illustrates the same shape but hasn't actually been encountered. Severity judged low because dual-view doesn't hide either reading: the individual-word definitions (この/先) and the "Also, as a set phrase" note are both displayed together with no extra click required to see the second one, unlike the multi-reading bug (wrong furigana shown with no indication anything's off) or duplicate-gloss noise (two contradictory-looking cards). Worst case is a moment of the first reading not quite fitting, immediately resolved by the adjacent note — accepted as-is on that basis, not left unexamined.

**2026-07-05 — Offset-reset hotkey fixed via `event.code`, not `event.key`.** ⌥+0 produced "º" (a macOS Alt-key character remap), not "0", so the existing `event.key === "0"` check never matched on Mac. Rejected trying to enumerate every possible Alt-shifted character across keyboard layouts — `event.code` reports the physical key regardless of what character the modifier produces, and needed no equivalent fix for the arrow-key hotkeys (unaffected by Alt on Mac).

**2026-07-05 — Phrase-matcher's "already correctly grouped" filter generalized to boundary-alignment, not just exact-span-match, rejection.** Found while investigating a QA report claiming してい (交換していた) and いいん (いいんだろう) were being wrongly "fused" — turned out both were dual-view attachments cutting across an existing baseline group's boundary (して|いた; ん+だろ+う), not full fuses. Rejected patching these two cases individually (a name-specific exception each) in favor of one general rule: any phrase-match candidate must start and end exactly on a baseline-group edge, never in the middle of one. Confirmed this doesn't just fix the two reported cases — it removed 60 other coincidental fuse matches across the corpus (397 → 337) that were the same class of bug, undetected until this generalization.

**2026-07-05 — TUBELEX scoring gated on POS-category compatibility, layered on top of (not replacing) the existing own-reading-match check.** Found while investigating why 子's ね-reading entry (zodiac sign) outranked こ ("child"): TUBELEX's own "ね" lemma row aggregates essentially all real-world ね usage under the sentence-final particle sense, since the earlier same-day-2026-07-03 contamination fix only guards against a DIFFERENT reading sharing a key (そうか/そうけ) — it doesn't catch the SAME reading being legitimately used by two completely different grammatical categories. Chosen over a stricter reading-only gate (already tried once this project, and already confirmed to collapse coverage from 59,703 to 9,450 entries) — a coarse POS-category cross-check (JMdict code vs. TUBELEX's own UniDic POS column) catches this specific contamination shape without that same collapse.

**2026-07-05 — `jmdict-compact.json`'s 子/ね-vs-こ tie left unresolved rather than force-fixed, after the POS-gate fix revealed a deeper issue.** The POS-gate correctly stopped ね from inheriting the particle's frequency, but ね and こ then tie on every remaining scored criterion, and the tiebreak (array position) reflects a stale order baked in by an earlier, now-fixed contamination bug — not true original JMdict order (raw jmdict-eng confirmed lists こ first). A clean fix needs a full pipeline rebuild from the raw release, but the original compact-generation script (2026-06-24) was never saved to this repo, and reconstructing it from a written description risks introducing new, silent format drift across all 217k entries. Flagged to the user rather than attempted solo, given the blast radius. Logged as Ongoing (Section 8), not force-resolved with a one-off hardcoded reorder (which would repeat the exact whack-a-mole pattern rejected elsewhere). (Resolved later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-05 — お's interjection-sense ranking left unresolved; NOT the same root cause as 子's, despite initially assuming otherwise.** Investigation showed お has zero TUBELEX data at all (no contamination to remove), so its correct "oh!" interjection sense (kp-tagged but not marked `common`) simply loses to お's other common-flagged senses (tail, cord, honorific prefix) on the existing common-flag-first tiebreak, regardless of the POS-gate fix. Left open rather than silently claiming it was fixed by the bundled work — would need either sourcing frequency data TUBELEX doesn't have, or reconsidering the common-flag-priority tiebreak itself (which exists for an unrelated, still-valid reason — the そうか protection). (Accepted as a permanent limitation later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-05 — 入る (いる/はいる) and まま (まんま/にゅうぼ) accepted as permanent limitations, same grounds as the kanji-heteronym precedent.** For 入る: TUBELEX's lemma field doesn't distinguish the two readings at all (both collapse to the shared kanji lemma with an identical count), so there's no data-driven signal left to prefer はいる, and hardcoding "はいる wins" would be the same whack-a-mole pattern already rejected for kana-merge and heteronym disambiguation. For まま: all four homograph entries are genuinely real (not index corruption), and none has any TUBELEX frequency at all, leaving nothing but accidental array position to sort them.

**2026-07-05 — Numerals/numbers not clickable deferred, not built.** Confirmed two separate gaps, not one: `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` doesn't include digit characters at all, and even if it did, JMdict has no entries for bare numeral glyphs — a real fix needs numeral-to-reading pronunciation logic (1→いち, irregular counters like 一人→ひとり), a small new feature rather than a bug fix. Scoped but not prioritized (Section 8). (Superseded later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-05 — 輝ける (godan potential-form) reverse-conjugation fallback built as a lookupWord-only fallback, gated to fire after a direct lookup miss only.** kuromoji provides zero conjugation signal for this case (unlike every other inflection fixed so far, which all lean on a kuromoji-provided tag) — the fallback instead reverse-derives the plain godan form from the word's own spelling (e-row ending → u-row) and re-looks that up, filtered to godan-tagged (`v5*`) JMdict entries. Restricting it to fire only on an existing lookup failure was deliberate, not incidental — it bounds the false-positive risk the user flagged (a word ending in e-row kana that isn't actually potential form) to cases where kuromoji already failed to recognize the word as anything AND the reverse-derived form happens to be a real godan verb, since any real word with its own headword (食べる, 見える, etc.) never reaches this fallback at all. Validated via a new batch-test.js Pattern 9 across the full 3-show corpus before trusting it, per explicit request.

**2026-07-05 — Potential-form fallback refined to prefer an irregular-verb match over a godan one.** 来れる (ら抜き potential of the irregular verb 来る/くる) reverse-derived to the same shared kanji key as きたる, an unrelated godan verb ("next, forthcoming") also spelled 来る — the original v5-only filter picked きたる's wrong sense, caught by corpus validation. Fixed by preferring an irregular-verb match when one exists under the derived key, since ら抜き applies to 来る specifically as well as to ordinary godan verbs.
