Re-validated: 8/8 real corpus resolutions confirmed correct after the fix (previously 7/8).

**2026-07-05 — おぉ fixed via a small-vowel-elongation lookup fallback, applied only at the JMdict-lookup layer, never to displayed subtitle text.** Confirmed against the raw jmdict-eng release that the real "oh!" interjection entry only lists おお/おう/おー/オー as readings, never おぉ — a real, common expressive-writing convention (a small vowel written after its own large-vowel counterpart for emphasis) that JMdict's own reading list simply doesn't enumerate, the same shape of gap as half-width katakana. Deliberately NOT applied to the displayed subtitle text (unlike the half-width katakana fix, which normalizes both layers) — the elongated spelling is a deliberate stylistic choice by the subtitle author, not an encoding artifact, and should stay visible as written.

**2026-07-05 — チヤホヤ (D21) diagnosis corrected mid-session after real-corpus verification contradicted the initial fix.** First attempt assumed the QA report's "チヤ...ホヤ split by a mid-word ellipsis" meant a literal ellipsis character embedded in the subtitle text, and built a katakana-run-plus-ellipsis merge mechanism on that premise — corpus-validated it, got zero hits, and traced the real subtitle line (fetched fresh from Jimaku) to find no ellipsis at all: チヤホヤされるんだ！, no punctuation involved. The actual bug was groupTokens' existing katakana-proper-noun rule silencing the whole run because kuromoji tags チヤ alone as 固有名詞, never checking whether the full run is a real word. Discarded the ellipsis-based mechanism entirely (not left in as unused dead code) and rebuilt as `findKatakanaUnsuppressCandidates`/`applyKatakanaUnsuppress` instead — see Section 5. Kept as a decisions-log entry specifically because it's a real example of why this project's "validate against the actual corpus before trusting a fix" rule exists, not just a quiet correction.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — `jmdict-compact.json` rebuilt from scratch, over accepting the 子 (ね-vs-こ) tie as a permanent limitation.** The user's call, reasoning that a stale, contamination-era array-position tiebreak likely affects more ties than just 子, not just this one case. Rejected patching the tiebreak logic itself (e.g. re-deriving "true" position from some other signal) in favor of a full pipeline rebuild from the raw release, since the array position's only claim to meaning is that it traces back to JMdict's own curatorial order — patching around a known-corrupted position would just be guessing at a fix for the symptom. Mitigated the "full-file rebuild is risky" concern by reverse-engineering the exact algorithm and validating it statistically against the current file (99.96%+ match) before trusting it, rather than working from the written spec alone — see Section 5 for the validation numbers.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — `jmdict-compact.json`'s duplicate-gloss detection uses exact `g[0]` string match, not a fuzzier overlap comparison.** The "TBD" comparison rule from the 2026-07-04 design entry is resolved: checked against the raw `jmdict-eng` release for the actual cases in question (酒/君/私) rather than assuming a fuzzy match was needed — every real case shares an exactly identical first gloss string, so a simple, cheap equality check is sufficient. Rejected building fuzzy/overlap matching pre-emptively, since no real case examined so far needs it — matches this project's general "don't build for a hypothetical gap" pattern.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — お's interjection-sense ranking accepted as a permanent limitation, same grounds as 入る/まま.** Confirmed a genuinely different root cause from 子 despite the surface-level similarity (a correct colloquial sense losing to rarer common-flagged senses): お has zero TUBELEX data at all, so there's no contamination to remove and no frequency signal to add. Fixing it would require either sourcing frequency data that doesn't exist, or weakening the common-flag-priority tiebreak that exists for an unrelated, still-valid reason (protects そうか's correct sense from TUBELEX's own coverage gaps). Not worth either cost for one word.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — Kanji-containing proper-noun popups un-suppressed by simply removing the special-case silent-close, not by adding new logic.** Confirmed that katakana-only invented names (ヒンメル, アイゼン) never reach this code path at all — they're already fully inert (no click handler) via groupTokens' separate katakana-run rule, upstream of this check. This meant the existing `isProperNoun` silent-close in `onWordClick` was, in practice, only ever suppressing kanji names — so the 2026-07-04 decision was implemented by deleting that branch entirely (and the now-dead `isProperNoun` plumbing through content.js) rather than adding a kanji-vs-katakana distinction that would have duplicated a check already made upstream.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — Auto-offset-detection on Jimaku import scheduled for Phase 6.** Closes the last open Jimaku-import UX gap vs. ManabiDojo (Section 3.3) — per-season memory and the title-search mismatch were already resolved 2026-07-04. Scoped to align subtitle offset automatically against Crunchyroll's own native subtitle track timing, replacing the first-watch manual nudge.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — Numerals/numbers made clickable, scheduled into Phase 4** (supersedes the same-day "deferred, not built" call above). Needs two independent pieces: extend `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` to match digit characters, and add numeral-to-reading pronunciation logic (1→いち, irregular counters like 一人→ひとり), since JMdict has no entries for bare numeral glyphs. Scoped as a small new feature, not a grouping tweak. **Built same session, approach refined:** implemented as a dedicated `NUMERAL_ONLY_RE` check inside `groupTokens`, ahead of the `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` non-match branch, rather than literally extending `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` itself — avoids leaking numeral tokens into unrelated logic keyed on that regex (phrase-matching windows, kana-merge, katakana-run detection). 一人-style kanji counters needed no new work at all: they already resolve correctly today via existing kanji-token JMdict lookups and the general phrase-matcher's noun+noun fuse — confirmed directly (一人 → ひとり already worked pre-session). Full technical detail in Section 5 (Grouping/merge pipeline).

**2026-07-05 (later session) — Fansub-provider markup: meaningless symbols stripped globally, real punctuation left alone.** ➡ (continuation arrows), 》(bracket markers), 📺 (TV-dialogue emoji), and 🎵〜 (music-note marker) carry no linguistic content in any provider's convention, so they're stripped with one global filter — same treatment as the existing `STAGE_RE` stage-direction filter, no per-provider logic needed. 「」(quote brackets) are left untouched: real Japanese orthographic punctuation, not a fansub artifact.

**2026-07-05 (later session) — Colloquial-contraction handling (どした→どす, etc.): attempt a general mechanism before accepting as a limitation.** Rejected hardcoding case-by-case — the same whack-a-mole pattern already rejected for kana-merge and kanji-heteronym disambiguation. Decided to first investigate whether a general reverse-expansion signal exists — e.g. mirroring the godan-potential-form reverse-conjugation fallback: detect a plausible dropped-vowel/mora contraction and re-look-up the expanded form — before building anything case-specific. If no reliable general signal turns up, falls back to accepting this as a permanent limitation rather than hardcoding individual cases. **Investigated same session; accepted as a permanent limitation.** Root-caused first: どした tokenizes as どし (kuromoji's own IPADIC entry for どす, a real but rare Kyoto-ben copula, 連用形) + た, not a segmentation artifact — どす's only JMdict entry ("dagger") is what actually surfaces, a genuine homograph collision, not a missing-word gap. Checked whether the godan-potential-form precedent actually generalizes here: it doesn't. That fallback reverse-derives a FIXED-position kana substitution (e-row → u-row, no ambiguity about where); どした→どうした instead requires INSERTING an entire mora with no anchor for where to insert it, a fundamentally more speculative transformation. Confirmed the pattern doesn't even recur consistently across superficially similar cases, which would be needed to trust a shared rule: どうやって contracted (どやって) tokenizes completely differently (ど surfaces as its own 接頭詞 token, never fused into the following verb the way どし fuses with た), and そした/こした/ああした each already have their OWN unrelated real JMdict headwords, so a general adverb+する contraction-reversal rule would risk overriding real, unrelated words for no benefit in those cases. No reliable general signal exists — permanent limitation, not built. (A narrower fix — loosening background.js's POS-category filter to allow hiding 100% of results when kuromoji's tag and JMdict's POS codes fully mismatch — was considered and set aside as out of scope: it's a different, broader change to an existing safety net that protects unrelated words elsewhere, not a "contraction detection mechanism.")

**2026-07-05 (later session) — 後藤-style multi-kanji name fusion designed, scheduled into Phase 4.** Reuses the same existence-check pattern already used for katakana-name un-suppression and kana-merge: fuse a multi-kanji-token proper-noun span into one lookup unit only if the full span has a real JMdict entry (e.g. 胡桃-style); otherwise leave normal tokenization splitting it. Reuses an already-trusted general mechanism, not case-specific. **Built same session; approach corrected after corpus testing.** 後藤 itself was never actually reproducible as a split — it tokenizes as one kuromoji token in every context tested; the real bug (confirmed via 伊地知虹夏, a real corpus line) is a given-name half that ISN'T in IPADIC's proper-noun list falling through as unrelated ordinary-word kanji (虹="rainbow", 夏="summer"). Critically, that word has NO JMdict entry either — so an unconditional "fuse only if the whole span exists" rule, as originally scoped, would not have fixed the reported problem at all. Rebuilt instead to always fuse a detected name run into one lookup unit regardless of existence (existence only decides what's DISPLAYED, not whether to fuse) — full reasoning and the length-gated false-positive guard (which correctly keeps 下北沢高校 split into its two real, correctly-defined parts, confirmed via corpus testing) in Section 5.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — New Phase 4.5 ("Jimaku integration") created between Phase 4 and Phase 5.** Absorbs per-season offset/uploader memory and the TMDB/AniList title-resolution decision out of Phase 6. Rejected keeping this work split across Phase 4 (auto-detection) and Phase 6 (memory + title-matching, as originally scheduled 2026-07-04) — investigation showed these are the same underlying feature (reliable show/episode identification feeding a ranked subtitle-selection and memory system), not separable concerns that happen to both touch Jimaku. Anki export (Phase 5) is pushed later in the overall build order to accommodate this — an intentional tradeoff, not an oversight: Jimaku search/selection is core-loop must-have scope (Section 4), not differentiation-layer polish, so it takes priority over Phase 5's place in the sequence.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — Show/episode auto-detection from the Crunchyroll page must be built and validated as its own standalone step before any other Phase 4.5 work begins.** Every later step in the phase (ID-based Jimaku matching, ranked subtitle selection, auto-select, the switcher panel, per-show-per-season memory) depends on reliably knowing what show/episode is currently playing with zero user input — building any of those first would mean building on an unverified foundation. This is also why TMDB/AniList ID resolution (Section 5, Phase 4.5) is now a hard dependency rather than a Phase 6 nice-to-have: accurate matching against Jimaku's catalogue depends on a reliably auto-detected show/episode existing first.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — "Best subtitle" ranking prefers a real Jimaku popularity signal, hardcoded list as fallback only.** Two alternative fallback designs considered and rejected: (a) ranking by an uploader's episode-completeness across the season — rejected because most active fansub groups fully sub a season anyway, so this ties constantly and doesn't discriminate; (b) recency (most-recently-uploaded file) as a tiebreak — rejected because trusted, reliable groups often upload LATER than obscure ones (more time spent on QC), so recency would actively favor the wrong provider, not the right one. Regardless of which ranking method ends up used, the candidate list is always hard-filtered to files matching the exact show + exact episode number, never a broader per-show list. Whether the real signal actually exists is unconfirmed — see Open Questions.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — Per-show-per-season subtitle-uploader preference reuses the existing per-episode offset-memory's show+season scoping, rather than inventing new scoping logic.** Rejected a global "preferred uploader across all anime" scope — different shows are subbed by different groups with different reliability, so one global preference wouldn't transfer between shows. Rejected a per-episode-only scope (re-prompting every episode) — defeats the purpose of "no re-prompt" memory the offset-memory feature already established, and a season's uploader choice is stable in practice. One scoping design, reused across both features, not two separate ones to keep in sync.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — Saved per-show-per-season uploader preference is sticky: a per-episode fallback never clears or overwrites it.** When the saved preferred uploader has no file for a given episode, the system falls through silently to the "best"-ranked option for that one episode only; the saved preference stays intact for every other episode of that show+season. Rejected clearing or overwriting the saved preference on a single missing-episode gap — a temporary gap (an uploader skipping one episode) shouldn't discard an otherwise-correct standing preference the user explicitly set.

**2026-07-05 (planning session) — Phase 4.5's UI (auto-select + manual switcher panel) ships rough/functional only; visual polish deferred to Phase 6.** Matches this project's established build-order pattern of function before polish. Polish work is bundled with the dictionary-popup UI/UX redesign already scheduled for Phase 6, rather than polishing Phase 4.5's UI in isolation now and revisiting it again later.

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing session) — Kanji spellings on homograph popup cards approved, conditional on a data-retention check.** When a kana-only lookup resolves to multiple entries (いる → 居る/入る/要る), each card currently shows an identical kana header with no way to tell them apart at a glance — approved fix: show each card's own kanji spelling next to its header instead. Building this is gated on confirming `jmdict-compact.json` still retains the kanji spelling(s) for an entry reached via a kana-only index key — low-cost if the raw data survives generation, a small regen if not (see Open Questions). (Checked and built later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing session) — それもそうか-style multi-word idiomatic dual-view is new scope, conditional on JMdict data.** Scope: multi-word colloquial combinations where each word is independently a real, correct JMdict entry, but the combination means something the sum doesn't capture (それもそうか and 2-3 similar combos). Before building anything: check the raw JMdict release for whether these combinations actually have their own headword. If yes, extend the existing existence-gated phrase-matcher to catch multi-word exact-phrase spans (same pattern as the existing single-word-plus-particle dual-view), sourcing the idiomatic gloss from that real entry. If no, do not write a project-authored note explaining the idiom — leave as an accepted limitation, same precedent as the already-accepted kanji-heteronym/入る/まま cases, rather than introducing content JMdict itself doesn't source. (Checked later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing session) — Numbered gloss display approved; gloss-per-sense cap bumped 2→3, sense cap (3) unchanged.** Senses will display with visible numbering (1., 2., 3.) instead of running together unnumbered, matching a ManabiDojo-style reference layout. The sense cap stays tied to the existing open question ("does the 3-cap lose meanings that matter") — revisit only on real evidence from testing, not closed as permanent by this decision. The gloss-per-sense bump and the numbering itself are both settled, not conditional — unlike the kanji-spellings and それもそうか items above, this one needs no fact-finding check, only the data-model change already noted in Section 5 (Dictionary-data precision) to preserve sense boundaries in `jmdict-compact.json`, which the current flattened `g` array doesn't carry. (Built later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing session) — Karaoke-style word highlighting declined, not deferred.** No per-word timing data exists in Jimaku/.srt/.ass or in Crunchyroll's own audio; real sync would need audio-text alignment (a nontrivial ML problem, cutting against the no-AI/no-backend architectural stance), and an even-timing approximation would drift out of sync almost immediately on real dialogue pacing. Same shape of rejection as the mono-ruby naive-heuristic call (Section 5, Phase 6) — a naive approximation would actively mislead rather than help, so it's declined outright rather than left as a deferred nice-to-have.

**2026-07-06 (later session) — jmdict-compact.json's kanji-spelling fact-finding check resolved "no," and `k` field built in the same regen pass as the gloss-cap/sense-grouping change.** Direct inspection confirmed the compact format was discarding kanji spellings entirely (raw release has them: 射る/炒る・煎る・熬る/入る for いる's first three senses), so kanji-spellings-on-homograph-cards needed a regen, not a pure display change. Combined into one regeneration alongside the already-approved gloss restructuring (below) rather than regenerating twice for two related jmdict-compact.json changes in the same session. Validated against the pre-regen file across all 462,753 index keys: 100% match on every non-gloss field. See Section 5 (Dictionary-data precision) for the full validation numbers.

**2026-07-06 (later session) — Numbered-gloss display and the gloss-cap bump built via a `g` restructuring (flat array → array-of-arrays grouped by sense), not just a wider flat cap.** A flat array with no sense boundaries can't be numbered by sense at all — confirmed by direct inspection (考える's old entry was one 6-item flat array spanning 3 senses with no marker between them). Two existing consumers assumed a flat array with `g[0]` as the first gloss STRING (content.js's duplicate-gloss check, `fix-jmdict-priority.js`'s fingerprint key) — both updated to `g[0]?.[0]`; `fix-jmdict-priority.js`'s re-run confirmed 0 of 217,674 entries lost their raw-release match after the fix, so the fingerprint change is safe. POS chips were kept as one line per entry rather than repeated per numbered sense (unlike the ManabiDojo-style reference screenshot) — `jmdict-compact.json`'s `p` field is still a whole-entry union, and per-sense POS wasn't part of the approved scope; revisit only if asked for specifically, not inferred from the reference image's exact layout.

**2026-07-06 (later session) — それもそうか-style multi-word dual-view needed no new code: the existing general phrase-matcher already handles it.** Fact-finding against the raw JMdict release found それもそうか itself has no headword, but several close variants do (それもそうだ, そういえば, そうだね, そうだよね, そっか). Rather than assuming the existing mechanism needed extending (as the original decision anticipated), tested it directly first: all of the real variants already fuse correctly through `findPhraseMatchCandidates`/`classifyAndSelectPhraseMatches` with zero changes. それもそうか itself, lacking a headword, correctly falls through to a two-part fuse (それも + そうか) instead — both independently real, correctly-defined entries, not a misleading split. Confirmed a real mechanism already covers the general case; nothing to build, matching this project's "verify before assuming a gap exists" practice.

**2026-07-06 (later session) — Manual subtitle upload fallback built: `subtitle-parser.js` added as a content script rather than routed through a background.js message.** `content.js` can call `parseSrt`/`parseAss` directly after reading the local file via `FileReader`, avoiding a `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` round-trip and the serialization concerns of passing a `File` object through it — the same sharing pattern already used for `tokenize-utils.js`. `init()`'s cue-rendering `timeupdate` listener was refactored to attach once, unconditionally, reading from the shared `cues` variable, so a Jimaku fetch and a manual upload can both populate it without each needing its own listener (previously only the Jimaku-fetch path attached one at all). Upload also dispatches a synthetic `timeupdate` event immediately after a successful parse, so a paused video reflects the new file without waiting for the next real timeupdate — the pre-existing Jimaku-fetch path doesn't get this same forced-refresh treatment, left as an existing, unrelated minor quirk rather than expanding this task's scope to fix it too.

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing, kanji-spelling round) — Kanji/kana-spelling filtering by rK/sK/oK-equivalent tags approved, needs building.** Filter displayed kanji spellings (`entry.k`) and alternate kana readings (`entry.rs`) to exclude any form tagged rK/sK/oK (rare/outdated/old kanji) or the reading-level equivalent — same tag family already used for the いる/居る kanji-rarity ordering fix (Section 5, Phase 4). Fixes two confirmed bugs: 来る showing the obsolete kyūjitai form 來る (never used in modern subtitles), and じゃない showing ぢゃ無い as a false alternate spelling (ぢ is outdated kana usage outside specific rendaku compounds). Show only the standard modern spelling(s)/reading(s) next to each entry header. Not yet built — needs a further `jmdict-compact.json` data-model change first (per-form tags for `k`/`rs`, which are currently plain string arrays with no way to know which specific form is rare) — see Section 5 (Popup content & display). (Built later this session, scope narrowed after implementation — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing, kanji-spelling round) — Verb-type-implied archaism (Yodan/Nidan, etc.) approved as a new tagging signal for the duplicate-gloss mechanism, needs building.** Extends `archaicTagLabel()` (Section 5, Phase 4 — Duplicate-gloss homograph entries fixed 2026-07-05) to also treat pre-modern conjugation classes as an implicit `arch` signal, equivalent to an explicit misc `arch` tag — specifically `arch`, not `dated`/`obs`, since the verb-type signal only carries "pre-modern grammar." Fixes こかす/呉る-style entries currently showing "(archaic)" in the verb-type POS label with no corresponding badge/demotion applied anywhere else on the card. Don't double-tag if an entry already carries its own explicit misc tag — one tag shown, sourced from whichever signal fired first (explicit tag takes priority; verb-type is the fallback). (Built later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (Frieren live-testing, kanji-spelling round) — そうか showing unrelated nouns (層化, 装花) confirmed as a real bug, needs fix.** Root-caused: `そうか` is always reached via the phrase-matcher's existence-gated fuse mechanism (kuromoji tokenizes it そう+か, never as one natural token, confirmed with and without preceding context), and fuse-outcome groups never carry a `pos` field — so background.js's existing kuromoji-POS-to-JMdict-POS-category filter (`POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS`, built 2026-07-01 for exactly this class of cross-category collision, こと's particle-vs-noun senses) silently never activates for ANY phrase-fused word, not a そうか-specific gap. Very likely a pre-existing issue the kanji-spelling display (built earlier the same day) merely exposed, rather than introduced — the same up-to-3-entries cap was probably already surfacing 層化/装花 before, just invisible while every card showed an identical kana header. Fix by reusing the existing filter, not designing new filtering logic — exact mechanism (what POS signal to derive/pass for a fused span covering tokens of different POS) still needs deciding. See Section 5 (Popup content & display). (Built later this session — see below.)

**2026-07-06 (later session) — そうか's phrase-fuse POS-filter gap fixed via a sentinel `pos: "__phraseFuse"`, not by deriving a real kuromoji category from constituent tokens.** Considered deriving `pos` from one of the fused span's own raw tokens (e.g. そう=副詞) instead, but confirmed this wouldn't actually work: そうか's real entry is JMdict-tagged `exp`, and none of `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS`' existing categories (動詞/形容詞/名詞/副詞/etc.) match `exp` at all — passing pos="副詞" would filter to `adv`-tagged entries and wrongly exclude the real "is that so?" sense along with the noun homographs, triggering the "never narrow to zero" safety net and silently reverting to the original bug. Added one new sentinel category instead, mapped to `exp`/`int` (both real JMdict categories a fused colloquial phrase plausibly belongs to), reusing 100% of the existing plumbing (`content.js`'s `pos` threading, `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS`, the safety net) — genuinely "inheriting the existing filter," not new filtering logic, just one more entry in an existing map. Precisely corpus-validated (not just spot-checked): 11 of 181 distinct real fuse-outcome words get narrowed, all 11 correctly (real interjection/expression sense kept, unrelated homograph nouns dropped); the other 170 are untouched by the new sentinel (either not phrase-fused via this path, or none of their entries are exp/int-tagged, so the safety net leaves them as before).

**2026-07-06 (later session) — Verb-type-implied archaism built exactly as approved, extended to iK verification.** `isArchaicVerbType()` checks any `p` code starting with `v4`/`v2`, as a fallback after the explicit `m`/`ki` checks. Confirmed by inspecting the FULL `posTags` map (not a couple of samples) that literally every `v4*`/`v2*` code carries "(archaic)" in its own JMdict-sourced label — a safe, complete signal, not a guess. Real repro found and validated: 捧ぐ (ささぐ, `v2g-s`) has no `m` tag at all and previously silently demoted despite its own POS chip already reading "archaic"; now correctly tagged.

**2026-07-06 (live re-test, same day) — Verb-type-implied archaism follow-up: `archaicTagLabel()` now checked unconditionally, not just for entries already flagged `isDuplicate`.** Live re-testing caught that the just-shipped fix still didn't show a badge for 呉る/こかす, the very entries it was built for — root-caused to a scoping bug, not the archaic-detection logic itself: `renderEntries` only ever CALLED `archaicTagLabel()` when an entry shared its first gloss with an earlier card in the same popup (`isDuplicate`), and neither 呉る nor こかす happens to share a first gloss with anything else shown alongside it (来る/繰る; 倒す), so the check silently never ran. Rejected leaving the gate as-is and trying to special-case these two words — the real fix is structural: an archaic/dated/obsolete signal is worth surfacing on any card, not only ones that happen to collide with a sibling's first gloss. Demotion itself stays duplicate-gated (an ordinary non-duplicate entry with no archaic signal still renders as a normal, undemoted card) — only the TAG check moved outside that gate.

**2026-07-06 (live re-test, same day, second follow-up) — `m`/`ki` changed from whole-entry unions to primary-sense/primary-reading-only.** Making `archaicTagLabel()` unconditional (the fix above) immediately exposed a second, deeper bug: こと showed "archaic" despite its primary sense being an ordinary common word, because `m` unioned misc tags across all 3 included senses, and こと's SECOND sense ("stringed instrument") happens to be `arch`-tagged even though its first ("koto zither") isn't; 君 showed "obsolete" the same way, from its THIRD sense ("he/she"), not its primary "you/buddy" sense; じゃない showed "dated reading" despite its own reading carrying no tags at all, because `ki` unioned tags across every reading and picked up `ik` from the alternate ぢゃない reading, never actually displayed. Rejected re-adding the `isDuplicate` gate as a workaround — that would just resurrect the original 呉る/こかす bug this same day already fixed. Instead regenerated `jmdict-compact.json` so `m`/`ki` reflect only the primary sense/reading (matching what a duplicate-check and the headword itself already treat as the card's real identity) — the correct fix once the tag check applies to every card, not a narrower patch. Re-validated all three reported cases and the original 2026-07-05 archaic-tagging precedent (あたし/わらわ/酒) against the regenerated file; `scripts/batch-test.js` unchanged.

**2026-07-06 (later session) — Kanji-spelling filtering by rK/sK/oK/iK tags built as a generation-time filter on `k` itself, not a per-form-tag data-model addition.** The original approval anticipated needing per-form tags (since `k`/`rs` are plain string arrays with no way to know which specific form is rare) — but `k` only ever needs to carry the spellings worth SHOWING, so filtering at generation time (excluding rare forms from `k` before it's even written) is sufficient and simpler; no new field needed. `rs` was reconsidered and deliberately left untouched: it's never actually displayed anywhere (confirmed via a full `content.js` grep), so there's no current display bug to fix on the reading side, and filtering it would break its actual job (resolving to whichever reading the user really clicked, e.g. くる → 刳る's えぐる) — directly contradicting this same session's own "show what was actually on screen" confirmation (see above). **A real bug was caught and fixed mid-implementation:** the first version fell back to showing the full unfiltered kanji list whenever every kanji form was tagged rare, on the assumption that a kanji-only word should never end up with zero spellings — real validation against じゃない (whose two kanji forms are BOTH sK) showed this reasoning was inverted: an entry where every kanji form is rare IS the "conventionally kana-only" signal, and the fallback was silently reintroducing the exact bug being fixed (ぢゃ無い). Removed the fallback entirely. Confirmed a desirable side effect, not scope creep: いる's own "to be" sense (居る, already known rK from the pre-existing sense-ordering fix) also correctly loses its kanji badge now, via the same signal doing a second job.

**2026-07-06 — Third live-testing show swapped from One Piece to Naruto, `scripts/batch-test.js`'s automated corpus left unchanged.** User's call: both are shounen, similar dialogue register/pacing, but personal familiarity with Naruto specifically (vs. One Piece) makes it easier to catch subtle wrong-definition/mistranslation bugs during manual testing, since the tester already knows what a line "should" mean. Scoped narrowly to the manual live-testing rotation (`content.js`'s hardcoded `SHOW_QUERY`/`EPISODE`) — the separate, already-validated 3-show automated corpus in `scripts/batch-test.js` (Bocchi/Frieren/One Piece, chosen for uploader/register diversity, not personal familiarity) is a different mechanism serving a different purpose and doesn't need to match the manual-testing show.

**2026-07-06 — Archive-file filtering ported into `background.js`'s `fetchSubtitles`, a real production gap `scripts/batch-test.js` never shared.** Discovered while switching the live-testing show to Naruto: episode 1's Jimaku file list has a `.zip` archive as its very first entry, which the unfiltered `files[0]` pick would have fetched and failed to parse. `scripts/batch-test.js` already had this exact `ARCHIVE_RE` filter from Phase 2, but it was apparently never ported to the actual extension code — a real, previously-undiscovered latent bug (not something introduced today), likely to affect other shows too whenever Jimaku's first listed file for an episode happens to be a bulk archive rather than a single-episode subtitle file. Fixed by reusing the identical filter/regex in production, throwing a clear error directing to the manual upload fallback in the case where literally every file for an episode is an archive.

**2026-07-06 — Live-testing show swapped a second time, from the original Naruto series to Naruto: Shippuuden.** User's call, same session. Not logged as a separate reasoning entry — same rationale as the One Piece → Naruto swap above (shounen-style similarity, personal familiarity aiding error detection), just landing on Shippuuden specifically.

**2026-07-06 — `fileHint` manual file-selection override added to `FETCH_SUBTITLES`, scoped as a stopgap, not Phase 4.5's real ranking work.** Setting up Naruto: Shippuuden ep 1 surfaced a worse version of the archive-filtering issue above: this entry's Jimaku files include an entire season's worth of per-season-restart-numbered releases (S07E01, S15E01, etc.) that Jimaku's own metadata tags as "episode 1" across the board, even though only S01E01 is actually episode 1 — the already-fixed first-non-archive pick would have confidently served the WRONG episode with no error at all (unlike the archive case, which at least would have failed to parse). Considered building real automatic episode-number extraction from filenames (matching patterns like `SxxE01`/`第NNN話`/etc. against the requested episode) to solve this generally right now, but rejected it as premature: that's real "best subtitle selection" territory, which Phase 4.5 already scopes deliberately (fact-finding on a popularity signal first, considered rejection of weaker heuristics like completeness/recency, hard-filtered to exact show+episode) — building an ad hoc version now risks a worse, less-validated version of work already planned properly. Added a manual override instead: `content.js` can supply an optional case-insensitive filename substring (`fileHint`), and `fetchSubtitles` prefers a matching file, falling back to the previous behavior when no hint is given or nothing matches — a deliberately narrow, temporary knob for getting a specific known-good file for testing right now, not a step toward the ranking algorithm itself.

**2026-07-12 — Live-testing show swapped a third time, from Naruto: Shippuuden to Witch Hat Atelier.** User's call. Verified before switching: Jimaku has a real entry (id 11793, "Tongari Boushi no Atelier") with usable ep 1 subtitle files, no archive-first-file trap. One real wrinkle found during setup: ep 1's Jimaku files include a dual Chinese+Japanese sub track ("[CHS, JPN]") from the same uploader as the one wanted — `content.js`'s `FILE_HINT` set to `"[JPN]"` to select the Japanese-only cut instead, same stopgap mechanism as the earlier Naruto per-season-numbering fix, not new scope.

**2026-07-13 — 3-sense display cap raised for particle/interjection-primary entries only (to 8), not globally, after data ruled out a global raise.** Real alternative considered and rejected: a small global bump (5 or 6) would have "just barely" cleared the two concrete cases found this round (の needs 6, ただ今 needs 4) without actually fixing the underlying shape of the problem — a direct scan of the raw JMdict release showed content words have an effectively unbounded long tail of legitimate senses (273 common entries exceed 6, up to 27 for 落ちる), so any small global cap would keep clipping real content for common verbs while adding clutter to the popup for words that don't need it. Chose the narrower, data-verified fix instead: only entries whose first glossed sense is tagged `prt`/`int` (806 total) get the raised budget, since that class is a small, closed set of distinct grammatical functions rather than open-ended semantic extension — verified a cap of 8 fully covers 99.5% of that class before committing to the number, not picked arbitrarily.

**2026-07-15 — s_inf display scoped universally, not to prt/int-primary entries as the originating report proposed.** The report (よ popup showing a bare "hey; you" with no indication of its sentence-final-particle usage) proposed reusing `isFunctionWordPrimary()` — already built for the sense-budget fix — to scope s_inf display to particle/interjection entries only, on the assumption that content-word notes are "usually register/dialect info already covered by existing mechanisms." Sampled the raw release across POS categories before building, per the report's own "verify before implementing" instruction, rather than trusting that assumption: it held for the *rate* (prt 62.8%, int 23.3% vs. content-word POS at 1.5-7%) but not for the *content* of what content words' notes actually say — real examples pulled from the sample (らしい's grammatical-construction constraint, あう's 逢う-vs-遭う kanji-nuance split) are exactly the kind of thing a learner would click that word to check, and none of it is covered by the archaic badge or kanji-rarity filtering. Asked the user to confirm before deciding, since it was a real scope deviation from the report's own proposal, not a judgment call within it — user confirmed universal display, with one follow-up check (long-note wrapping) verified against the popup's existing CSS (320px max-width, no max-height/overflow clipping) before building.

**2026-07-15 — TMDB/AniList ID resolution decision (2026-07-04) reversed after real page inspection; plain title matching built instead.** The original decision assumed Crunchyroll's page exposes an external database ID (TMDB/AniList/MAL) that could be handed straight to Jimaku's `anilist_id`/`tmdb_id` search params, avoiding fuzzy title matching entirely. Couldn't verify this by fetching the page directly — Crunchyroll's watch pages sit behind a Cloudflare bot challenge that blocks both `curl` and WebFetch — so had the user paste a DevTools console dump from a real logged-in session instead. That dump found zero external-ID references anywhere in the page's meta tags, JSON-LD, or embedded script state; the assumption the 2026-07-04 decision rested on was simply wrong. Considered building the ID-resolution step anyway via a third-party hop (query TMDB's or AniList's own search API by title to obtain an ID, then query Jimaku by that ID) — rejected: this doesn't eliminate fuzzy title matching, it just relocates it to a different vendor's search endpoint, adding a new API key requirement and failure mode for no clear reliability gain. Instead verified directly against the real Jimaku API (not assumed) whether plain title matching is good enough: querying with the exact title Crunchyroll's schema.org markup exposes ("Witch Hat Atelier") returned exactly one unambiguous match, resolved via Jimaku's own `english_name` field — confirming Jimaku already indexes English titles as aliases alongside JP/romanized names, which is exactly the case the original decision was worried about breaking. Built `detectShowEpisode()` (`content.js`) around Crunchyroll's schema.org `TVEpisode`/`TVSeries` JSON-LD instead — reliable, standardized SEO markup rather than React-internal state that could break on a frontend rebuild — feeding the existing (unchanged) exact-title-match-preferring Jimaku search in `fetchSubtitles`.

**2026-07-15 — Jimaku API confirmed to expose no per-file popularity/usage signal; "best subtitle" ranking falls back to a hardcoded uploader-preference list.** Checked directly against real API responses (`/entries/search`, `/entries/{id}`, `/entries/{id}/files`), not just the documented contract: file objects carry only `url`/`name`/`size`/`last_modified`; entry objects add only `flags` (anime/unverified/external/movie/adult), `anilist_id`, `english_name`, `japanese_name`, `creator_id` — no view/download/favorite count anywhere. This was the fact-finding check the 2026-07-05 ranking-algorithm decision was gated on; with it resolved "no," the fallback (a small, explicitly temporary hardcoded uploader-preference list) is now the only path, not a choice to make later. Side-finding worth flagging for that fallback's design: there's no structured per-file uploader field either — a file's uploader/release-group only exists as a bracket tag baked into its filename (e.g. `[Haruhana]`), same as `FILE_HINT`'s existing filename-substring matching already assumes — so matching against the preference list will need to be filename-substring-based, not a clean field comparison.

**2026-07-15 — Uploader-preference list seeded from this project's own testing history, not researched from general fansub-community reputation.** With the popularity-signal fact-finding resolved "no" (see the entry above), the ranking algorithm needed a concrete starting list. Asked the user directly rather than picking groups myself from outside knowledge (a real product-quality judgment call, not a technical unknown) — user chose to seed from SubsPlease/Haruhana/VCB-Studio (each already confirmed reliable through this project's own live-testing sessions, not just generic reputation) and treat the list as a living document to extend later, rather than spend time researching a more complete list now before any real multi-uploader case has actually been hit.

**2026-07-15 — Crunchyroll confirmed NOT to reload the page between episodes; `detectShowEpisode()`/`FETCH_SUBTITLES` given a re-run trigger on SPA navigation.** The open question logged earlier the same day (does episode-to-episode navigation reload the page?) was answered by direct live testing: clicking to the next episode left the previous episode's subtitles showing, only fixed by a manual page refresh — confirming the once-per-load detection built earlier that day was incomplete. First fix attempt: wrapped `history.pushState`/`replaceState` to dispatch a custom event on any real pathname change (plus a `popstate` listener for back/forward navigation) — the standard technique for content scripts observing SPA routing without requesting the `webNavigation` permission. Extracted the existing detect+fetch block into a reusable `loadSubtitles()` function so the reload path doesn't duplicate `init()`'s one-time DOM/listener setup, just re-runs the data-fetching part. Also fixed a related but separate latent bug found while touching this code: `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY` was computed once at module load from `location.pathname`, so a saved subtitle-timing offset would have silently leaked from one episode's storage key into another's session once SPA navigation was in play at all — changed to recompute per navigation alongside the subtitle reload, not left as a separate follow-up. **This first attempt shipped and then failed a second live test — subtitles still didn't refresh after switching episodes.** See the follow-up entry below for the root cause and the working fix.

**2026-07-15 (same day, follow-up) — `history.pushState`/`replaceState` wrapping doesn't work from a content script's isolated JS world; replaced with polling `location.pathname` directly.** The pushState/replaceState wrapper above looked correct and matched a commonly-cited technique, but failed live testing — a real lesson to log, not just the eventual fix, since the same mistake (assuming a widely-documented technique works without confirming it in THIS specific context — content scripts, not regular page JS) could recur. Root cause: Chrome content scripts execute in an isolated JS world, which gets its own separate `history` object wrapping the shared browsing-context navigation state — reassigning `history.pushState` on the isolated world's `history` object has zero effect on Crunchyroll's own (main-world) code, which continues calling ITS OWN unpatched `history.pushState` reference. Confirmed this is specifically a function-override problem, not a general state-sharing problem: `location.pathname` reads correctly across both worlds (navigation state is genuinely shared), only overriding a BUILT-IN FUNCTION fails to cross the isolated/main-world boundary. Considered injecting a `<script>` tag (or an MV3 `"world": "MAIN"` content script) to patch history from the main world instead, dispatching a DOM CustomEvent the isolated-world listener could pick up (DOM events do cross the boundary) — rejected as more moving parts (a second script file, more manifest surface, still needs the isolated-world listener anyway) for no benefit over the simpler fix: poll `location.pathname` directly every 1s. Polling sidesteps the whole world-isolation question, since it only ever reads state, never overrides a function. Kept the existing `popstate` listener as a supplementary fast path for actual back/forward navigation (a genuine browser-dispatched event, not a JS function call, so it does legitimately cross the world boundary) — polling is now the primary, reliable mechanism, `popstate` just shortcuts the up-to-1s poll delay for that one case.

**2026-07-15 — Phase 4's live re-test formally closed by explicit user decision, not by exhaustive completion.** Three shows and several testing rounds surfaced and fixed a substantial number of real bugs (see Build Order Phase 4 Done list), but the user judged this sufficient to move forward rather than continue an open-ended testing loop, explicitly acknowledging more issues may surface later. Consistent with how this project already treats untested edge cases elsewhere (e.g. forced-subtitle overlay behavior, deprioritized until a real case appears) — a live-testing pass doesn't need to reach a state of "provably no more bugs" before the project moves on, it needs to reach the point of diminishing returns the user is willing to accept.

**2026-07-15 — Manual switcher panel's response data folded into the existing `FETCH_SUBTITLES` message instead of a new `LIST_SUBTITLE_FILES` message.** The panel needs the full ranked candidate list, not just the auto-picked winner `fetchSubtitles` used to return alone. Considered adding a separate message the switcher panel would call independently to fetch the file list — rejected: `fetchSubtitles` already computes the full ranked list internally (via `rankFiles`) before picking its winner, so a separate message would mean querying Jimaku's search+files endpoints TWICE per episode load for identical data, doubling real API calls against Jimaku's 25 req/min budget for no benefit. Instead extended `FETCH_SUBTITLES`'s existing response to include `files` (the full ranked list) and `selectedUrl` (which one got auto-picked) alongside `cues` — one round trip serves both the auto-load path and the switcher panel's initial render. A user's manual pick from the dropdown still goes through its own new `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` message (just `url`/`name`, no search+files-list work needed since the candidate was already resolved).

**2026-07-16 — Uploader preference threaded into the existing ranking pass rather than a separate "auto-load, then swap if a preference exists" flow.** Considered fetching the default-ranked file first (as before) and then, if a saved show+season preference existed and pointed elsewhere, issuing a second `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` call to swap to it — rejected as the naive approach: it would mean an extra download+parse round trip on EVERY episode load for any show with a saved preference, the common case once the feature is used at all, not an edge case. Instead extended `rankFiles()` to accept the preference directly and give it top priority within the same ranking pass background.js was already doing — `fetchSubtitles` downloads whichever file that ranking now puts first, correct on the first try, no swap-after-the-fact needed. This same design choice is also why the "sticky fallback" build-order item needed no separate code: since the preference is just another (highest-priority) entry in the SAME priority list, a preference with no matching file this episode falls through the ranking exactly like an absent one would, with no special-cased "check if it matched, else do X" branch anywhere.

**2026-07-16 — Uploader preference memory only ever stores an extractable uploader TAG, never a specific file URL or the raw filename.** A saved file URL would go stale the moment the episode changed (each episode has its own distinct files); a raw filename wouldn't generalize across episodes either (episode numbers/titles differ file to file). An uploader tag (`[Haruhana]`-style bracket prefix) is the one piece of a filename that stays stable across a show's episodes, so it's the only thing worth persisting. Files with no extractable tag (direct-source Netflix/Amazon rips, confirmed real in Witch Hat Atelier's own ep 1 candidate list) simply don't create a saved preference when picked — considered saving SOMETHING for these (e.g. a source-name heuristic parsed from elsewhere in the filename) but rejected as over-engineering a real but rare case with no clear general pattern to extract, when "no preference persists, pick applies for just this episode" is already the exact behavior that existed before this feature was built at all — not a regression, just a case this feature doesn't (yet) extend to.

**2026-07-17 — Two stacked Jimaku search/selection bugs fixed, both API-confirmed first.** Reported symptom: "No Jimaku entry found for 'Frieren: Beyond Journey's End'" despite the show being on Jimaku. Direct `curl` calls against the live API (not assumed from docs) found two separate causes stacked on top of each other. **Bug 1 — apostrophe mismatch breaks the search entirely:** Crunchyroll's schema.org title uses a straight apostrophe ("Journey's End"), Jimaku's own indexed title uses a typographic one ("Journey's End" vs "Journey’s End") — querying with the straight form returns zero results even though the same query minus the apostrophe word (or with the apostrophe stripped) returns the correct entries. Fixed by stripping apostrophe characters from the query and from both sides of the exact-match comparison, rather than guessing which Unicode apostrophe variant to convert to. **Bug 2 — no season-level entry disambiguation, more serious than the visible error:** Jimaku indexes each season of a multi-season show as a wholly separate entry with its own free-text name (season 1: "Sousou no Frieren" / "Frieren: Beyond Journey's End"; season 2: "Sousou no Frieren 2nd Season" / "...Season 2") — there's no structured season field in Jimaku's API to match against instead (confirmed via the raw response). Crunchyroll's own `partOfSeries.name` is stable across a franchise's seasons (season 2 episodes still report the plain, un-suffixed title), and the existing exact-match logic used seriesTitle only, with no season awareness — so fixing Bug 1 alone would have made things WORSE, not better: the search would start succeeding, but the exact-match/`entries[0]` fallback would land on whichever entry has no season suffix (i.e. season 1's), silently serving season 1's subtitles for a season 2 episode under a matching episode NUMBER, with no error at all. Both bugs fixed together for this reason — shipping the apostrophe fix alone was rejected as net-negative for the exact case being tested. Fix: `detectShowEpisode()`'s already-captured `seasonNumber` (previously computed but unused, see the 2026-07-15 entry above) is now threaded through `FETCH_SUBTITLES` into `resolveTextFiles`, which parses a trailing "Season N"/"Nth Season" marker off each candidate entry's name (defaulting to season 1 when absent) and requires it to match the detected season before accepting an exact title match, falling back to the old season-agnostic exact match only when no season-qualified match exists (preserving current behavior for the common single-entry-per-show case). A heuristic, not a structured lookup, since Jimaku has nothing structured to key on instead — same "seed and refine" shape as the uploader-preference list. Verified against the real API end-to-end with the exact code path (search query, entry selection) and a standalone scenario script covering season 1 / season 2 / season `null` / single-entry-show cases, all correct — not yet re-exercised through the live extension UI. See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) and Build Order.

**2026-07-17 (same live-testing session) — Forced-re-render fix ported to the two paths that were still missing it, rather than adding a THIRD ad hoc dispatch call.** The manual-upload path (`buildUploadControl`) already dispatches a synthetic `timeupdate` event after setting `cues`, with a comment explaining why (a paused video won't otherwise fire one, so the display just sits stale/on its placeholder text). That same reasoning applies identically to `loadSubtitles`'s auto-fetch success path and the switcher panel's manual-pick success path — both were setting `cues` with no equivalent dispatch, confirmed as the cause of a real "sometimes stuck on Loading subtitles, sometimes switches fine" report (the difference was purely whether the video happened to be actively playing at that moment). Fixed both the same way rather than inventing a different mechanism, and threaded `video` through as a new parameter to both functions to reach it — the alternative (module-scope `video` variable) was rejected as an unnecessary global for something only these three call sites need.

**2026-07-17 (same session) — Added a bounded 2-retry (500ms apart) to `loadSubtitles`'s detection-failure path as a MITIGATION, not a confirmed fix, for intermittent "couldn't detect the show/episode" on next-episode SPA navigation.** Reported live: sometimes moving to the next episode shows the manual-upload message until a full page reload. Couldn't directly observe Crunchyroll's real page-update timing to confirm a root cause (no live DevTools access from here) — the leading hypothesis, based on this project's own prior confirmed finding that Crunchyroll's SPA navigation doesn't reload the page (Decisions Log 2026-07-15), is that the URL updates slightly before the new episode's schema.org `TVEpisode` block is actually in the DOM, and a full reload "fixes" it only by giving the DOM more time to settle before anything reads it. Rejected waiting for a confirmed root cause before shipping anything, since a bounded retry is low-risk (only fires on outright detection failure, never changes behavior when detection already succeeds) and directly mirrors what a manual reload already does (give it more time) — but flagged in Section 6/here as unconfirmed pending the user re-testing whether it actually resolves the symptom. Deliberately does NOT address the DOM possibly holding the PREVIOUS episode's still-parseable (stale) block instead of failing outright — that would silently fetch the wrong episode with no error at all, a different and worse failure mode that hasn't been reported and isn't fixed by a detection retry (retrying would just re-read the same stale block again).

**2026-07-17 (same live-testing session, continued) — `video` promoted from an `init()`-local `const` to a module-scope `let`, rather than threading it as a parameter through every function that needs it.** Testing the sticky-fallback scenario required switching from Frieren to Witch Hat Atelier and back — this surfaced a real bug the episode-navigation testing hadn't: subtitles stayed permanently blank after a full show change, fixable only by a page reload. Tried threading `video` as an explicit parameter first (matching the pattern just used for the timeupdate re-render fix earlier this session) — rejected once `buildUploadControl(video)` was reached, since its `video` is captured once into a closure used later, asynchronously, by a `FileReader.onload` callback; if the element were ever swapped after that control was built, the closure would still target the stale one, the same bug in a different spot. Module-scope promotion fixes every consumer (`loadSubtitles`, `renderSwitcherOptions`, `buildUploadControl`, the `timeupdate` handler itself) at once, since they all read the same live variable rather than a value snapshotted at their own creation time — also simpler than the parameter-threading it replaces. The `timeupdate` listener itself was extracted from an inline arrow function into a named `handleTimeUpdate`, so it can be `removeEventListener`'d from an old `<video>` node and re-added to a new one on SPA navigation, rather than accumulating a fresh listener on every element without ever cleaning up the old one.

**2026-07-17 (same session, continued) — Stale-detection guard added to `loadSubtitles`, closing a gap the same day's earlier detection-retry fix had explicitly flagged as unaddressed.** That fix only retried when `detectShowEpisode()` found NO parseable block at all; it explicitly did not handle the DOM still holding the PREVIOUS episode's still-valid block during the transition window, since no real case had surfaced yet. One did, the same session: Witch Hat Atelier episode 1→2→1→2 landed back on episode 1's default uploader instead of episode 2's. Fixed by tracking the last successfully loaded episode identity (series+season+episode) and, ONLY on SPA-navigation-triggered reloads (never the initial page-load call, which has nothing meaningful to compare against), treating a fresh detection that still matches the pre-navigation identity as stale — reusing the exact same bounded-retry mechanism as the null-detection case rather than building a second, parallel one. After retries are exhausted, proceeds with whatever was detected regardless of staleness, on the reasoning that showing possibly-stale data is better than blocking indefinitely — matches the existing null-detection fallback's own reasoning.

**2026-07-17 (same session, continued) — `FILE_HINT` matching scoped to the top-ranked file's own uploader, not left searching every candidate.** Found via a real report (Witch Hat Atelier's uploader preference appeared completely non-functional — always Haruhana, regardless of pick, episode change, or full page reload) and confirmed via direct Jimaku API calls before touching code: Haruhana is the ONLY uploader whose Witch Hat Atelier filename contains the literal "[JPN]" substring `FILE_HINT` was hardcoded to look for (a Phase 4 stopgap for disambiguating Haruhana's own dual-track CHS+JPN release from its JPN-only cut), so the old unscoped `textFiles.find(...)` returned Haruhana's file regardless of what `rankFiles` (default ranking OR a saved preference) had actually chosen — the override had no awareness of ranking at all. Considered removing `FILE_HINT` entirely now that real ranked selection exists — rejected: it still solves a real, different problem (disambiguating WITHIN one uploader's own multiple releases) that uploader-level ranking structurally can't, since ranking only chooses BETWEEN uploaders. Instead scoped the hint match to only files sharing `ranked[0]`'s own bracket tag, restoring the original narrow intent without it competing with uploader selection. This is also why Frieren's own testing never surfaced this bug: none of its candidate files happen to contain "[JPN]" as an exact bracketed substring, so the old unscoped search silently never fired there — the bug was real and latent since `FILE_HINT` was first added (2026-07-05/06), just invisible until testing specifically exercised the one show it was originally hardcoded for.

**2026-07-17 (same session, continued) — Non-watch-page activation fixed with a URL-pattern gate (`/watch/`), not a stricter DOM-only check.** A real report: the "couldn't detect the show/episode" error was appearing on Crunchyroll's homepage and search page. Root cause: the manifest deliberately matches all of crunchyroll.com (required so SPA navigation INTO a watch page, from a page where the content script was already running, can be detected without a full reload) — but those non-watch pages often have their own unrelated `<video>` element (an autoplay hero/promo banner), which the pre-existing `document.querySelector("video")` check alone couldn't distinguish from a real episode player. Considered checking only for a parseable `TVEpisode` JSON-LD block (reusing `detectShowEpisode()` itself) instead of a URL pattern — rejected: a slow-loading REAL watch page legitimately has no JSON-LD block yet for a brief window (the same race the day's earlier retry fix addresses), so using its absence as the "not a watch page, stop trying" signal would reintroduce false negatives on pages that only needed to wait a bit longer. A URL check doesn't have that failure mode. The `/watch/` segment itself is standard/well-known Crunchyroll URL structure, not independently re-verified against the live site this session — flagged as an assumption, not a confirmed fact, per this project's own standard (see Open Questions).

**2026-07-17 (same session, continued) — Root-caused and fixed the "stray oval artifact" report, once the user's counter-evidence (disappears when the extension is disabled; not selectable via Inspect Element) ruled out my own initial "probably a native Crunchyroll element" theory.** `#jp-immersion-subtitle` (the subtitle box) has real `padding`/`border-radius`/`background` in `content.css` — `renderCue` was clearing it to an empty string on every gap between subtitle lines (`subtitleBox.textContent = ""; if (!text) return;`) but never hiding the element itself, so an empty-but-still-`display: block` div rendered as a small dark rounded box at its fixed screen position (bottom-60px, horizontally centered) for as long as no line was active — true for most of typical dialogue pacing, not just an edge case. The second report (over Crunchyroll's own episode-list text, not near the usual subtitle position) is the SAME bug, not a different one: the box's position is fixed to the viewport, not to the video, so whatever page content happens to occupy that exact screen coordinate at a given moment is what it visually overlapped. The "can't find it in Inspect Element, can't click it" detail is what actually broke the initial wrong theory and pointed back to our own code: `#jp-immersion-subtitle` has `pointer-events: none` (deliberate, so it never blocks clicking the video underneath it) — right-clicking it doesn't hit-test to it at all, it hit-tests to whatever's behind it, so a real element we fully control looked "un-inspectable" purely as a side effect of a completely unrelated, correct design choice. Fixed by setting `display: none` specifically in the empty-text branch, and clearing it back on every other path that gives the box real content to show (`renderCue`'s non-empty branch, and `loadSubtitles`'s status/error messages, which could otherwise have inherited a stale `display: none` from a gap-in-dialogue moment right before they ran). Not yet re-confirmed live.

**2026-07-17 — Phase 4.5 formally closed, user's explicit call, project moves to Phase 5.** An unusually long single-day live-testing session (Frieren + Witch Hat Atelier) surfaced and fixed eight real bugs beyond the original build-order scope — two Jimaku search/selection bugs, a missing forced-re-render on two paths, an SPA-navigation detection race, a show-to-show `<video>`-rebinding gap, a hardcoded file-hint silently overriding uploader preference, activation on non-watch pages, and a stray empty-subtitle-box artifact (full list in the Build Order Done section above) — every one confirmed fixed via live re-testing before this closure, not just shipped and assumed. Matches the same pattern as Phase 4's own closure (2026-07-15): closed by explicit user judgment that testing has reached the point of diminishing returns, not by a claim that no more bugs exist — future issues get logged and fixed as they come up, same as any other phase.

**2026-07-17 (later same day) — "Opt-in metadata toggles" build-order item split into three independently-scoped pieces (POS built now, frequency-rank and JLPT deferred) rather than treated as one task.** The original build-order wording bundled all three under one line, but investigating readiness before writing code found they're wildly different in size: POS data is already fully computed and displayed (just needed a toggle gate), frequency-rank needs a `jmdict-compact.json` regeneration plus real threshold-derivation work, and JLPT needs an entirely new external data source chosen first — none of that was visible from the build-order wording alone. Considered building all three toggles as inert placeholders now and wiring the underlying data later — rejected: a "toggle" for data that doesn't exist yet would either do nothing (confusing) or need its own not-yet-real fallback logic, more complexity than just marking them "Coming soon" in the UI (visually present, disabled, so the eventual toggle set doesn't visually grow later) and building each for real only once its data exists. Built POS end-to-end (toggle UI, popup gating, Anki field) since it had zero open prerequisites; the other two are logged as their own scoped build-order items instead of silently started.

**2026-07-17 (later same day) — "Add to Anki" button captures sentence context synchronously at click time, using an exact character offset, not a lazy re-read of `lastText` or a substring search.** Two separate correctness risks considered together: (1) `lastText` is module-scope and reused for whatever subtitle line is CURRENTLY showing — if the sentence were read only when the "+ Anki" button itself is clicked (after the async dictionary-lookup round trip, and however long the user takes reading the popup afterward), the video could have already advanced to a different line, silently attaching the WRONG sentence to the card. Fixed by capturing it once, synchronously, inside `onWordClick` itself, before any async work starts. (2) Even captured at the right moment, finding the target word's position via `sentenceText.indexOf(surface)` would silently bold the wrong occurrence if the same word appears twice in one line — rejected in favor of having `renderGroups` track each span's exact character offset as it builds the line (cheap, since it already iterates every group in order to render them), so the eventual bolding is a direct slice, not a search. Also decided: "edit last card" (part of the original build-order wording) was deliberately not built in this pass — a separable, smaller follow-up, not required to prove the core capture flow works. (Built 2026-07-29; redesigned as an in-page editor 2026-07-30.)

**2026-07-17 (later same day) — Core-fields Anki setup: dedicated deck/note type, sentence-first card direction, both the user's explicit call.** Discovered the user's real Anki state via `deckNames`/`modelNames`/`modelFieldNames` (not assumed) before designing anything: a fresh Anki install with only a "Kaishi 1.5k" study deck and Anki's stock note types, none with more than 3 fields. User explicitly wants immersion-sourced cards kept separate from Kaishi 1.5k (their curated, methodically-worked-through deck) — confirmed this is a deliberate build-order-relevant preference, not a passing comment, so a new "Japanese Immersion" deck was built rather than adding to the existing one. Rejected reusing Anki's stock "Basic" note type despite it requiring no new setup: only 2 fields (Front/Back), not enough room to keep word/reading/gloss/sentence independently stylable/queryable — a dedicated 4-field note type was built and provisioned automatically via `createModel`/`createDeck` (idempotent, checked-before-created) rather than asking the user to set it up by hand in Anki. Card direction (sentence-first: front shows the sentence with the target word bolded, back reveals word/reading/gloss) was offered as an explicit choice against the alternative (word-first, closer to a traditional vocab flashcard) rather than assumed — the user's pick matches this project's core-loop framing (confirm what you half-know from the actual scene, not cold vocab recall). Verified end-to-end with a real test card via the service worker console before considering this done, not just code-reviewed.

**2026-07-17 (later same day) — AnkiConnect wiring built as a single shared `invokeAnkiConnect()` helper in `background.js`, not a separate fetch call per feature.** Every later Phase 5 feature (card creation, deck/model lookups, connectivity checks) needs to talk to AnkiConnect, and they all share the same request/response shape (`{action, version, params}` in, `{result, error}` out) — a single helper keeps the "network failure vs. AnkiConnect-level error" distinction (see Build Order) written once instead of duplicated at every call site. Confirmed live via the background service worker's own DevTools console rather than building throwaway test UI, since the function is a real global in that script's scope — same "test the actual code path directly" approach used for earlier fact-finding this session. The `webCorsOriginList` requirement (AnkiConnect doesn't trust extension origins by default) was expected going in from general knowledge of this integration pattern, not discovered by surprise — flagged proactively before the first test attempt rather than let the user hit an opaque failure first, which paid off (the user did need the config change, plus a JSON-syntax fix on top of that).

**2026-07-17 (later same day) — `chrome.desktopCapture` (OS-level screen capture) considered as a screenshot workaround and rejected without a live test.** The two blocked APIs (`canvas.drawImage`/`captureStream`) both go through the browser's protected media pipeline, which Widevine is specifically designed to block; `desktopCapture` grabs the actual rendered screen output instead, so it might sidestep the block on some DRM security-level configurations. Rejected testing it anyway: it requires a native "choose what to share" permission picker per capture (a real Chrome API constraint, not a design choice we could work around), which conflicts with this project's core design principle (quick capture, keep watching) regardless of whether the DRM question resolves in its favor — not worth spending a test cycle confirming a capability we likely wouldn't ship even if it worked. User agreed to drop it on this reasoning rather than requesting the live test.

**2026-07-17 — DRM feasibility test run live; screenshot Anki field dropped from the roadmap entirely, per the rule decided 2026-07-02.** Ran a console script against 2 different real Crunchyroll episodes (not a synthetic/local test page) testing all three candidate APIs. `canvas.drawImage()`: no exception, but the resulting canvas is provably blank — sampled pixel data across the frame came back all-zero, not just "looked black" from a screenshot. `video.captureStream()`: threw `NotSupportedError: Stream capture not supported with EME` — an explicit browser-level error naming the DRM mechanism directly, the clearest possible signal, not an inference. Both methods agreeing, across two independent shows with identical results, is enough to close this out with confidence — no ambiguity left to resolve with further testing. Matches this project's own precedent (the forced-subtitle-overlay item): verify hands-on before closing a platform-limitation question, which this now has.

**2026-07-17 (same day, resolved) — Audio Anki field confirmed viable via a real signal-level measurement, not just an absence of errors.** Deliberately didn't close this out on the same "no error = works" logic used for captureStream, since the two cases aren't equivalent: captureStream's error was explicit and unambiguous, while `createMediaElementSource` merely failing to throw doesn't prove real (non-silenced) audio data is flowing — some DRM implementations mute captured audio silently rather than rejecting the API call outright. An `AnalyserNode` signal-level check over 3 seconds of actual dialogue came back clearly non-silent (max deviation 34, avg ~7.9, well above the ~0 silence baseline), settling it. Real side-finding from running the tests, not from research: `createMediaElementSource()` permanently claims the video element's audio output for that page session — the first test script's own cleanup (disconnect after 500ms) silenced the episode's normal playback until a page reload, confirming any real capture implementation must keep the graph connected through to the speakers the whole time, not just during a capture window. Where the audio field gets slotted into the Phase 5 build order, and its capture-timing/UI design, is left as the user's call — see Build Order Phase 5.

**2026-07-19 — Frequency tiers locked to absolute TUBELEX counts, not percentiles.** Presented the user 3 absolute-count candidate schemes plus 3 percentile-based ones, each with real bucket sizes and boundary-word spot-checks (not just abstract percentages). The user's deciding factor was precision over recall on the "Common" label specifically — since the badge functions as a trust signal for users who already run their own SRS tools, a false "this is common" claim is worse than an under-tagged genuinely-common word. The Strict (20/500) scheme's common/uncommon boundary words (悉く, ちょくちょく, ぶら下がる) read as more solidly common than the looser schemes' boundary words (カッと, ぎょっと) — that comparison, not the raw percentages, decided it. Percentile framing (top 20%/30%/50%, etc.) was considered for staying meaningful if TUBELEX coverage changes later, but shelved: it landed almost identically to the absolute "Middle" scheme in practice, because the real distribution is so Zipfian that the floor threshold (10 vs. 15 vs. 20) barely moves bucket sizes — only the high cutoff does. Could reconsider percentiles if a future TUBELEX version or corpus swap changes the distribution's shape.

**2026-07-19 — Orphaned-entry detection uses a direct signal, not a structural proxy.** The original hypothesis (words whose reading tokenizes into 2+ kuromoji tokens are the ones TUBELEX's own UniDic-lineage tokenizer also fails to lemmatize as one unit) tested out false: after fixing a bug where the check only compared kana readings and missed that TUBELEX's own lemma field is often kanji, re-running found 61.3% of ALL content-word JMdict entries fragment under kuromoji regardless of whether TUBELEX has real data for them — fragmentation and TUBELEX-coverage turned out to be almost uncorrelated, just normal Japanese morphology (お客様 → お+客+様 is 3 tokens whether or not the word is common). Replaced with a much cleaner, principled signal: JMdict's own curator-assigned `common` flag, cross-referenced against a real (not proxied) zero-TUBELEX-score check reusing `apply-tubelex-frequency.js`'s exact scoring logic. Confirmed 7,737 such entries exist (e.g. でしょう/かもしれない/おかね/おにいさん) — real gap, not a false-positive-inflated one this time, and covering both function words and content words (content words are actually the larger share), closing an explicit open question about whether the gap was function-word-scoped.

**2026-07-19 — Orphaned-entry tiering falls back to nf-priority, corpus data first.** jmdict-simplified's public JSON has no priority-tier data at all (re-confirmed this session, matching the 2026-07-01 finding) — but the raw EDRDG `JMdict_e.xml` does, extractable via a scoped regex pass (only `<ent_seq>`/`<ke_pri>`/`<re_pri>` text, none of which involve the DTD custom entities that made full XML parsing awkward back on 2026-06-23 — this is a one-time supplementary lookup, not a reconsideration of that decision). Confirmed 100% of the `common:true`-but-unscored entries carry a priority tag (expected in hindsight: jmdict-simplified's `common` boolean is itself derived from priority-tag presence). The catch: nf/news priority data is newspaper-frequency-derived from the 1990s — the exact formal-writing-corpus bias TUBELEX was chosen over BCCWJ to avoid in the first place (2026-06-23 decision) — confirmed real via spot-check, not hypothetical: nf01-ranked orphaned entries included 委員会/外務省/共産党/総選挙/自民党 (newspaper-common, not anime-dialogue-common). Rejected two alternatives: corpus-only tiering (too incomplete — most of these words won't appear at all in a 3-episode corpus, including confirmed-common words like でしょう) and skipping nf-priority entirely (throws away complete, real data over a partial bias). Landed on: real corpus counts (from the same 3 shows/episodes `batch-test.js` already uses) override nf-rank whenever a word actually appears; nf-rank is the fallback for everything else; a bounded, enumerable keyword check (kanji substrings like 党/省/議会/選挙, gloss keywords like "ministry"/"election") demotes entries matching a recognizable political/institutional class one tier — explicitly NOT a general register classifier, just closing the one confirmed cluster. 118 of 7,737 entries were demoted this way.

**2026-07-19 — A raw JMdict `id` (ent_seq) field added to every `jmdict-compact.json` entry.** Needed to cross-reference orphaned entries against the raw XML's priority tags without re-deriving the match by kanji/kana text every time (fragile — homograph readings can collide). Small footprint cost (~1.7MB) for a durable capability; also expected to be useful for the future JLPT-tagging phase, which will likely need the same kind of external-source cross-referencing.

**2026-07-22 — JLPT data source: stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab, over the elzup/jlpt-word-list lineage.** Both packages trace back to the same underlying source (Jonathan Waller's JLPT Resources list, the same one Jisho.org uses) — the real difference is downstream packaging. stephenmk's `original_data/*.csv` carries the exact JMdict `ent_seq` per word, letting the merge be a direct ID join against `jmdict-compact.json`'s own `id` field (added 2026-07-19 anticipating exactly this). The elzup lineage has no such keys (would need fuzzy kanji/reading matching, the same approach that caused a real cross-reading contamination bug in the TUBELEX merge) and re-licenses the data MIT downstream despite the underlying content being Waller's own CC BY-SA-licensed work — a re-licensing that doesn't obviously hold up. Confirmed CC BY-SA 4.0 attribution isn't yet surfaced anywhere user-facing in the extension; tracked as a Phase 7 packaging item, see Open Questions.

**2026-07-22 — JLPT per-entry conflicts resolved to the easier level.** Inspected a real sample before assuming this was a parsing bug: ~450 of ~8,300 source rows genuinely do share an id with a row on a different level list, because Waller's list distinguishes different SPELLINGS of the same JMdict entry by level (会う/あう is N5, the same entry's 遭う spelling is N2) — a distinction `jmdict-compact.json`'s per-entry (not per-spelling) structure can't preserve through an id-only join. Rejected surfacing both levels or picking the harder one: the compact format already treats a homograph entry's various kanji spellings as informational context rather than separately tagged data elsewhere (kanji-spelling display, Phase 4), so keeping the easier level is the consistent default, not a special case invented for this field.

**2026-07-22 — Audio capture uses a continuous rolling buffer, not per-click rewind-and-recapture (user's own call, from a written analysis).** Rewind-and-recapture (pause → seek to the cue's start → replay while recording → seek back → resume) was rejected: it produces a visible rewind blip on every single capture, directly undercutting the "capture and keep watching" core positioning (see Core Goal, Section 1). Dropping the audio field from Phase 5 entirely was also rejected as premature, given DRM feasibility was already confirmed viable (2026-07-17).
