**`season_number` is not unique within a series.** Mobile Suit Gundam uses 1 for both "Mobile Suit Gundam (Dub)" and "Char's Counterattack"; Fate/Grand Order Babylonia likewise. The number therefore cannot identify a season on its own — which the resolver already doesn't rely on, since the season NAME tier runs first, but it retires any idea of using the number as a key.

**2026-08-01 — The episode number comes from Crunchyroll's page TITLE, not from `episodeNumber`.** One Piece's Elbaph arc resolved episode 1156 against **episode 1**, live. The entry was right; the number was not. Two JSON-LD captures settle why:

| page | `episodeNumber` | code in `name` | CMS `episode_number` | displayed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| One Piece Elbaph | **1** | **E1156** | 1156 | 1156 |
| Naruto S2 opener | 33 | E33 | 33 | 33 |

`episodeNumber` is season-relative on one and absolute on the other; the **code inside the compound `name` matched the displayed episode on both**, and the CMS API's own field on both. So the code is preferred wherever it is numeric, falling back to `episodeNumber` otherwise — non-numeric codes ("EEX" on an extra) say nothing about position, and the standalone-film shape has no code at all. A disagreement is logged once per page, since it means the two fields diverged and that is worth seeing.

**This also invalidates a conclusion drawn earlier the same day.** The catalogue sweep's absolute-vs-per-season table was built from the CMS API's `episode_number`, but the extension reads JSON-LD — different fields that agree on Naruto and disagree on One Piece. The table describes Crunchyroll's catalogue, not the extension's input, and should not be read as the latter. What survives is the design consequence: the magnitude rule was built not to need a per-catalogue convention, and it doesn't.

**2026-08-01 — A lone search result needs a floor, or a collection's name identifies one of its members.** The live pass found AoT's Chronicle loading **The Last Attack's** subtitles, confidently and silently — the same shape as the bug the "load nothing" decision was made to prevent, reappearing through a tier that decision never touched. Cause, measured: Crunchyroll files Chronicle under a *separate series entity* named "Attack on Titan Movies", and searching Jimaku for that returns exactly **one** entry — The Last Attack's. `matchEntryByFullTitle`'s `wasFullQuery && entries.length === 1` branch accepted it outright, with no containment check of any kind. The bidirectional check added later guards single-result *searches* (`soloHit`), not this branch, which predates it — the live report's first hypothesis was exactly right.

The floor is that **every word of the query appears in the entry's name**. Weaker than containment, deliberately: it still admits the case the branch exists for ("Attack on Titan: THE LAST ATTACK" → "Attack on Titan the Movie: The Last Attack", where the entry merely says more), while rejecting a bucket name whose own word "movies" appears nowhere in it. The bidirectional franchise check could not be reused — it rejects *both* of those. Both cases are pinned as adjacent fixtures so the floor can't be tightened until it breaks the one it must not.

**2026-08-01 — Sibling exclusion also matches on the segment before a colon.** OADs 4–8 were still listing episode 1's file. Not the cache fault from earlier the same day — a **re-translation**: Crunchyroll's title is "Ilse's Notebook: Memoirs of a Scout Regiment Member" while the file says "…Memoirs of a Recon Corps Member", the same episode with the Survey Corps rendered two different ways. No shared text in the subtitle, but the segment before the colon is identical, so it is now kept as a third title variant alongside the full title and the qualifier-stripped one. Unlike the Frozen Bond case — which remains a genuine limitation, since "The Frozen Bond" and "Hyouketsu no Kizuna" share nothing at all — this one *is* fixable, and the distinction is worth keeping: a re-translation of part of a title is recoverable, a wholly different rendering is not. Episodes sharing a prefix protect themselves, because a sibling variant overlapping the current episode's own title is skipped: "No Regrets: Part 1" and "Part 2" never rule each other out, asserted by its own fixture.

**2026-08-01 — Catalogue sweep: 30 series, 134 seasons, 4,829 episodes, and Crunchyroll's numbering answered.** The full capture is archived at `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01.json` and swept by `scripts/analyze-crunchyroll-fixtures.js`. Findings, in order of consequence:

**Crunchyroll's episode numbering is inconsistent BETWEEN SHOWS, and both conventions occur on ordinary two-season series.** SPY×FAMILY's season 2 begins at episode 26, The Apothecary Diaries' at 25, Detective Conan's third season at 754, Naruto: Shippuuden's seventh at 144 — all absolute. Frieren, Re:Zero, Chainsaw Man, Overlord, Mushoku Tensei and KonoSuba all restart at 1. There is no per-catalogue rule to rely on, which retrospectively justifies the magnitude test built for Naruto: it needs no such rule. Checked against Jimaku for the two absolute cases and both resolve today — Jimaku's index carries **both** conventions, because different uploaders use different ones (SPY×FAMILY season 2's entry holds `S02E01` and `S02E26` files for the same episode).

**The Naruto open question is closed, with evidence.** Crunchyroll reports that series absolutely (`S7:144`), so the season-relative branch — the one whose residual risk was that Crunchyroll's season grouping might disagree with the uploader's `Sxx` — never fires for it. And the groupings *do* disagree: Crunchyroll splits it into 17 seasons where the Hulu upload uses 25. The risk was real and is neutralised by the numbering, not by luck.

**"MIXED" is the normal shape for a long franchise, not a defect:** main-line arcs are numbered absolutely while side seasons restart at 1 (One Piece's Log remaster and HEROINES; My Hero Academia's, Black Clover's and Slime's OVA/movie seasons). Both branches of the magnitude rule handle it.

**No pagination anywhere** — every season's episode list matched its declared count, so nothing here is drawn from a partial listing. **`season_number` is unbounded in practice**: 0, 29, 82, 100, 102, 122, 133, 143, 153 and 902 all occur, confirming that nothing may assume a range.

**One false alarm worth recording, because it nearly became a false fix.** The sweep flagged "Sword Art Online EXTRA EDITION" as an unclassified side format. Investigating it with a hand-written 4-entry candidate list showed it resolving to the franchise's season 1 — a silent wrong-content load — and the fix was half-designed before the real 13-entry search result was checked. Jimaku holds an entry named exactly "Sword Art Online EXTRA EDITION", the franchise search *does* return it, and the season-name tier resolves it correctly. Classification was never needed. Pinned as a fixture with the full real result list, and the analyzer's label changed from "MISSED" to "REVIEW" — a truncated candidate list is as capable of manufacturing a bug as an invented response shape.

**2026-08-01 — "Special Edition" is a qualifier, not a format (first finding from the catalogue sweep).** The bulk collector's first partial capture — three series, before an auth token expired mid-run — was enough to find a real classifier bug. One Piece lists `"East Blue Special Edition HD, Subtitled (1-61)"` alongside `"East Blue (1-61)"`: a remastered cut of 61 ordinary TV episodes. The plain `/specials?/` rule read "Special" as the *format* and classed the whole arc as side content, which would have suppressed the season match and skipped Jimaku's episode filter across three arcs (61, 82 and 63 episodes). Now excluded by a negative lookahead for the qualifiers that follow it — edition, version, cut, feature, screening, broadcast. Pinned by a block of fixtures using **verbatim season titles from the sweep**, including the true positives ("OVA Season 1", "Attack on Titan OADs", "…THE FINAL CHAPTERS Special 1") so the narrowing can't quietly cost the cases the classifier exists for. This is what a catalogue-wide sweep is *for*: the bug is invisible on any of the six shows the work was built against.

**2026-08-01 — One Piece's numbering, and why it needs nothing new.** The same partial capture answered the numbering question for the largest long-runner: its main arcs are numbered **absolutely** (season 24, `"Elbaph (1156-current)"`, first `episode_number` 1156) while side seasons restart at 1 — so a per-series "which convention" verdict reads as MIXED, correctly. The magnitude rule handles it: 1156 far exceeds season 24's length, so it can only be absolute. Separately, One Piece's Jimaku files carry *neither* parseable pattern (`第N話` absent; the Netflix rips use `S40E1156`, an absolute number in the `Eyy` position, which the season-relative pattern deliberately declines to match), so `matchFilesByEpisodeNumber` returns null and the resolver defers to Jimaku's own filter — which is correct here, since that filter answers `?episode=1156` accurately. Naruto and One Piece are the two extremes and are handled by opposite branches of the same rule.

**2026-08-01 — A long franchise's episode is resolved from the entry's own filenames when Jimaku's filter can't.** Jimaku's `?episode=` filter reads whatever numbering is baked into a filename, and on a series a provider splits into cours that collides across seasons. Measured on Naruto: Shippuuden (entry 2142): `?episode=1` returns 28 files — every season's opener — and `?episode=5` likewise; `?episode=144`, the episode those tags actually describe, returns only archives; and `?episode=33` returns exactly ONE usable file, `S12E33 第275話`, a confident-looking answer for the wrong episode. Unlike the OAD case this cannot be fixed by declining to ask, because the episode number here is genuinely meaningful — it has to be resolved instead.

The entry's own files make that possible: 500 of its 506 carry BOTH their season-relative tag and their absolute one, absolute numbers cover 1–500 with **no gaps and no duplicates**, and the season boundaries are therefore derivable from the listing rather than from Crunchyroll. **Which convention Crunchyroll reports for such a show is not measured**, and deliberately doesn't need to be: the two readings are separated by magnitude, since an episode number larger than its own season's length cannot be season-relative. Swept over all 500 files under both conventions — 0 failures either way.

Three trigger signals, each from a measured failure shape: the filter's answer disagreeing with itself, answering with archives only, or answering consistently with an episode that isn't the one asked for. **The obvious signal was rejected as unsafe:** "the results disagree about which episode they are" false-positives on ordinary shows, because Frieren S2 episode 1 legitimately returns both `…S02E01…` (Amazon's per-season numbering) and `…S02E29…` (Netflix's absolute) for the *same* episode. `第N話` is the discriminator — absent from Frieren's and KonoSuba's filenames entirely, present with distinct values on every Naruto collision. Verified quiet on Frieren, KonoSuba and the AoT OVA entry. Residual risk, worth knowing: the season-relative branch assumes Crunchyroll's season grouping matches the uploader's `Sxx`; the absolute branch depends on nothing but the episode number, and a wrong grouping is the one thing that would still mislead it.

**2026-08-01 — `fileHint` is kept, because it no longer does what it was built for.** Checked whether the general non-episodic fix subsumed the per-show Naruto: Shippuuden workaround from 2026-07-06. It does not, and the premise that `fileHint` still addresses that problem is out of date by its own 2026-07-17 rescoping. Two findings, both verified rather than reasoned about. **Naruto: Shippuuden is EPISODIC**, so the `?episode=` fix never touches it — that fix is gated on non-episodic content by design. And `fileHint` **structurally cannot** address Naruto anyway: it only matches within the top-ranked pick's own bracket tag, and those files are unbracketed direct-source Hulu rips, so `topTag` is undefined and it returns before comparing anything. What it does do is choose between one uploader's dual CHS+JPN cut and its JPN-only cut of the same episode — which `rankFiles` cannot, since that orders by uploader only. Confirmed still live on Witch Hat Atelier's Haruhana release. So it is not a redundant per-show special case; it is a general language-track rule that happens to have been born from a per-show bug. Extracted into `applyFileHint` and pinned by four cases, including one asserting it cannot fire on the Naruto shape, so the same question doesn't get asked a third time.

**2026-08-01 — Jimaku's `?episode=` filter is not consulted for non-episodic content.** The live pass found five consecutive OADs loading a file whose *uploader release tag* happened to match Crunchyroll's episode number: episode 2 got "[Kamigami] …#3.25 OAD2", episode 3 got "[ReinForce] …OAD3". Neither is that OAD; both beat the files that name it outright. **The numeric matching is not in this extension at all — it is Jimaku's own server-side filter**, confirmed directly against the API (`/entries/1597/files?episode=2` returns the OAD2 file, `?episode=3` returns OAD3, `?episode=1/4/5` return nothing). The legacy assumption was on this side: that a non-empty `?episode=N` result is meaningful. It is, for a numbered TV run; it is not for a side-format entry, where the numbers in filenames are the uploader's own release numbering. Worse, the title matching added earlier the same day only ran when the filter returned **nothing**, so a wrong-but-non-empty answer was trusted outright — the failure mode where a wrong file looks like a confident match. The filter is now skipped entirely when the content is non-episodic, and the whole listing goes through title matching and sibling exclusion instead.

**This was a legacy heuristic, not a sound rule, and it was general.** The same per-file numbering problem was first seen on Naruto: Shippuuden in 2026-07-06 and worked around per-show with `fileHint`; the general case was never revisited when the non-episodic work landed. Any OVA/special/film entry whose uploader numbers releases in a way that collides with Crunchyroll's sequence hits it, which is common, not Attack-on-Titan-specific. The fix is the mechanism (don't ask a question whose answer can't be meaningful) rather than validating the digits, because there is no digit to validate on this side — the parsing happens on Jimaku's server. Both measured cases are pinned by fixtures carrying Jimaku's real per-episode answers, asserting the exact filename selected rather than only the count.

**2026-08-01 — The sibling cache merges against storage, not against memory.** Live testing showed episode 1's title still not excluded by episode 8, despite every episode in between having been visited. Cause: the accumulated list was merged against `seasonEpisodeTitles`, which SPA navigation resets to empty and `loadSubtitles` repopulates asynchronously. Crunchyroll fires its adjacent-episode lookup early in page load, normally before that read resolves, so each navigation wrote just the one or two freshly-sniffed titles and clobbered everything previous visits had learned — the cache never grew past its last two neighbours, which is exactly the observed symptom. The write is now a read-merge-write against storage itself.

**2026-08-01 — The non-episodic file fallback rules files OUT by sibling episode title, rather than listing everything.** The live pass found the fallback wasn't merely failing to narrow — it was showing files that belong to other, identifiable episodes: Frozen Bond's list included Memory Snow's file. Each candidate file is now checked against **every** episode title in the season, not just the one being resolved, and a file matching a *different* episode is dropped even when it can't be confirmed as belonging to the current one. The asymmetry is the point and is what makes this work where positive matching can't: confirming a file IS this episode requires the current title to survive translation into whatever the uploader named the file, which is exactly what fails on Re:Zero's OVAs ("The Frozen Bond" vs. "Hyouketsu no Kizuna", no shared characters); confirming a file is SOME OTHER episode only needs one of a season's worth of sibling titles to land, and "Memory Snow" appears verbatim in the sibling's filename. Rejected alternative: keep trying only to positively match the current episode's own title, which does nothing in precisely the case that was showing wrong files. If exclusion leaves **nothing**, that is reported as its own error routing to manual upload — the entry was identified correctly, so a different entry isn't the fix, and a list of confirmed-wrong files is worse than an honest "nothing here". Sibling titles get the same trailing-qualifier variant as the current one, without which Crunchyroll's "(Director's Cut)" suffix stops every sibling from ever matching a filename.

**Where the sibling titles come from — and the batched-list assumption was wrong twice over.** The request assumed the list was already available "from wherever the season/episode metadata is fetched". It wasn't: `detectShowEpisode()` parses only the *current* episode's JSON-LD block, nothing fetches Crunchyroll's `/content/v2/cms/seasons/{id}/episodes`, and Jimaku's file objects carry no episode field either. The first implementation therefore watched for a batched "episodes in this season" response — **which does not exist**; a search of a real page's network log found nothing like it. What Crunchyroll's frontend actually fires on every episode load is ADJACENT-episode lookups (`/content/v2/discover/previous_episode/{id}`, and a next_episode counterpart), each returning exactly one episode and nested differently from the CMS endpoint: the title is at `data[].panel.title`, the identifiers under `data[].panel.episode_metadata`.

That changes the architecture, not just the selector. Since one page load yields one or two siblings rather than a season, `content.js` **accumulates** them in `chrome.storage.local` across visits. A 2-episode OVA set is fully covered by a single load; an 8-episode OAD set fills in as episodes get watched. Partial coverage is still useful precisely because this rules files OUT rather than matching them in — every title learned is one more file that can be excluded — so it never waits for a complete set. The cache is keyed on the page's own series+season titles rather than Crunchyroll's `season_id`, because the id appears only in sniffed responses while those two are on every page, so a cache written on one visit is findable on the next before anything has been sniffed. An adjacent episode can legitimately belong to the *previous* season (the last episode of season 1 sits before the first OVA), so both series and season have to agree before a title is trusted — checked in `content.js`, which has the JSON-LD, rather than in the MAIN-world sniffer, which has no extension state.

Still passive: no extra request, no auth token, consistent with that script's original deliberate choice. Still fails closed — no match means no siblings and the previous listing behaviour, pinned by a fixture. `__jpImmersionSnifferStats().siblingEpisodesSeen` reports which. **After three wrong guesses at Crunchyroll response shapes, this one is pinned by `scripts/test-sibling-sniffer.js`, which runs the real script in a sandbox against the verbatim captured JSON** rather than a description of it.

**2026-08-01 — Entry resolution is tested by running the real function, not a copy of it.** `test-season-resolution.js` covered this logic by testing the selection *helpers* individually plus a hand-copied mirror of `resolveTextFiles`' selection block. A mirror can agree with itself while disagreeing with what ships, and it cannot see anything the real function does around the selection — the query ladder, the per-episode file lookup, the cour-sibling retry, the unfiltered-listing fallback, the archive filter, or any of the logging. `test-entry-resolution.js` loads the whole of `background.js` into a `vm` sandbox with `fetch` mocked and calls `resolveTextFiles` directly, so all of it is under test with no browser, no network and no AnkiConnect. Every case asserts three things — the entry, the exact console lines, and the resulting UI state (silent success / warning + dropdown / hard error) — because the 2026-07-31 pass produced both failure shapes: right subtitles with no log at all, and a missing log hiding a wrong entry. **Logging is asserted unconditionally:** a case that resolves correctly but emits no entry line fails, which is the gap that made Frieren S2 unverifiable. The older file is kept rather than replaced — it covers the helpers at a granularity the end-to-end suite can't — but its mirror is now synced to the new tiers.

**2026-08-02 — The offset between Crunchyroll's numbering and an entry's own is ASKED of Jimaku, not parsed out of filenames.** Shangri-La Frontier 43–50 and My Dress-Up Darling past episode 16 loaded nothing: Crunchyroll numbers those seasons absolutely while Jimaku's season-2 entry numbers from 1, so the file exists and the number asked cannot reach it (`?episode=43` empty, `?episode=18` three files — the same episode).

**The Naruto method was checked against the real listings first and rejected.** It works because 500 of Naruto's 506 filenames carry both numberings at once (`S07E01.第144話`); **neither of these entries has a single such file** (0/103 and 0/78). A population comparison fails too, and My Dress-Up Darling shows why: `[Haruhana]` numbers 13–15 absolutely while `[shincaps]` numbers 01–12 per season, both bare, with nothing in the number saying which — that entry would derive an offset of 0. Shangri-La Frontier only appeared to "work" for 26–42 because its absolute-numbering uploader stopped at 42.

What resolved the ambiguity is Jimaku's own index, which already files Haruhana's "‑ 13" under episode 1. So when an episodic lookup comes back empty on a **later-season entry**, `?episode=1` is requested and the highest episode position stated in those filenames is read as that episode's absolute number — 26 for Shangri-La Frontier, 13 for My Dress-Up Darling, both correct. One extra request, only on an entry that has already failed, and the retry has to return something before it is believed. Gated on the entry being a later season, since a season-1 entry has no offset to have and a batch file sitting under its episode 1 would send the retry somewhere arbitrary. Position numbers are anchored on `" - "` and a following delimiter so a hyphenated title, a year in parentheses or a resolution can't be read as one.

**2026-08-02 — A season number and a season title must be read from the SAME field.** Reported the other way round from every previous season bug: *season 1* was playing *season 2's* subtitles, on Slimes 300 Years and Mushoku Tensei. Cause, reproduced from the live page's own metadata: Jimaku's season-2 entry carries its marker only in the ENGLISH name, while its Japanese name has none ("…: Sono ni") or a Roman numeral the parser doesn't read ("Mushoku Tensei II") — so the Japanese field reads as season 1. The old test took the season number from *either* field and the title from *either* field, independently, so an entry could pass on a combination describing no real season: season 1 from the Japanese name, title from the English one. Jimaku returns the season-2 entry first for Mushoku Tensei, so `find()` reached it before season 1's. Each field is now checked as a unit — the English name fails the number, the Japanese name fails the title, and neither can lend the other half a match.

**2026-08-02 — An episode-title search may only widen the pool with entries from the same franchise.** Searching a film's own title is what finds an entry the series search misses, but a *short* episode title is a common word: Tokyo Ghoul's OVA "Jack" pulled in 24 unrelated shows, several containing "jack", which made the title match ambiguous and lost an episode that had been resolving fine. Merged results are now filtered by the same bidirectional franchise check used elsewhere. With that and the split-franchise fix, both Tokyo Ghoul OVAs resolve to their own entries ([PINTO] and [JACK], two files each) where episode 1 had been listing all 71 files of the main series entry.

**2026-08-02 — The mass-test sweep never tested resolution, and the record said otherwise.** `analyze-crunchyroll-fixtures.js` imports five helpers from `background.js` and calls none of them `resolveTextFiles`; it never contacts Jimaku. Its "zero misses" result meant *the season-title classifier didn't mislabel a season*, and was repeatedly summarised here as though it meant those shows resolved correctly. They didn't: Tokyo Ghoul's "Root A" and ":re", Slimes 300 Years' season 2 and My Dress-Up Darling's season 2 were all serving season 1's subtitles while that claim stood. `scripts/sweep-resolution.js` now does the real thing — every season in a capture through the real resolver against the live Jimaku API, scored OK/SUSPECT/ASKS/ERROR. Two properties matter: **SUSPECT is a reading list, not a verdict** (a franchise Jimaku keeps in one entry produces SUSPECT on every season, correctly), and it **saturates Jimaku's rate limit**, so it must not run beside the other live tests — that combination produced a confusing failure in `test-render-pipeline.js` that was contention, not a regression.

**2026-08-02 — On a franchise Jimaku splits, the bare-franchise entry is season 1, not a container.** Tokyo Ghoul's "Root A" resolved confidently to season 1's entry because nothing lexical reaches Jimaku's "Tokyo Ghoul √A" from Crunchyroll's "Root A", and the exact-title tier then matched the franchise name. The fallback is now declined — but only on **visible evidence of splitting**, some entry carrying a season marker above 1. That gate is the whole design: One Piece's 27 search results contain no such entry, its single "ONE PIECE" entry genuinely holds all 24 arc seasons, and every arc resolving to it is correct. Both arrangements measured before the rule was written; both are fixtures. Where the season truly can't be reached, the picker is the honest outcome rather than a guess.

**2026-08-02 — An episode code that isn't a plain integer means the episode has no position.** Shangri-La Frontier's bonus episode is `"Season 1 | E14.5 - …"` with `episodeNumber: 15`; Dr. STONE's extra is `"… | EEX - …"` with `episodeNumber: 1`. Both loaded a real, different episode's subtitles confidently — 14.5 played 15's, the extra played episode 1's. A non-integer code now makes the episode non-episodic, exactly like an OVA collection, so the number is never used to pick a file. This is the same principle as the OAD work, arriving from the opposite direction: there the *season* had no numbering, here the *episode* has no position within one.

**2026-08-02 — Narrowing must not discard alternates for the same work.** On a film every file in the entry IS that film, so filtering by title can only drop other providers' releases: Mugen Train showed 1 of its 4 files because only Fujitv's spells the title as Crunchyroll does. Positive narrowing is skipped for single works; sibling exclusion still runs, since that only removes a file belonging to a *different* episode. The distinction the report drew — narrow to the right episode, never away from valid alternates for it — is now the rule.

**2026-08-04 — Listing an entry's whole contents requires knowing WHICH WORK it is.** Demon Slayer's "TV Specials" season reached the main series entry through the series title alone and then handed over its entire catalogue — 131 files spanning 26 episodes, identically for all five of its episodes. Dr. STONE's SCIENCE FUTURE season did the same. Every positive tier (season name, content title, format class, a lone Jimaku hit) means the work is identified; falling back to the series title does not — it means the *show* was found, not this season — so the unfiltered listing is now refused there. Two wrong turns on the way, both caught by fixtures rather than shipped: gating on "the entry's files name several episodes" broke every legitimate OVA collection, which is indistinguishable from that angle, and the first attempt passed file OBJECTS to a function expecting names, so the guard silently never fired while the audit kept reporting the bug.

**2026-08-04 — An `(English Dub)` qualifier is an audio label, not part of the season's name.** Jimaku entries never carry it, so the whole season-name tier missed on every dubbed season — 19 of the 90 seasons in the 2026-08-04 capture, covering 577 episodes; Code Geass failed on all 55 of its own. The name is now tried with the qualifier and then without. Confirmed **not** a regression from this session's work: Code Geass and Detective Conan fail identically against pre-fix `background.js`, which is what the earlier sweeps could never have shown.

**2026-08-04 — Detective Conan's 580 refusals are correct, not defects.** Crunchyroll calls it "Detective Conan"; Jimaku indexes it as "Case Closed" / "Meitantei Conan". No lexical overlap for the query ladder to bridge, so the picker is the honest answer. Recorded because a raw defect count would have read as a catastrophe: the same run reported 876 DECLINED, of which this show alone was two thirds.

**2026-08-04 — `audit-resolution.js`: what a resolution test can and cannot prove.** Four checks are PROVEN (a flag is a defect): MIXED (one episode's files state episode numbers with nothing in common), DUPLICATE (two episodes of a season return an identical list), COLLISION (two seasons resolve to one entry), EMPTY (nothing loaded and it wasn't a deliberate refusal). Three are FLAGGED for a human: DECLINED, NARROWED, UNACCOUNTED. **Three things are NOT checked because no automatic ground truth exists**, and this is written into the script's own header so it can't be misread again: whether the resolved entry is the RIGHT entry (nothing states which Jimaku entry a Crunchyroll season corresponds to — a show whose every season resolved to one wrong-but-consistent entry would pass every check); whether a file stating no episode belongs to this one (bare numbers carry opposite meanings inside one entry); and whether the subtitles match the scene, which only a human can establish. Proving the audit against pre-fix code found a flaw in the audit itself first — comparing identity *labels* made `[Cleo]Tokyo_Ghoul_-_01` and `…S01E01` read as different episodes — so identities are compared as sets of episode numbers and disagree only when they share none.

**2026-08-04 — Two audit-tool defects that inflated its own first result.** Failures were being cached, so one transient 429 became permanent for that URL and produced 59 spurious EMPTY findings out of 84; only successes are cached now, and a failed-request count is reported so a run's integrity is visible rather than inferred. And NARROWED fired on any subset with a silently-excluded file, which on a 277-file Frieren entry is nearly always — 661 findings burying the shape it exists for; it is now restricted to entries whose files state no episode identities at all, where every file is plausibly the same work. **Verified by inspection that the caching defect could only inflate EMPTY**: every Jimaku fetch in the resolver throws on a non-OK response, so a cached failure can never yield a successful-but-wrong file list, meaning MIXED/DUPLICATE/COLLISION could be masked but never manufactured.

**2026-08-11 — A bare trailing number is the only ambiguous cour marker, and the numbering has to settle it.** Haikyu!!'s "TO THE TOP" is one 25-episode Crunchyroll season and two Jimaku entries, and the back half is named by appending a bare number to the arc title — which `parseSeasonMarker` reads as a season number, so `courSiblingEntries` rejected it for having "a different season" than an entry it is actually the continuation of. Episodes 14–25 failed outright, 12 of the 25. Two things had to be got right, and the first attempt got the second one wrong:

- **Which names count as a continuation.** Only "the chosen entry's name plus a BARE trailing number". The first attempt used `parseSeasonMarker`'s base instead, which strips a Part suffix *and* a season marker — so "Mushoku Tensei: Jobless Reincarnation Season 2 Part 2" collapsed to the bare franchise name and was accepted as a continuation of **season 1's** entry, serving season 2's episodes under season 1's numbers on a show that had been clean. Caught by the known-bug regression run, and confirmed as newly introduced by re-running the same show against the committed `background.js` via `--background` (COLLISION 0 → 1). An explicit "Season 2" / "2nd Season" says which it is; "Part 2" is already `courSiblingEntries`' job; only a bare number is genuinely ambiguous.
- **That the bare-number shape is still not conclusive.** KonoSuba's "… 2" and "… 3" are separate SEASONS written exactly like Haikyu's cour. So the caller reads both entries' full listings and requires the sibling's episodes to start **above** everything the chosen entry holds (14 > 13) rather than restarting into it (1 ≤ 10).

Rejected loosening the season comparison inside `courSiblingEntries`, which cannot express either check. Both directions are pinned as fixtures — Haikyu accepted, KonoSuba rejected by numbering, Jujutsu Kaisen rejected by name shape — and all the entry names and listings are real; the single constructed value (an empty `?episode=24` on entry 712) is labelled as such, since a gap is the only way to reach the retry at all.

**2026-08-11 — Where a season can't be identified, decline rather than guess (extended to arc-named seasons).** Crunchyroll's generic "Season N" titles and Jimaku's arc-named entries have no field in common: Dr. STONE Season 2/3 and Fruits Basket Season 2/The Final Season all load season 1's subtitles, silently. Considered mapping "Season N" onto the Nth Jimaku entry of the franchise and rejected it — Jimaku's search order is relevance, not chronology, so the mapping would be a guess that *looks* authoritative, which is the exact failure mode of the bug. Declining to the entry picker instead, consistent with the 2026-08-01 no-valid-entry decision and for the same reason: wrong-show sentences reach permanent Anki cards, and a missed banner is cheaper than a wrong card. **Not yet implemented** — the decline condition needs a precise definition of "no entry matches season N" first, because the name and number tiers currently test only the chosen entry rather than the candidate set.

**2026-08-11 — Fix the audit's scoring, not the resolver, where Jimaku simply has nothing (built).** KONOSUBA's dubbed season 11s are OVAs held under separate Jimaku entries, KONOSUBA 3's last two episodes have blank Crunchyroll episode codes, and Steins;Gate 0's episode 24 is the unaired 23β that entry 466 does not carry. The audit scored all of these as proven EMPTY defects — refusals, not faults, and leaving them that way would put permanent false failures into every future pass, the same category error as Detective Conan's 580 refusals (2026-08-04). Rejected "make the resolver find them": chasing a separate OVA entry per dubbed season is real scope, and the litmus test doesn't favour it. Three over-reporting shapes were fixed together, adding two flagged-not-proven categories, `MISSING` and `SHARED-ENTRY`:

- **EMPTY → MISSING** when the season's own entry states no such episode *and* no offset could be hiding it. **An offset can hide it in two opposite directions, and the first implementation only handled one** — it checked whether the entry numbers above the season (Vigilantes' files run to 26 for a 13-episode season) but not whether Crunchyroll numbers above the entry (Shangri-La's season 2 is episodes 26–51 on Crunchyroll, 1–26 on Jimaku). That gap excused three of the six bugs the audit exists to catch. The second direction needs no lookup at all: Crunchyroll's highest episode number exceeding its own episode count proves the season is absolutely numbered.
- **COLLISION → SHARED-ENTRY** when Jimaku holds only one TV entry for the show, so the seasons have nothing else to resolve to. The header had always *claimed* this check meant "while a distinct entry plausibly exists"; nothing verified it, and Black Clover's four Crunchyroll "parts" were flagged for sharing the only Black Clover entry that exists.
- **MIXED suppressed** where one number falls inside the season's range and one above it — the same episode under two conventions. The boundary is the season's highest Crunchyroll episode number, **not** its episode count, because Crunchyroll numbers some seasons absolutely (JUJUTSU KAISEN Season 2 is episodes 25–47). SPY x FAMILY's episode 13 correctly stays flagged, since 13 and 25 both sit inside season 1's range.

**Verified by the `--background` proof, which is what caught the one-direction bug**: against the pre-fix 2026-08-02 resolver the audit still reports 9 proven defects covering all six known bugs, and against the current resolver the same fixture reports 0.

**2026-08-12 — A later season's own name is enough to refuse season 1's entry.** Dr. STONE seasons 2 and 3 and Fruits Basket's season 2 and Final Season all played season 1's subtitles, silently, through the exact-title fallback. The existing guard could not see it: `franchiseIsSplit` only detects a split Jimaku spells with a NUMBER, and these franchises live under arc names — `STONE WARS`, `NEW WORLD`, `The Final`. **The decline condition avoids the definition problem rather than solving it**: we never decide which entry a season *should* be, only that the bare-franchise entry is season 1 (2026-08-02) and this is not season 1, so that entry is wrong by definition. Two constraints, both measured rather than assumed:

- **Read the season NAME, never `seasonNumber`.** That field is a positional index, so One Piece's 24 arcs are all numbered 2 and above and using it would decline every one of them. From the name, "East Blue (1-61)" carries no marker and Black Clover's "Season 1 Part 1" parses to 1. "Final Season" counts as a marker — Fruits Basket and Attack on Titan both use it with no digit.
- **Only where the franchise genuinely has several works.** Naruto: Shippuuden is the trap: Crunchyroll splits it to "Season 17" while Jimaku keeps all 500 episodes in ONE entry, so refusing that entry blanks the whole show — a case the live checklist exercises on three episodes. Its other five results are films, excluded by Jimaku's own `movie` flag, leaving a count of 1. Counting entries alone would have read those films as alternatives. Dr. STONE is the opposite: eight entries, no films.

**Fruits Basket needed a second, different fix**, found only by probing it: Jimaku's season-1 entry carries the same "(2019)" as Crunchyroll's series title, so the search returned exactly one entry and the franchise looked like a single work — the resolver never knew seasons 2 and Final existed. The query ladder now looks past its first hit in exactly that case, and season 2 **resolves correctly** to its own entry rather than merely declining. The Final Season still declines, since Jimaku calls it "The Final" and nothing bridges that lexically.

Verified over 1,267 episodes across every affected show plus the known-bug six: both COLLISIONs gone, no new proven defects, and the only new refusals are the intended 46 episodes (Dr. STONE 33, Fruits Basket Final 13). **Also corrects the record**: JUJUTSU KAISEN Season 3 was written up as part of this defect and never belonged there — it already declined correctly through the existing guard.

**2026-08-12 — A cour entry has to be checked twice: that it covers the episode, and that its answer is that episode.** Both faults were found by verifying the two-numberings fix rather than by the fix itself, and both served an hour of the wrong show without complaint.

- **Coverage.** Dr. STONE's SCIENCE FUTURE is one 37-episode Crunchyroll season split into three Jimaku cours. Episode 13 finds nothing in Cour 1, so the retry asked Cour 3 — which holds 25–37 — for "episode 13" and got its thirteenth file, `S04E37`. Episodes 13 and 37 both played episode 37. The retry now requires the sibling's own filenames to state the requested episode, which skips Cour 3 and reaches Cour 2, resolving episode 13 *correctly* rather than merely refusing. Enforced only where the sibling states episode numbers at all, so the collections the cour retry was built for are unaffected.
- **The answer itself.** A cour entry is numbered from its own start, so Jimaku returns the cour's Nth file alongside episode N: SPY x FAMILY's Cour 2 answered episode 13 with `S01E13` **and** `S01E25`, seven of thirteen files being a different episode. Files that positively state a different episode are now dropped; files stating nothing are kept, since nothing places them elsewhere. **Confined to the cour retry deliberately** — a blanket version would break the 2026-08-02 rule that narrowing must not discard valid alternates, since Frieren's season 2 legitimately answers episode 9 with files numbered both 09 and 37. Verified Frieren still returns all twelve.

Recognising `[13]`-style bracket numbering was needed for the second of these, restricted to digits-only 1–3 characters so release tags sharing that syntax (`[1080p]`, `[BDRip]`, CRCs like `[E395C71D]`, years) cannot be read as positions.

**2026-08-12 — One unnumbered file in a positively identified entry is a work, not a missing episode.** My Hero Academia's "I am a Hero too" is a TV special Crunchyroll lists as an ordinary "episode 1". Its entry matched on the season name and holds exactly one file, which names no episode — so the resolver asked for episode 1, got nothing, and failed on a special Jimaku actually has. Now served as a single work, gated three ways so it can never hand one file to a whole season: the entry must have been positively identified rather than reached by the series-title fallback, hold exactly one usable file, and that file must state no episode of its own.

**2026-08-12 — The audit must not invent an episode number for a trailer.** Crunchyroll lists PVs inside a season with no episode code and no episode number; falling back to 1 made Haikyu!!'s PV "resolve" to episode 1's files and read as a DUPLICATE of the real episode 1, and Fairy Tail's read as MIXED because that season is numbered from 176. Two of the three findings left in the full run were this, and they would have recurred on every future run. Listed as `NO-POSITION` and not scored — the same principle the resolver settled on for non-integer episode codes (2026-08-02).

**2026-08-12 — An entry's own numbers reveal a second numbering; the season range was never needed.** MHA: Vigilantes' season 2 lost 6 of its 13 episodes because entry 11309 holds one uploader's files numbered 01–08 and another's numbered S02E14–S02E26 for the same episodes, and six exist only in the second set. This was recorded as blocked on Crunchyroll's season episode range, which `resolveTextFiles` never receives. **That was wrong**, and the three arrangements it has to separate are all measurable from the entry alone:

| Entry | Stated numbers | States 1? | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vigilantes S2 (11309) | 1–8 **and** 14–26 | yes | offset 13 |
| Haikyu TO THE TOP 2 (2886) | 14–25 | no | no offset derivable |
| My Dress-Up Darling S2 (9834) | 1–15 contiguous | yes | no second numbering |

**Anchoring on "does the entry state episode 1" is what makes it safe.** A cour entry holding only the back half of a season looks identical by its lowest number — Haikyu's is also 14 — but it never states 1, so nothing is derivable and its episode 14 cannot be sent to 27. That collision was the stated reason for calling this unfixable. My Dress-Up Darling has one contiguous run and no second population, so the Jimaku-index method chosen for it on 2026-08-02 stays in charge.

Further guards, because a derived offset is a guess until something confirms it: the higher run must be contiguous and at least 3 files (a stray special or batch archive is not a numbering), the requested episode must be absent from the lower run, and the derived episode must actually return files. It runs last among the retries, only on an episode that has already failed everything cheaper, so nothing that works today can reach it. Confirmed externally rather than internally: episode 6 resolves to `S02E19.雨と雲` and Crunchyroll titles episode 6 "Rain and Clouds".

**2026-08-12 — A stage play is not animation, so it gets the picker, not the anime.** Crunchyroll lists "Chainsaw Man The Stage" as season 3 of Chainsaw Man; with no Jimaku entry to match it fell through to the bare-franchise entry and rendered the TV series' episode 1 over a stage play, silently. The existing guard against that fallback (`franchiseIsSplit`) only counts entries carrying a season number above 1, and Jimaku splits this franchise by named work instead — Reze-hen, Soushuu-hen. **Rejected broadening that guard to "any entry extending the franchise name"**: One Piece's search results are full of such entries, and its 24 arc seasons resolving to the single "ONE PIECE" entry is correct (2026-08-02), so the broader rule would decline all 24. Added a `stage` content class instead, refused unless something names it specifically — a stage play is a different medium and an anime subtitle index will never hold one, so this is classification rather than heuristic. Narrow by design: one season across all three captures matches it, and a false positive costs the picker, which is the safe direction.

**2026-08-12 — Narrowing by title identifies the WORK, not which part of it.** "Chainsaw Man - The Compilation" is two recap episodes in one Jimaku entry, so both Crunchyroll episodes narrowed to the same four files and each offered the other's subtitles as readily as its own. Fixed by selecting the numbered part after title narrowing. This re-admits per-file numbering, withdrawn on 2026-08-01 after Jimaku's filter read release tags like "OAD2" as episode numbers and served five consecutive OADs the wrong file, so it is fenced by three conditions that each rule that failure out: only among files already narrowed to one work by title; every file must state exactly one position, so an unparseable tag disqualifies the set rather than being guessed at (Attack on Titan's "- OAD3", "- #3.25 OAD2"); and the positions must run 1..N with no gaps (Re:Zero's OVA entry states 26 and 27 and stops here). Both fixtures are in the suite and were the check on this change. **Correcting the record**: this was written up as both Chainsaw Man seasons resolving to the anime entry — The Compilation never did.

**2026-08-12 — A dropped connection is not a defect.** A single transient `fetch failed` inside the resolver was scored as a proven EMPTY, and put a phantom Haikyu!! failure into a regression run that briefly read as a real regression from the Chainsaw Man work. The run-integrity counter did not catch it because it tracks the AUDIT's own requests, not the resolver's. Network errors are now retried on the same backoff as a 429 (they previously escaped the retry loop entirely, since a dropped connection throws rather than returning a status), and a transport failure that still gets through is reported as `REQUEST-FAILED` — a run that learned nothing about that episode, not a verdict on it. Same principle as the 2026-08-04 cached-failure fix: infrastructure noise must never read as a resolution defect.

**2026-08-13 — Refusing to guess only works if the manual answer is remembered.** The 2026-08-12 decision above (decline where Crunchyroll's "Season N" cannot be tied to Jimaku's arc-named entry) traded a silent wrong load for an entry picker. What that traded was only acceptable once per season; the pick was in fact repeated every episode, because the switcher's entry choice was never persisted — the uploader preference beside it was, which is what made the gap easy to miss and easy to mis-state. The chosen entry id is now saved under the same series+season key. Three properties matter and are deliberate: it is only ever reachable after a manual pick, so it can never override a working automatic answer; any failure to use it (deleted entry, renamed, nothing for this episode) falls back to ordinary resolution rather than throwing, because a memory the user cannot escape is worse than no memory; and the picker stays on screen while a remembered entry is in use, since hiding it would make the memory a one-way door.

**2026-08-13 — An already-rejected lone candidate is not a choice.** Showing the entry picker in the unresolved state was extended to a single candidate (it previously required more than one, which hid the panel entirely on Attack on Titan's Chronicle and left manual upload as the only visible option). But Jimaku has NO Chronicle entry — a search on Crunchyroll's own "Attack on Titan Movies" series entity returns only The Last Attack, a different film, and a direct search finds nothing — so "pick one" presented an option the resolver had already ruled out, which is the wrong-subtitles bug the refusal exists to prevent, moved one click away. A lone candidate in that state now says what it is and points at manual upload, with the dropdown kept for anyone who knows better.


**2026-08-15 — The capture clock counts samples, not seconds.** Cue extents and clip bounds were measured on `audioCtx.currentTime`, chosen in 2026-07-22 precisely because it keeps running through a pause or seek and so needs no reconciliation with a video timeline that jumps. That property is what broke: a paused `<video>` still feeds its graph, so the clock advanced while the ring buffer filled with silence, and a cue left open across a pause recorded the pause as part of the line — the 44-second mostly-silent waveform reported live. **Rejected: capping the damage harder.** A 20-second ceiling already existed and had already fired on this exact case; it bounds the export and says nothing about whether the recorded timings mean anything. The clock is now the buffer's own write count, and the processor doesn't write while the video is paused or seeking, so a pause takes literally zero space and the two can no longer disagree. Everything the original reasoning wanted still holds — it never has to reconcile against `video.currentTime` — and it gains the property that a position on the clock is a position in the buffer by construction rather than by assumption.

**2026-08-15 — An interrupted capture keeps the audio it heard, rather than none.** Since 2026-07-31 a capture spanning a seek or an episode change resolved to no audio, on the grounds that the buffer would by then hold the wrong episode. Correct about the audio AFTER the jump and wrong about the audio before it: at the instant of the jump the buffer still holds the line the user clicked, usually most of the way through. Pending captures are now flushed at that instant instead, before the epoch bump, so the card gets a short but correct clip. **Rejected: waiting to see whether the jump was navigation or a small seek** — both invalidate the same timings, and the flush is correct for both. The wrong-episode risk is closed from the other end: a flushed clip is capped at the clicked line's own subtitle window plus 0.25s, which matters because SPA navigation is only noticed by a 1s poll and the next episode's audio can already be arriving by then. This is what makes "switch episodes before the chip appears" produce audio at all.

**2026-08-15 — The edit buffer is sealed on navigation, not dropped.** `resetCaptureForNavigation` cleared the retained PCM along with the ring buffer, so the trim editor said the audio was "no longer available to edit" for a clip sitting complete in memory — the user only had to move to the next episode. The retained buffer is an independent copy; what navigation actually invalidates is the ability to grow it. It is now topped up one last time and its bounds dropped. **Rejected: re-decoding the exported WAV back out of Anki as a fallback** — that was already rejected in 2026-07-30 for the reason that still applies (it can only ever tighten a clip, never extend one), and it would have been a second, worse code path for a case that needed no path at all.

**2026-08-15 — Which note the audio buffer belongs to is decided by the capture, not by recency.** `audioBufferNoteId` was set the moment the note was created, but the buffer is only cut when the capture RESOLVES — which for a split sentence is seconds later. In that window the newest note pointed at the PREVIOUS card's samples, so opening the trim editor drew and played the wrong clip (reported live). Each capture now carries a token issued at click time and the note only claims the buffer once that token's clip has landed. **Rejected: clearing the buffer at click time** — that would have replaced the wrong clip with no clip, when the honest answer is that the audio is still being captured, which is what the panel now says while it waits on the pending promise.

**2026-08-15 — The trim editor gets a playhead and a transport, not more handles.** The live request was for the editor to work "like a video editing program". Two readings: give the waveform a DAW's drag-to-select-and-audition model, or an NLE's scrub-and-set-in/out model. Chose the NLE: the task here is always "find the exact moment this line starts" against a clip whose rough bounds are already correct, which is a scrubbing problem, not a selecting problem. Handle dragging stays as the fast coarse adjustment. Playback mutes the episode rather than pausing it — pausing to check a card is exactly the interruption that ruled out rewind-and-recapture in the first place (2026-07-22).

**2026-08-15 — Position tells a background English line from the translation; nothing in the text does.** A capture's `Translation` field carried both its own sentence and "Move your feet.", an unrelated line from elsewhere in the scene. **Rejected: picking the single best-overlapping cue** — one Japanese line legitimately translates as two consecutive English sentences, and that rule would throw the second away. **Rejected: never trusting `\an8`** — a line placed at the top because the speaker is at the bottom of frame is ordinary dialogue and the only line there. The rule is relative: a top-positioned cue is dropped only when a bottom-positioned one is competing for the same moment, which is exactly the situation the subtitler was resolving by moving it. Needs a live confirmation that Crunchyroll's own caption files really do carry the override on the line the report described — the mechanism is right, the assumption about which file it appears in is not yet verified.

**2026-08-15 — An orphaned content script asks for a reload instead of half-repairing itself.** After an extension reload the old content script keeps running with every `chrome.*` call throwing, which is the reported "only the offset, upload and switcher panels appear, and no subtitles". Chrome can re-inject content scripts into an already-open tab, and everything except the audio capture would come back that way — but `createMediaElementSource` can be called only once per `<video>` for the life of the page, and the dead script's graph is still holding it and can never be disconnected (2026-07-17). Re-injecting therefore produces an extension that looks completely fixed and silently has no audio field, which is worse than the state it replaced. The script now detects the invalidation on its own ~4Hz tick (and a 2s timer, so a paused video still notices), removes every element it mounted, and shows one banner with a Reload button. **Rejected: reloading the page automatically** — a page reload mid-episode is a bigger interruption than a click, and an automatic one triggered by a Web Store update would be unexplained.

**2026-08-15 — The Japanese full stop is stripped from displayed subtitles; the comma is not.** Requested from live use. Japanese subtitling convention uses a space where prose uses 。, most releases already do it, and the ones that don't look inconsistent beside them. Applied in `cueDisplayText`, so the Anki `Sentence` field matches the screen by construction rather than by a second rule. Scoped deliberately to 。 alone: 、 is mid-sentence phrasing that wasn't asked about, and ？／！ carry tone the sentence would lose. Replaced with a space rather than deleted, so a line holding two sentences doesn't run them together.

## 8. Open Questions

### Ongoing

**Needs testing or real-world feedback to resolve:**
1. **Forced-subtitle overlay behavior** — still fully untested hands-on. Deprioritized, and skepticism is warranted: no research or testing so far has turned up a single Crunchyroll title with an actual untoggleable forced-subtitle layer (One Piece ep 894 confirmed clean). Treated as speculative until a real case appears — don't invest further design thought here without one. Related context for whenever this is picked up, not a bug: Bocchi ep 1 QA found hardcoded in-scene translated text (e.g. book pages) with no fixed overlay zone, varying per scene and per fansub. *(Phase 4)*
2. **lindera-wasm vs. Vibrato-wasm fallback** — which is the better choice if kuromoji.js segmentation quality proves insufficient on colloquial/slang-heavy dialogue. Deferred until real testing shows a gap. *(Phase 4)*
3. **Dual-view "noise"** — the mechanism sometimes attaches a real-but-not-strictly-relevant coincidental match. Non-destructive; accepted as an inherent trade-off unless live testing shows it's genuinely distracting. Confirmed real-corpus instance 2026-07-03: もの…になる attaches a wrong idiomatic gloss ("to prove successful") to a sentence where もの is used compositionally, not idiomatically — a different failure mode (a wrong gloss shown with equal weight, not just an extra click) but still non-destructive, so priority stays as scoped. *(Phase 4)*
4. **あ's interjection sense (あっ, "ah/oh/hey") ordering — status unverified.** Bocchi ep 1 QA flagged the correct colloquial sense as buried below rarer prefix/noun senses; grouped at the time with 私/あたし as "should be validated by the already-scheduled duplicate-gloss/multi-reading work" rather than treated as its own fix. That work is now built (2026-07-05), and 私/あたし was explicitly confirmed fixed by it, but あ itself was never separately re-checked — needs a live/corpus spot-check before assuming it's resolved. *(Phase 4)*
5. **Sentence-only capture, no target word** — reconsidered 2026-07-04: the use case is real (a sentence with no confusing vocabulary can still be worth saving), not just speculative, but it requires its own capture paradigm (a line-level trigger, decisions about what fills the word/reading/gloss fields with no target word) rather than a toggle on the existing flow — real build cost, not a quick add. Kept deferred rather than cut: expected to come up less often than ordinary target-word capture, and real usage of the word-click flow post-Phase-5 will show whether it's actually needed better than guessing now. *(Phase 5)*
6. **Orphaned-entry corpus override thinness** — the frequency badge's corpus override (Section 5, Phase 5) only has 3 episodes of real dialogue to rank against, so most of the 7,737-entry JMdict-common-but-TUBELEX-unscored set falls back to newspaper-derived nf-priority instead of real anime-register data. Not blocking (nf-priority + the formal-register demotion already produce reasonable tiers, confirmed live), but if `batch-test.js`'s corpus ever expands for unrelated reasons, re-running `build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` would improve coverage for free. *(Phase 5)*
7. **Pricing specifics** — monthly and lifetime price points. Model is decided (subscription + lifetime); exact numbers revisit after soft-launch feedback. *(Phase 7)*
8. **20–30 minute continuous-viewing capture stability is still untested — the only checklist item no live pass has reached.** SPA navigation during capture was tested on 2026-07-31 and again on 2026-08-15; it no longer produces the wrong episode's audio, and as of 2026-08-15 it produces the right episode's audio rather than none. **Pause-mid-line dead-air splicing was accepted as expected behaviour on 2026-07-31 and that was wrong** — the 2026-08-15 pass showed it as a 44-second clip of silence, and the underlying cause (the clock advancing while the buffer filled with silence) is fixed. *(Phase 5)*
9. **JLPT attribution not yet surfaced anywhere user-facing.** The CC BY-SA 4.0 source data (Decisions Log 2026-07-22) requires attribution; nowhere in the extension currently shows it. Not blocking Phase 5 — needs a credits/about surface, realistically a Phase 7 packaging item, but flagged here so it isn't lost track of before public launch. *(Phase 7)*
10. **English subtitle dual-display — working, but permanently dependent on Crunchyroll's own undocumented API staying stable.** The four bugs found on 2026-07-26 (desync, vertical-order swapping, audio loudness, Translation on the front) are all fixed, and the 2026-07-31 pass exercised English display heavily without reporting any of them again — the bug it did find was in the capture chip, not the display. What keeps this open is the dependency, not a defect. If Crunchyroll ever changes that response's shape, this breaks silently (no English track, not an error) — worth remembering if it stops working after an unrelated Crunchyroll update. *(Phase 5)*
11. **Dual-language stripping and JP/EN timing pairing need a live pass (2026-07-26).** Both were built after the second live-testing round and are verified only offline: the stripping against 27 real Jimaku files (zero Japanese lines lost, the tested `_ja-en.ass` cleaned exactly), the timing pairing by simulation at the real ~4Hz tick rate (staggered ticks 5/51 → 0/51, flagged two-cue exception preserved). Needs confirming on a real episode that a dual-language provider now shows exactly one English line — ours — with a clean Japanese line and a clean Anki `Sentence` field, and that the two lines now appear and disappear together. `window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` remains available if the caption pipeline regresses. *(Phase 5)*
12. **Split-sentence Anki rejoining — text side confirmed live; the audio side has now failed two live passes and been re-fixed twice (2026-07-27, 2026-07-31).** The 2026-07-27 live pass confirmed the card itself is correct: capturing from the first half produced the complete Japanese sentence and the matching English. The audio was wrong (first half only, after a long busy wait) and is fixed — see Build Order — so what still needs confirming is the clip covering both halves, from a capture in *either* half, and ordinary single-cue captures being unchanged. The 2026-07-31 pass found it unreliable rather than uniformly wrong: capturing from the first half sometimes covered both halves and sometimes only the first (a wait that expired — now non-blocking and much longer), and one second-half capture ran on into the following line. That second symptom has two possible causes and the fix addresses one of them (display windows being wider than the sentence); the other is the merge span itself over-extending when consecutive lines share an English translation, which can't be told apart without the file and timestamp it happened on. **If the bleed recurs, capture which line and which provider** — that distinguishes them. Best tested on a `[NanakoRaws]` file, whose stacked-cue shape is what broke the first attempt. *(Phase 5)*
13. **Captured-audio loudness normalization needs a real-volume live check (2026-07-26).** Unit-tested against synthetic signals — the same tone captured at player volumes 1.0 through 0.01 exports at an identical RMS, and no input amplitude clips — but synthetic tones aren't dialogue over a music/effects bed. Not reached by the 2026-07-31 pass either. Needs the user to capture the same line twice at very different player volumes and confirm both cards play back at comparable loudness, and that the target level (`AUDIO_TARGET_RMS`, ~-21 dBFS) sounds right next to their existing Anki cards rather than conspicuously loud or quiet. *(Phase 5)*
14. **Subtitle-text filtering is still incomplete (2026-07-31), though the punctuation half is now done.** The 2026-07-29 markup generalisation held for what it covered, but the live pass found four annotation types still rendering as dialogue: parenthetical stage directions that aren't the whole line, speaker-attribution notes, doubled `((`/`))` around ordinary dialogue, and full-width punctuation. **The punctuation item was closed on 2026-08-15** (。 stripped, `{\anN}` override tags stripped in both parsers), and the three that remain share one root cause — `STAGE_RE` only matches a parenthetical spanning the entire line — which is still not fixed. Separately, a NanakoRaws file rendered one Japanese sentence across four line breaks, which is about how stacked cues are joined for DISPLAY and needs a real file to settle. *(Phase 5)*
15. **The in-page edit panel failed its first live pass in four places, all fixed 2026-07-31 and none re-tested.** Found and fixed: sentence corruption when changing the target word, overlapping preview playback, playback surviving the panel's close, and panel keystrokes reaching Crunchyroll's player shortcuts. **Unexplained and needing one more observation: a report that changing the word left the original note in place and created a second one.** The panel's only Anki write is `updateNoteFields` on the existing note id — there is no `addNote` path reachable from it — so either something else created that note or the two cards were separate captures. Re-check by capturing one card, changing its word, saving, and counting notes in Anki's browser. Still never exercised at all, on either pass: extending a clip past what was captured, and Anki being closed mid-edit reporting an error without discarding pending edits. **The panel's audio half was rebuilt on 2026-08-15 and none of it has been in a browser** — see checklist group D. *(Phase 5)*
16. **Chip auto-expand duration curve — retuned once from live feedback, not yet confirmed (2026-07-31).** The first pass found a long merged sentence didn't stay readable long enough, with no complaint about single-word captures lingering, so the per-character term and cap rose (2400ms + 110ms/char, capped at 12s) and the base barely moved. The cap is deliberately a few seconds under the chip's own 15s lifetime, or the collapsed state would never appear. Needs a second look at both ends; the untried alternative shape is still discrete short/medium/long tiers. One named group of constants in `content.js`. *(Phase 5)*
17. **PCM buffer pre-roll/post-roll padding duration.** `AUDIO_EDIT_PAD_SECONDS` stays at 3 seconds each side. Reported short again on 2026-08-15 — "the right side never reaches 3 seconds while the left always does" — and that turned out to be a third distinct cause rather than the number being wrong: the editor read the buffer once at build time, so whatever post-roll had played by then was all it ever showed. It now keeps re-reading until the pad is full. Two cases where a short right-hand pad is now correct and stated in the UI: the buffer was sealed by an episode change before 3s had played, and the padding hit a seek and was clamped to it. Still open on its own terms — adjust if extending a clip turns out to hit the 3s limit often in practice. *(Phase 5)*
18. **The whole 2026-08-15 audio rework needs its own live pass.** Five interacting changes to the capture path — the sample-based clock, the paused-video write skip, asymmetric padding with a tail wait, flushing an interrupted capture, and the discontinuity clamp — all verified only offline, against harnesses that drive the clock by hand rather than through a real `ScriptProcessorNode`. The one thing a harness cannot check is whether a paused media element really does stop producing input the way this now assumes; if it doesn't, clips will be short rather than long, which is the safer failure but still a failure. Checklist group B is written for this. *(Phase 5)*
19. **Whether Crunchyroll's caption files actually carry `\an8` on background lines.** The fix for an unrelated English line landing in the `Translation` field keys off ASS alignment overrides, which is the only signal in the file that distinguishes the two — but no real Crunchyroll caption file has been inspected for one. The mechanism and its guards are tested offline; what is unverified is the premise. If the stray line survives, the next step is to dump a caption file (`window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` for the URL) and look at what the two cues actually differ by. *(Phase 5)*
20. **The extension-reload banner is a stopgap, and the reason it is one may be worth revisiting.** A content script can be re-injected into an open tab, and everything except audio capture would recover — the blocker is that `createMediaElementSource` is once-per-`<video>`-per-page and the dead script still holds it. If a way is ever found to hand that graph over (or to detect that a page's video is already tapped and degrade explicitly rather than silently), the reload prompt could become a genuine hot-swap. Not worth building speculatively; noted so the constraint isn't rediscovered. *(Phase 5)*

**Needs an actual decision to resolve:**
1. *(resolved 2026-07-26 — see Resolved below; kept numbering stable for the entries after it.)*
2. **ひとりも absorbing a character's name (ひとり) in ひとりもついに.** Structurally the same shape as the accepted kanji-heteronym limitation — distinguishing "ひとり the idiom/quantifier" from "ひとり the character's name" needs show-specific knowledge the general phrase-matcher can't safely apply. Candidate for acceptance as a permanent limitation, same grounds as kanji-heteronym, but not formally decided yet — left open, revisit if more instances of this pattern turn up. *(Phase 4)*
3. **Independent JP/EN subtitle visibility toggles.** Raised 2026-07-29: many Japanese-immersion resources recommend watching without English subs, so making EN independently toggleable is a plausible Phase 6 addition. JP-only and JP+EN modes are both worth supporting; an **EN-only mode is explicitly rejected**, since it would suppress the Japanese overlay and dictionary popup, which are the extension's core purpose rather than a display variant. Needs a decision on toggle mechanism (hold-to-peek vs. persistent) and persistence scope (global default vs. per-session) before design starts. *(Phase 6)*

### Resolved

- **Entry resolution as a whole** (2026-08-13) — closed. Verified live (checklist Section A passed in full) and offline-clean over 2,526 episodes. This subsumes three questions previously listed here as open: the four undiagnosed 2026-08-04 audit findings, whether films/OVAs/specials resolve to their own entry and whether the picker rescues the rest (the 2026-07-27 question), and whether `resolveTextFiles` needed Crunchyroll's season episode range — **it did not**, the entry's own stated numbers separate the cases. See Section 5 (Phase 4.5) for the current state and Decisions Log 2026-08-11/12/13.
- **Films the CMS reports with no episode number** (2026-08-01) — resolved: not a real gap. JSON-LD carries `episodeNumber: 0` for Cucuruz Doan's Island where the CMS API says `null`, and JSON-LD is what the extension reads. Confirmed by running the page's verbatim metadata through the resolver: entry 2034, confident, three files, and the episode filter correctly never asked. Audited the whole episode path for falsy-zero handling at the same time — every site is `??` or `Number.isInteger`, none is `||`. See Decisions Log.
- **Whether Crunchyroll numbers a long franchise's episodes absolutely or per season** (2026-08-01) — resolved: **both, depending on the show**, measured across 30 series. Absolute for SPY×FAMILY, Apothecary Diaries, Detective Conan and Naruto: Shippuuden; per-season for Frieren, Re:Zero, Chainsaw Man and others. The magnitude test handles both without needing to know which. See Decisions Log.
- **Whether Crunchyroll publishes `partOfSeason.name`** (2026-07-31) — yes, confirmed live: the warning built to report its absence never fired across the whole pass, and season resolution by name worked on every ordinary TV season tested. The fallback plan (a manual per-show season override) is not needed. See Decisions Log 2026-07-27.
- **`JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` at 1.0s** (2026-07-31) — confirmed correct by eye on real footage; no change. The value was never derivable from the gap distribution, so live confirmation was the only way to settle it.
- **English displaying only while Japanese displays** (2026-07-31) — confirmed working as designed and not distracting in practice, including the mirror case the user noticed (a Japanese line like んっ with no matching English shows Japanese alone). No special-casing needed. See Decisions Log 2026-07-26.
- **PCM buffer padding duration** (2026-07-31) — 3 seconds each side stands; the live report was that the padding wasn't reachable, which was a separate bug (a fixed top-up timer, now an on-demand re-cut). **Reported short again on 2026-08-15, from a third cause** (the editor only ever read the buffer once) — see Ongoing #17, which is where this now lives.
- **Wrong-season Jimaku entry selection** (2026-07-26) — resolved: season parsing now covers all four naming conventions Jimaku uses, with a cour-marker exclusion and a title-match guard. Validated against 17 real show/season combinations. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **How to handle dual-language Jimaku files** (2026-07-26) — user's call: strip the embedded track, don't exclude the provider. Implemented as per-ASS-style script classification; a general mechanism proved feasible, so no per-provider special case was needed. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **JP/EN subtitle timing stagger at offset 0** (2026-07-26) — resolved by pairing English cues to the Japanese cue window instead of matching them independently. See Decisions Log.
- **Crunchyroll's subtitle CDN hostname** (2026-07-26) — `vod-fy-mod.crunchyrollcdn.com`, confirmed from a real signed URL in live console output. The existing `*.crunchyrollcdn.com` allowlist and host permission already covered it; no change needed. See Decisions Log.
- **Whether `caption-url-sniffer.js` can see Crunchyroll's `play` response at all** (2026-07-26) — yes, confirmed live: the MAIN-world fetch/XHR override catches it and the shape detection works. The Web Worker / service worker theory is ruled out. See Build Order (Phase 5).
- **Anki Translation field appearing on the card front** (2026-07-26) — resolved by the user: it was the dual-language `[SubsPlease]` file putting English into the `Sentence` field, not a template problem. The template was always back-only. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **Whether the audio field should be empty when Crunchyroll is muted** (2026-07-26) — confirmed with the user: yes, intentional, kept as-is. A muted capture produces no `[sound:…]` reference and no play button at all, rather than a silent clip. See Decisions Log.
- **Show/episode opt-in metadata toggle live-tested** (2026-07-26) — confirmed working, along with the other three "Extra info" toggles (POS, frequency, JLPT), in the same live pass. See Build Order (Phase 5).
- **JP/EN subtitle desync** (2026-07-26) — resolved; cause was the English update sitting behind the Japanese half's early return, not the offset the user had already ruled out. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **JP/EN vertical ordering** (2026-07-26) — resolved structurally via a single flex stack; Japanese-on-top is fixed intent, not configurable. See Decisions Log.
- **Whether a fully-silent audio capture should be exported** (2026-07-26) — decided: no, dropped to the existing no-audio-field path rather than exporting silence or amplified noise. See Decisions Log.
- **English subtitle display: data-source approach** (2026-07-23) — Crunchyroll's own native captions (option c), not a Jimaku fetch. Sidesteps the same-file-dual-language-extraction coverage gap and the independent-fetch timing-sync problem entirely (both real issues found via direct Jimaku API fact-finding, see Decisions Log) at the cost of needing fresh fact-finding on how Crunchyroll actually exposes them.
- **English subtitle display: how Crunchyroll exposes captions in the page** (2026-07-23) — not native `<track>` cues, and not reachable via any DOM-inspection technique (six rounds of live fact-finding, all negative — see Decisions Log). Resolved by pivoting to the Network tab instead: Crunchyroll's own `play` API response already contains a direct signed URL to a raw `.ass` file. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **Settings panel scheduling** (2026-07-23) — moved to Phase 6 (user's call); exact priority within Phase 6's list not yet decided. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **Phase 5/6 build order reprioritization** (2026-07-23) — English subtitle display + Anki translation field pulled into Phase 5 ahead of "edit last card"; show/episode opt-in toggle added to Phase 5. See Decisions Log and Build Order.
- **JLPT tag live-tested** (2026-07-22) — confirmed working correctly on a real Crunchyroll page in the first live-testing round (badges, toggle, Anki field all correct). See Decisions Log.
- **JLPT data source** (2026-07-22) — stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab, chosen for its direct JMdict-id join over the elzup lineage's fuzzy-matching requirement. See Decisions Log and Build Order (Phase 5).
- **Where the audio Anki field slots into the Phase 5 build order, and its capture-timing/UI design** (2026-07-22) — built directly into Phase 5, to the user's own design analysis. See Decisions Log and Section 5 (Phase 5).
- **Anki deck/note-type design for the core card fields** (2026-07-17) — dedicated "Japanese Immersion" deck + note type, sentence-first card direction, both confirmed live against a real test card. See Decisions Log and Build Order (Phase 5).
- **DRM feasibility test — both screenshot and audio resolved** (2026-07-17). Screenshot: confirmed blocked, dropped from the roadmap entirely. Audio: confirmed viable via a real signal-level measurement, not just an absence of errors. See Decisions Log.
- **Oval artifact behind the subtitle text / over unrelated page text** (2026-07-17) — confirmed live gone, see Decisions Log for the root cause (`#jp-immersion-subtitle` rendering as a visible empty box during gaps between lines).
- **`/watch/` URL-segment assumption for gating non-watch-page activation** (2026-07-17) — resolved in practice: the user confirmed the fix works (homepage/search no longer show the false error), which only works if `/watch/` is the right segment.
- **Phase 4.5 formally closed** (2026-07-17) — see Decisions Log and Build Order (Phase 4.5).
- **Te-form auxiliary chains** (2026-06-30) — handled by Rule 1 (split into main-verb+て / auxiliary) and Rule 2 (contracted forms). Verified against 食べてしまった, 話しかけてくれる, 持っていった, 見てる, 読んでいる — 89 occurrences across 1236 lines (~7%) in the first batch run.
- **Fullscreen subtitle visibility** (2026-06-30) — fixed via `getContainer()` + `fullscreenchange` re-parent listener; verified on Bocchi ep 1.
- **ASS simultaneous multi-line subtitles** (2026-06-30) — fixed via `cues.filter()` + `.join("\n")` instead of `cues.find()`.
